# `canoa-host` — API header

Header-file-style reference for the `canoa-host` crate, whose command-line program is `canoa`: every public item with its signature, contract and gotchas, so the crate can be used without reading its source. It ships inside the package and is self-contained. Development documentation lives with the source at <https://github.com/Boriken-Dev/canoa>.

MSRV Rust 1.89. Version `0.0.0-alpha.0`, not published (`publish = false`): its git dependencies are not released. It depends on `anycfg` (`bf71f70`, from its repository), `canoa`, `clap`, `coqui-acp`, `coqui-acp-api`, `coqui-acp-bridge`, `canoa-antigravity`, `canoa-claude`, `canoa-codex`, `canoa-foundry` (Foundry Local as a library the host carries: an account provider, a model gateway, a Store program and type, and the SDK engine's helper), `canoa-litellm` (a LiteLLM proxy as an account kind, offered through its library), `canoa-grok`, `canoa-pi` (each agent's source preset, Store recipe and, for Claude, Codex and Pi, coqui-acp provider), `coqui-acp-harness` (`kind`), `coqui-acp-history` and `coqui-acp-provider` and `coqui-acp-secrets` (coqui-acp `34de3e9`), `coqui-aos` (`815aa3c`), `coqui-link` (`40123baa`, features `sealed` and `forward`; it brings `coqui-peer-noise`), `coqui-agentic-peer` (`161307f9`), `coqui-peer`, `coqui-peer-webcrypto`, `coqui-transport-pipe` (`1bf161f`), `coqui-transport-guatiao` (`f09bf2d`; `pipe`, `websocket`), `coqui-transport` and `coqui-transport-web` (`websocket`), `coqui-builder` (the error a listener builder returns), `guatiao` (`provider`, `load`), `guatiao-serde` (no format), `guatiao-intake` (the speech providers' forms), `httparse`, `if-addrs`, `indexmap`, `percent-encoding`, `rcgen` (the authority a session's proxy signs with), `rustls` (`ring`), `rustls-native-certs` (the roots a program is given beside it), `serde`, `serde_json`, `serde-saphyr` (feature `yaml`), `toml`, `tracing` and `tracing-subscriber`, and for accounts `keyring-core` with the platform's store (`windows-native-keyring-store`, `apple-native-keyring-store`, `zbus-secret-service-keyring-store`), `aes-gcm`, `zeroize`, `time` (`parsing`) and `rpassword`.

| Feature | Adds | Needed for | Default |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `web` | the site `CANOA_WEB_DIST` names at build time, built into the binary; `web::BUILT_IN` is `true` | serving the web app with no directory | off |
| `foundry` | `canoa-foundry`'s `sdk` feature: an account's `engine = "sdk"` runs its model through Foundry Local's Rust SDK (a native library the person points `library` at) in a process of its own, `canoa helper canoa-foundry` (hidden; one of the host's own commands, started like `provider`, through the spawner the host hands the library's gateway), which the gateway talks to over its standard input and output, so a crash of the library is that process's; it is started again by the next request. It brings `reqwest` with the system's TLS library (OpenSSL on Linux), `hyper`, `tokio` and `async-openai` | running a Foundry Local model in the host's own process | off |
| `portal` | `canoa portal` and `canoa_host::portal` (Unix only): `coqui-access`, `coqui-access-system` (PAM and NSS, linking the system's `libpam`), `coqui-peer-tls` (a certificate's fields) and `tungstenite` | signing people in with their accounts and giving each their own host | off |

`CANOA_WEB_DIST` must be an absolute directory holding `index.html` (a `flutter build web` output): with `web` on and it unset, relative or without an index, the build stops. Names starting with a dot are left out; a link leading out of the directory stops the build. `--all-features` therefore needs it set too.

The root re-exports `SITE_META`, `ACCOUNT_META`, `MODEL_META`, `CACHE_META`, `CACHE_LIFETIMES`, `cache_lifetime_name`, `cache_lifetime_named`, `AccountConfig`, `PoolConfig`, `StoreConfig`, `Config`, `ConfigError`, `Entry`, `EntryKind`, `HarnessOptions`, `Listen`, `Origin`, `Settings`, `WebConfig`, `Holder`, `Home`, `HomeLock`, `LockError`, `Peer`, `PeerState`, `PeersError`, `HostLine`, `ProviderError`, `ProviderOptions`, `serve_stdio`, `Listening`, `ServeError`, `ServeOptions`, `Serving`, `Stopper`, `Local`, `LocalError`, `Link`, `LinkError`, `ToolSpec`, `WebError`, `WebServer` and `WebStopper`. Public modules: `cli`, `config`, `harness`, `home`, `kb`, `options`, `peers`, `pipe`, `portal` (feature `portal`, Unix), `portal_link`, `profile`, `provider`, `serve`, `tool`, `web`.

`#![deny(unsafe_code)]`: the one `unsafe` module is `usage_splice/members/win32.rs` (Windows' Toolhelp snapshot of the process table, through `windows-sys`). Loading a provider library or source runs its code; see `serve_stdio`.

## canoa.cfg (`canoa_host::config`)

canoa.cfg is one file in TOML, YAML (`#!yaml` first line) or JSON (`#!json`), read through anycfg (revision `bf71f70`) into `toml::Value` by `Config::load`, `Config::load_layers(&Layers)` and `Config::from_str_in`; the examples below are TOML. The Cargo features `yaml` and `json`, on by default, register the other two; without them `#!yaml` or `#!json` is refused naming the formats read. A null in YAML or JSON leaves its key unset; a TOML date or time is refused. `ConfigError::Parse` has `file`, `line` and `column` where known, and for a setting of the wrong shape the key's path (`host.agents.a.max_peers: expected usize, found a string`), never a value.

The host reads `[host]`, `[usage] limit_phrases` (`Config::limit_phrases`: `None` when unset, which means coqui-acp-api's `DEFAULT_LIMIT_PHRASES`; a list replaces them, `[]` infers nothing, an empty or blank phrase is refused) and `[permissions] protected` (`Config::protected_paths`: a string with a pattern a line, or a list of strings; `None` when unset; it is handed to the built-in Pi source and to nothing else); the app's tables (`desktop`, `network`, `assets`, `appearance`, `agents`, `overlays`, `profile`, and `[usage] warn_permille`) are not read. Every table the host reads refuses a key it does not know.

```toml
[host]
origins = ["https://canoa.example"]  # optional: browser origins that may connect; empty or absent lets any, and the proof decides
shell = "git-bash"                    # optional: the shell a terminal starts and each harness entry is told to use: an id `shell/list` gives (see Shells) or a program, an absolute path or a name found on PATH, started with no arguments; absent, $SHELL, else pwsh or powershell on Windows, else /bin/sh

[host.agents.NAME]                    # a program speaking ACP on its standard streams
program = "gemini"                    # this or harness, one of the two; found as the OS finds a command
args = ["--acp"]
profiles = ["review", "plain"]        # optional: [host.profiles] a client may choose among, the first by default
own_profile = false                    # optional: canoa.cfg's top level (`[profile]`, `[overlays]`, `[agents.NAME]`) is this entry's profile too, as it is the host's own sources': the person's own layer and the overlays they installed; never with `profiles`, and `peers` is required: the keys that reach the entry get the person's own layer
usage = "antigravity"                 # optional, with program: read the agent's quota too, by the `usage_reader` a bundled agent offers (`antigravity`, for agy acp); see Usage
title = "Claude for review"             # optional: what a client shows for this entry instead of its name; 1 to 64 characters, not blank, no control characters (MAX_TITLE)
shared = false                        # optional: a session outlives its connection and any key the entry admits attaches to it; see Shared sessions
shared_idle_seconds = 86400           # optional, with shared: how long a session nobody is attached to is kept; 0 keeps it until a client ends it
shared_permission_window_seconds = 0  # optional, with shared: log a permission request nobody has answered for this long (it stays open; nothing is answered for anyone); 0 or unset logs nothing
shared_keep_log = true                # optional, with shared: keep each session's log of updates in the home so it survives the host's restart (see Shared sessions); false keeps nothing
shared_log_entries = 100000           # optional, with shared: updates kept for replay, the oldest going first
shared_log_bytes = 67108864           # optional, with shared: serialized bytes of updates kept for replay

[host.agents.OTHER]                   # an acp_harness provider chosen by id, run by the host itself; see Serving
harness = "claude"                    # the id of a bundled agent's harness (`providers/list` names them) or of an outside provider that library or source loads; takes no args
executable = "/opt/claude/bin/claude" # claude and codex: a name found as the OS finds a command, or a path from the file's directory
setting_sources = ["project"]         # claude only: of user, project, local; [] loads none; default Claude Code's own
# the access (sandbox, network, approvals) is the profile's, not a key here: `profiles = [...]` with `[host.profiles.NAME] access = { ... }`; every mode the agent has is offered, Bypass included
shell = "canoa-pwsh"                  # claude, codex and pi (a built-in harness): this entry's shell, in place of [host] shell; see Shells
cache_lifetime = "short"              # optional, with harness: how long the provider's prompt cache lives, one of CACHE_LIFETIMES ("default", "off", "short", "long"; off only where the provider lists it, else it is the default); the entry's default for a session whose client chooses none; absent leaves the client's (the chat asks "long")
account_settings = { renew_poll_ms = 30000 }  # optional, with harness: the configuration of the account provider the same library offers as `<harness>_accounts` (`claude_accounts`, `codex_accounts`, `pi_accounts`, `opencode_accounts`, an outside library's `stdio_accounts`), which signs in, renews, watches and reads plan limits; its `executable` (and `harness`, for `stdio_accounts`) default to the entry's. `renew_poll_ms` (30000; 0 looks only when a watch begins and ends) and `command_timeout_ms` (60000) are its settings, no longer the harness's
accounts = ["work", "personal", "keys"]   # optional, with harness: the [host.accounts] a client may run a session on, and the [host.pools] it offers (a pool takes the place of the accounts it holds, and an `any-<standard>` pool the host makes is named without being in the file), the first by default; absent offers the automatic `local` account (the harness on the login it has where it runs, when its provider has one); `local` may be named among them to place it

[host.agents.OUTSIDE]                 # an outside acp_harness provider
harness = "acp_echo_harness_echo_harness"  # its id; library or source is required for an id the host does not carry
source = "guatiao_source_py.dll"      # or library = "..."; exactly one; paths from the file's directory
import_search = "library"            # with source only, as for a provider
settings = { title = "Echo" }         # the provider's own configuration, checked against its schema when the host starts

[host.agents.OUTSIDE.source_settings] # with source only, as for a provider
dirs = ["harness"]

[host.profiles.NAME]                  # a profile (see Profiles below), read as the person's own layer
overlays = ["team"]                   # optional: AOS overlays the app installed in overlays/ beside this file
allow_mcp = { team = "sha256:…" }     # optional: an overlay's MCP server and the definition digest it may start at
allow_hooks = { team = "sha256:…" }   # optional: an overlay and the tree digest its hooks may run at; see Overlay hooks
context = { text = "..." }            # and every key of the profile schema

[skill_pins]                          # top level: a skill from elsewhere (name, without regard to ASCII case) and the tree digest it must have
review = "sha256:…"

[host.accounts.work]                  # a login for an entry's `accounts`; written by `canoa accounts add`, and never holds a credential
harness = "claude"                    # a provider, as an entry's harness
kind = "claude-login"                 # one of the kinds the provider's catalog lists
id = "0b7d3c1e-5a52-4f0e-9d0f-6c1f5d5b8e21"   # Canoa writes it when it makes the account; never reused, and the key of its stored credential
settings = { base_url = "https://llm.example" }   # optional: the kind's settings that are not secrets, checked against its schema

[host.pools.keys]                     # a name for accounts that provide one standard, which an entry offers and a session may run on (written by the app's Accounts section or `pools/set`)
standard = "anthropic-api"            # any standard id: lower-case letters, digits and single hyphens; each account has to provide it
accounts = ["work", "personal"]       # [host.accounts] names, or the automatic `local`; the order is the order they are taken in

[host.providers.NAME]                 # a Guatiao agent_provider, from a library or a source
library = "echo_provider.dll"         # a library file, relative to the file's directory; or:
source = "guatiao_source_py.dll"      # a source file, the same way; exactly one of the two
provider = "echo_provider"            # required; the provider's id
max_sessions = 8                      # sessions one connection may hold
import_search = "library"            # with source only, Windows: "library" or "path"; default library when import_dirs names a directory, else path, which is logged as a warning

[host.providers.NAME.source_settings]  # with source only; what the source is added with
dirs = ["python"]                     # for guatiao_source_py: directories of manifests, each <name>.guatiao[.<major>[.<minor>]]
paths = ["/opt/guatiao/python"]       # and what goes on sys.path; relative paths are made absolute from the file's directory
isolation = "shipped"                 # optional: "shipped" (the source's default), "isolated" or "inherit", which is logged as configuration
home = "python/runtime"               # optional: the Python installation the interpreter runs from; made absolute too
import_dirs = ["python/runtime"]      # optional, Windows: where python3.dll is found; made absolute too

[host.providers.NAME.settings]        # the provider's configuration, for one that is built from one
prefix = "hi"                         # checked against the provider's schema when a connection loads it

[host.extensions.NAME]                # a Guatiao store_extension: adds types to the Store; see "Store extensions" below
library = "..."                       # or source = "..." (with source_settings, import_search, as a provider)
provider = "..."                      # the store_extension provider id
pin = "sha256:..."                    # the Store wrote these when it installed the extension; each is checked again every time the host loads it
root = "..."                          # with pin: the folder the tree digest covers, which holds the library
sha256 = "..."                        # or, instead of pin and root, the library file's SHA-256 (with library)
[host.tools.NAME]                     # a command-line tool with no ACP, served as an ACP agent; see "A tool entry" below
program = "pi"                        # required; found as the OS finds a command
args = ["--mode", "rpc"]
runs = "session"                      # required: "session" or "message"
unasked = "what it does without asking"   # required, not blank; shown when a session opens
prompt = { type = "prompt", message = "{prompt}" }   # session: required, a table; message: refused
cancel = { type = "abort" }           # session only; optional
resume_args = ["--session", "{session}"]  # message only; optional
max_sessions = 8                      # sessions one connection may hold
[host.tools.NAME.kinds]               # optional: a kind template's result -> one of ACP's tool kinds
bash = "execute"
[[host.tools.NAME.events]]            # the rules, in order
when = { type = "message_update", "assistantMessageEvent.type" = "text_delta" }
send = "text"
text = "{assistantMessageEvent.delta}"

# Any of the three tables, each optional:
listen = "127.0.0.1:7801"             # default 127.0.0.1:0, any free port; another address is configuration, logged; or "pipe:NAME" (Windows) or "unix:PATH" (Unix) for a native client on this machine, see "A pipe or Unix socket"; with `transport`, the endpoint that provider listens on, required
transport = "websocket"               # optional: carry this entry's connections through a `transport` provider by id: "pipe" and "websocket" are built in, any other id needs transport_library or transport_source; see "A transport provider"
transport_library = "t.dll"           # with transport: an outside provider's library, or transport_source; exactly one, paths from the file's directory; never with a built-in id
transport_source = "guatiao_source_py.dll"   # with transport: as an entry's `source`, with transport_source_settings and transport_import_search as source_settings and import_search
transport_settings = { listen = { frames = "binary" } }   # with transport: the provider's own configuration, checked against its schema when the host starts
working_roots = ["/home/me/src"]      # absolute, no . or ..; a session's cwd must be under one; default any
                                      # a Windows share (\\server\share\src) is a root and a cwd like any path; \\?\UNC\ is compared and forwarded plain; a device path is refused
worktrees = true                      # agents and tools: a git worktree per session inside working_roots; see "Worktrees"
peers = ["sha256:…"]                  # paired keys that may use it; default every paired key
max_peers = 4                         # connections at once

[host.web]                            # optional: the web app on a listener of its own; see "The web app" below
dir = "site"                          # the site, relative to the file's directory; without it the site built in (feature web)
listen = "127.0.0.1:7800"             # the default; beyond loopback only with cert and key
cert = "web.crt"                      # PEM: the certificate, then any intermediates; with key, HTTPS
key = "web.key"                       # PEM: its private key
hosts = ["canoa.example"]             # Host names a request may carry besides localhost and an IP address
mode = "local"                        # "local" or "remote": where the person sits, told to the page in /canoa.json as `web`; absent, local for a page reached as localhost or a loopback address and remote for any other name
connect = ["https://fonts.gstatic.com"]   # origins the page may connect to besides the entries; each logged as configuration
office = false                        # serve the Office add-in's pages under /office/ with a policy of their own (see "The Office add-in's routes"); off by default, logged as configuration when on
office_local = false                  # with office and a loopback listen: the add-in runs in Office on this computer, so the page's own key is accepted when it asks (see "The Office add-in's routes"); off by default, logged as configuration when on
office_secret = false                 # with office_local: the page's requests (admission, tools, starting a host) carry the secret in <home>/office/secret, so another account on a shared computer cannot make them; off by default, logged as configuration when on
office_frames = ["https://*.officeapps.live.com"]   # with office: origins besides the built-in ones that may frame the add-in's pages; https, a host that may start with *. and holds a dot more, an optional port; each logged as configuration
max_connections = 64                  # the limits, each at its default; 0 is refused
max_connections_per_address = 16
connection_seconds = 600              # a connection's whole life, however busy
max_request_bytes = 16384
max_requests = 1000                   # per connection
request_seconds = 10
idle_seconds = 30
send_seconds = 30
immutable_seconds = 31536000          # how long a hashed file may be kept; 0 allowed
```

Names are 1 to 64 letters, digits, `-` or `_`, unique across the three tables. Two entries may not name the same non-zero address, nor may an entry and `[host.web]`.

```rust
pub const DEFAULT_MAX_PEERS: usize = 4;
pub const DEFAULT_MAX_SESSIONS: usize = 8;
pub const MAX_NAME: usize = 64;
pub const MAX_TITLE: usize = 64;

#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Config { pub origins: Vec<String>, pub entries: Vec<Entry>, pub profiles: IndexMap<String, HostProfile>, pub web: Option<WebConfig>, pub limit_phrases: Option<Vec<String>>, pub protected_paths: Option<Vec<String>>, pub accounts: IndexMap<String, AccountConfig>, pub pools: IndexMap<String, PoolConfig>, pub secrets: Option<serde_json::Value>, pub shell: Option<String>, pub store: StoreConfig, pub skill_pins: BTreeMap<String, String> }   // Default, Clone, Eq
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct WebConfig { pub dir: Option<PathBuf>, pub listen: SocketAddr, pub tls: Option<WebTls>, pub hosts: Vec<String>, pub connect: Vec<String>, pub limits: web::Limits, pub keep_out: Vec<PathBuf>, pub mode: Option<WebMode>, pub office: bool, pub office_frames: Vec<String>, pub office_local: bool, pub office_secret: bool }   // Default, Clone, Eq
pub fn WebConfig::new() -> WebConfig            // the site built in, on web::DEFAULT_LISTEN, plain HTTP, nothing else allowed, default limits
// keep_out is never read from the file: `serve` fills it with canoa.cfg's directory and the identity home
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct WebTls { pub cert: PathBuf, pub key: PathBuf }   // Clone, Eq
pub fn WebTls::new(cert: impl Into<PathBuf>, key: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> WebTls
pub fn Config::load(path: &Path) -> Result<Config, ConfigError>
pub fn Config::from_str_in(text: &str, base: &Path) -> Result<Config, ConfigError>   // touches no file
pub fn Config::entry(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&Entry>

#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Listen { Address(SocketAddr), Pipe(PipeName) }   // Clone, Eq, Display (the text `listen` is written in), From<SocketAddr>
pub fn Listen::parse(text: &str) -> Result<Listen, String>   // "ADDRESS:PORT", "pipe:NAME" or "unix:PATH"; the error is a clause naming `listen` and quoting nothing
pub const fn Listen::address(&self) -> Option<SocketAddr>    // None for a pipe
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct HostProfile { pub profile: Profile, pub overlays: Vec<String>, pub allow_mcp: BTreeMap<String, String>, pub allow_hooks: BTreeMap<String, String>, pub directory: PathBuf, pub skill_pins: BTreeMap<String, String>, pub options: BTreeMap<String, Document>, pub protected: Option<Vec<String>> }   // protected: `[permissions] protected`, which a hook's allow never answers for; options: `[overlays.NAME] options`, by overlay, for the host's own profile and every `[host.profiles]` one
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Entry { pub name: String, pub kind: EntryKind, pub listen: Listen, pub working_roots: Vec<PathBuf>, pub worktrees: bool, pub peers: Option<Vec<PeerId>>, pub max_peers: usize, pub title: Option<String>, pub local: bool }   // local: served over Local only, no address; only the host's own sources are
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum EntryKind { Agent { program: OsString, args: Vec<OsString>, profiles: Vec<String>, usage: Option<String> /* the id of a bundled agent's usage_reader */ }, Harness { harness: String, origin: Option<Origin>, executable: Option<OsString>, options: HarnessOptions, settings: Option<Settings>, account_settings: Option<Settings>, profiles: Vec<String>, accounts: Vec<String> }, Provider { origin: Origin, provider: String, settings: Option<Settings>, directory: PathBuf, max_sessions: usize }, Tool { spec: ToolSpec } }
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct AccountConfig { pub harness: String, pub kind: String, pub id: String, pub settings: Option<Settings> }   // Clone, Eq: one [host.accounts.NAME]
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct PoolConfig { pub standard: String, pub accounts: Vec<String> }   // Clone, Eq: one [host.pools.NAME]; `Config::pools` is by name in the file's order
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct KbConfig { pub tools: Option<bool>, pub embeddings: Option<String>, pub model: Option<String>, pub api_key_env: Option<String>, pub document_prefix: Option<String>, pub query_prefix: Option<String>, pub chunk_bytes: Option<usize>, pub overlap_bytes: Option<usize>, pub llm_endpoint: Option<String>, pub llm_model: Option<String>, pub llm_api_key_env: Option<String>, pub llm_candidates: Option<usize>, pub llm_timeout_secs: Option<u64> }   // Default, Clone, Eq: `[host.kb]`, as `Config::kb`
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct StoreConfig { pub protected: Option<Vec<String>>, pub credential_variables: Option<Vec<String>>, pub index_ttl: Option<u64>, pub index_cache_bytes: Option<u64>, pub tools: Option<bool> }   // Default, Clone, Eq: `[host.store]`; None is the recommended list, or for the index and the tools their default
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct NetworkConfig { pub proxy: ProxyChoice, pub proxy_user: Option<String>, pub proxy_password_env: Option<String>, pub no_proxy: Option<Vec<String>>, pub negotiate: Option<bool>, pub client_certificate: ClientCertificate, pub ca_file: Option<PathBuf>, pub git_tls: GitTls, pub endpoints: Vec<String>, pub proxy_idle_seconds: Option<u64> }   // Default, Clone, Eq: `[host.network]`; `settings()` reads the password from its variable (`canoa::network` has the types)
pub const CACHE_META: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/cache_lifetime";   // the `_meta` key of `initialize`: {"lifetime": "default"|"off"|"short"|"long", "chosen"?: bool}; `chosen` (true when absent) says the person chose it for this session and it beats the entry's `cache_lifetime`; false is the client's default for its kind of session, which the entry's setting replaces; no key leaves the entry's, else the provider's default; any other value turns the client away (-32602)
pub const SITE_META: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/site";   // the `_meta` key of a webpage's `session/new`, `session/load` and `session/resume`, in the request ({"site": "<host name>"}) and in the answer to `session/new` ({"site", "id", "folder"}); see "A webpage's session folder"
pub const ACCOUNT_META: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/account";   // the `_meta` key of `initialize`, in the request (the account's name) and in the answer ({"name", "kind"})
pub const MODEL_META: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/model";   // the `_meta` key of `initialize` naming the model the session starts on, by the id the account lists (text); a model that account does not list refuses the start with `-32602`, naming the account and the model, and a provider that cannot list before a session leaves the model to the session's own options
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Origin { Library(PathBuf), Source { file: PathBuf, settings: Settings, import_search: ImportSearch } }   // paths absolute
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum ImportSearch { Library, Path }   // Copy
pub const fn ImportSearch::as_str(self) -> &'static str     // "library", "path"
pub fn ImportSearch::named(text: &str) -> Option<ImportSearch>
pub fn ImportSearch::default_for(settings: &Settings) -> ImportSearch   // Library when import_dirs names a directory, else Path
pub struct Settings(..)                         // Clone, Default, Eq; Debug shows keys, never values
pub fn Settings::new(map: serde_json::Map<String, Value>) -> Settings
pub fn Settings::as_map(&self) -> &serde_json::Map<String, Value>
```

- `entries` keeps the file's order, every agent before every provider, every provider before every tool. A file with no `[host]` gives no entries.
- An agent names `program` (with optional `args`) or `harness` (a provider id, not empty), one of the two; a `harness` other than `claude`, `codex`, `pi` or `opencode` with no `library` or `source`, `args` with `harness`, and `executable`, `setting_sources`, `cache_lifetime`, `library`, `source`, `source_settings`, `import_search` or `settings` with `program` are refused, and so is another harness's key or a value not in its list. `executable` and `setting_sources` are the `claude`, `codex` and `pi` providers' settings (`setting_sources` is Claude Code's alone; `pi` has `settings` for the rest: `node` with `script`, `protected`, `isolated`; `opencode` takes `executable` and `settings` for the stdio provider's limits, `max_requests`, `max_message_bytes` and the `replay_*` ones) and are refused for any other id, which takes `settings`; one the entry's `settings` also holds is refused as set twice. An entry has no key for the access (`permission_mode`, `approval_policy`, `sandbox`, `bypass`, `modes`), and a `settings` table that names `access`, `permission_mode`, `approval_policy` or `sandbox` is refused: the access is the profile's. `settings = { modes = [...] }` and `{ allow_bypass = ... }` stay the person's own and are logged at `info` when the host starts. The `library` or `source`, `source_settings` and `import_search` keys read as a provider entry's. An `executable` holding `/` or `\` is made absolute against the file's directory, since the command line runs in a session's directory, which a client chooses. A harness entry is an `agent` in `check`'s and `serve`'s lines.

```rust
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct HarnessOptions { pub setting_sources: Option<Vec<String>>, pub cache_lifetime: Option<CacheLifetime> }   // Default, Eq
pub fn built_in_harnesses() -> impl Iterator<Item = &'static str>;  // the acp_harness providers the bundled agents' libraries register (`bundled.rs`)
pub fn is_built_in_harness(id: &str) -> bool;
pub const SETTING_SOURCES: &[&str];    // user, project, local
```
- `[host.kb]`: `tools` (default true), `embeddings`, `model`, `api_key_env`, `document_prefix`, `query_prefix`, `chunk_bytes`, `overlap_bytes` and the `llm_*` keys; see "The knowledge tools". `[overlays.NAME] kb = "index"` or `"files"` (absent: the overlay's own hint) is read from the app's table by `Config::local_profiles` into `kb_choice` (name to whether it is searched), and each `HostProfile::kb_choice`; any other value is refused naming the overlay.
- `[host.network]`: how every fetch and every program the host starts reaches the network; with no table, the system's proxy (environment, then the operating system's, PAC and WPAD included), proxy login, certificate verifier and keystore client certificates. `proxy` (`system`, `none`, `pac:LOCATION` or a proxy), `proxy_user` with `proxy_password_env` (a variable's name, never the password), `no_proxy` (a string split on commas and whitespace, or a list; replaces the system's), `negotiate`, `client_certificate` (`system`, `none` or a PEM file of a chain and its unencrypted key), `ca_file` (trust exactly these), `git_tls` (`system`: Schannel on Windows; `git`), `endpoints` (URLs added to the list a PAC script is asked about for the programs' proxy variables) and `proxy_idle_seconds` (how long the proxy a profile's `system` or `pac` mode runs is kept after its last session; default 0); paths are from canoa.cfg's folder, any other key or value is refused naming the key. `Serving::bind` installs it (`ServeError::Network` for one it cannot use: an unreadable proxy, a certificate file that is missing or empty, a password variable that is not set). See "The network".
- `[host.store]`: `protected` and `credential_variables`, each a string with a pattern a line or an array of such strings, in gitignore syntax (a leading `!` allows what an earlier line protected, the last matching line wins, `#` comments, `~/` the home, a drive or `/` the root, otherwise anywhere, a folder's pattern covers what is under it, names compare whatever their case). Absent, the recommended list applies (`store::protect::RECOMMENDED_PLACES`, `RECOMMENDED_VARIABLES`); a list written replaces it, an empty one protects nothing. `protected` is the places a program store's declared change may not be at, under or hold (`~/.ssh`, `~/.config/autostart`, ...) and the shared folders it may enter only by a place of its own (`~/.config` then `!~/.config/*`); `credential_variables` is the variable names an install is never given and a launch never sets (`*TOKEN`, `*_API_KEY`, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, ...). It is `[host.store]` and not `[permissions] protected` because the question differs: a state folder `~/.name` is a program's own place here, and the app's `.*` would protect it. `index_ttl` (seconds, default 300; 0 asks on every request) and `index_cache_bytes` (default 64 MiB) are the catalog index's ("The catalog index" below). `tools` (default true) is whether the host offers the `canoa-store` tools ("The store tools" below). Any other key is refused by name. `Options::from_config` applies the lists.
- `[host.accounts.NAME]`: a name as an entry's, with `harness`, `kind` and `id` (1 to 128 bytes, no control character, another account's refused) required and `settings` optional. Any other key is refused by name (`api_key is not a key of an account`), never quoting its value: **a credential never goes in canoa.cfg**. `[host] account_store` is `os` or `file`. `local` is taken: the automatic account every harness that has a login of its own offers (`AccountOffer::local`) is called that, so `[host.accounts.local]` is refused. An agent entry's `accounts` names accounts the file holds (and may name `local`), none twice; with `program` it is refused. When `serve` starts, each account an entry offers is checked against the provider's catalog: its harness is the entry's, its kind is listed, its `settings` fit the kind's schema (a key the kind marks as a credential is refused as one, naming the key) and an endpoint setting is an `https` address or an `http` one to this machine.
- `[host.pools.NAME]`: a name as an entry's, `standard` (a standard id as `coqui_acp_api::standard::is_standard` takes it: any, the set is open) and `accounts` (names in `[host.accounts]`, or `local`, each once, possibly none). Any other key is refused by name. A pool's name is not an account's, not `local`, and not `any-<standard>` for any standard (that is the host's own pool of it, and an account may not be named so either); an entry's `accounts` may name an account, `local`, a pool, or such an `any-<standard>`. That every member provides the pool's standard is checked where the account providers' kinds are known (`pools/set`, `accounts/offer`, and an entry that names the pool when the host starts): the refusal names the account, its kind's standards and the pool's. `pools` is in `ENTRIES`: a higher layer's pool of a name replaces a lower one's whole.
- `[host.profiles.NAME]`: a name as an entry's; `overlays` valid AOS overlay names; `allow_mcp` and `allow_hooks` values `sha256:` and 64 lower-case hex digits (`allow_hooks` keys valid overlay names); the rest `Profile::from_table` as `LayerKind::User`, its relative paths from the file's directory. An agent's `profiles` must name profiles the file holds, each once. Errors say `profile NAME:` and a setting, never a value.
- `[skill_pins]` (top level, read whether or not there is a `[host]`): each key a skill name (a letter or digit, then letters, digits, `_`, `.`, `-`; no device name), each value `sha256:` and 64 hex digits; two keys equal without regard to ASCII case, or anything else, refuse the file with `[skill_pins]` and what is wrong. Kept by lower-case name in `Config::skill_pins` and in each `HostProfile::skill_pins`.
- An entry's `listen` is `ADDRESS:PORT`, `pipe:NAME` or `unix:PATH` (`Listen::parse`). `pipe:NAME` is a Windows named pipe `\\.\pipe\NAME`, and on Unix the socket `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/NAME.sock` (else `$TMPDIR/NAME.sock`); `unix:PATH` is an absolute socket path, and is refused on Windows. A name or path `coqui-transport-pipe` cannot use is refused naming `listen`, never quoting it. Two entries cannot share a pipe or an address with a port; a pipe never conflicts with `[host.web]`'s address.
- An entry that names a `transport` listens through that provider (`Listen::Transport`): `listen` is then the provider's endpoint, not read by the host and required (a blank one is refused), `transport_settings` and the origin keys follow the rules of an entry's `settings`, `library` and `source` (an id that is not `pipe` or `websocket` needs `transport_library` or `transport_source`, a built-in one takes neither, the two exclude each other, and each `transport_*` key without `transport` is refused). Two entries cannot name one endpoint. `transport` and its keys are read in the same way in an agent, provider and tool entry; the entry's own `library`, `source` and `settings` stay the entry's provider's.
- A tool entry's `listen`, `working_roots`, `worktrees`, `peers` and `max_peers` are read as in the other tables; the rest is a `ToolSpec`, refused as a whole when `ToolSpec::check` refuses it. Its errors name a setting and a rule's index, never a value.
- `library` and `source` are made absolute against `base` (the file's directory for `load`); `directory` is `base` made absolute, where the provider's process runs.
- In `source_settings`, every text in a `dirs`, `manifests`, `paths` or `import_dirs` list, and `home` when it is text, is made absolute against the directory, since a source may otherwise resolve it against a later working directory or its own file's; other keys, `isolation` included, pass as written, for the source to refuse.
- `source_settings` and `settings` are TOML tables held as JSON. A date, or a float that is not a number, is refused; nothing checks them further until a connection loads the provider, since checking means loading its code.
- A leading byte-order mark is ignored; anything else not UTF-8 is a parse error.
- `[host.web]`: `mode` is `local` or `remote` (anything else is refused, `[host]: web: mode is local or remote`); `dir`, `cert` and `key` are made absolute against the directory; `cert` and `key` go together; `listen` beyond loopback without them is refused; `hosts` are names of letters, digits, `-` and `.`, kept in lower case; each `connect` source is `http`, `https`, `ws` or `wss`, `://`, a host of letters, digits, `-` and `.`, an optional port and an optional path of letters, digits, `/`, `-`, `.`, `_` and `~`, nothing else; every limit but `immutable_seconds` must be at least 1; `office_local` needs `office = true` and a loopback `listen` (`[host]: web: office_local needs ...`; behind a proxy every request looks local), `office_secret` needs `office_local = true`, and with `office_local` a `[host.control]` that lists an address needs no key under `grants`, since the page's key is not known to canoa.cfg; `office_frames` needs `office = true` and each source is `https://`, a host of letters, digits, `-` and `.` that may start with `*.` (and then holds another dot), and an optional port. Errors say `[host]: web:` and a setting, never a value. The table read is not checked for a site: `WebServer::bind` does that.

## Shells (`canoa_host::shells`, `[host] shell`, an entry's `shell`)

`shells::detect(&Lookup)` lists the shells on this computer, Canoa's own copies and everyone else's alike, each a `Shell {id, label, path, family, origin}`: on Windows Git Bash (`canoa-git-bash` for the Store's copy; `git-bash` for Git for Windows' own, found under `%ProgramFiles%`, `%LocalAppData%\Programs\Git` and beside a `git` on the `PATH`), MSYS2's bash (`msys2`), WSL's `bash.exe` (`wsl`), any other `bash.exe` on the `PATH` (`bash`), PowerShell 7 (`pwsh`, and `canoa-pwsh`) and Windows PowerShell (`powershell`); elsewhere `bash`, `zsh`, `pwsh` and the login shell (`login`) when it is none of those. A second of a kind has `-2` after its id. `Lookup::here()` is this computer; a test gives its own folders. Nothing is run to find one.

A setting (`[host] shell`, an entry's `shell`) is an id of that list, else a program: an absolute path (`.exe` added on Windows) or a name found only in an absolute folder of the `PATH`, never the folder the host is in. A setting that names none stops an entry that has its own `shell` from starting (`host entry NAME: ...`) and is logged for the host's, which every other entry then runs without.

One setting, translated for each harness by `shells::for_harness(harness, &Shell, windows)` into `Applied {env, notice}`: only what the harness documents is passed, and nothing writes a program's own settings file. Claude Code gets `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH` on Windows for a `bash.exe` or `sh.exe` that is not WSL's launcher (its documentation: "path to the Git Bash executable (`bash.exe`)", ignored unless the file is named `bash.exe`, `sh.exe`, `bash` or `sh`), and `CLAUDE_CODE_SHELL` elsewhere for a `bash` or `zsh` ("a path to a `bash` or `zsh` binary"); a PowerShell is not passed (its PowerShell tool runs the first `pwsh` on the `PATH`, which a Store copy is on). Codex has no setting for its shell (it looks for `pwsh` and `powershell` on the `PATH` on Windows and takes the account's login shell elsewhere), Pi reads `shellPath` only from its own `settings.json` (and otherwise finds Git Bash in `%ProgramFiles%`, then `bash.exe` on the `PATH`, which a Store copy of Git Bash is on), and Grok and Antigravity document none: each is logged at `info` as "shell X is not passed: why". The variables reach the harness the way the cache-lifetime ones do (`kind::Harness::with_shell_env`): added to each connection's environment, a profile's own variable of the same name winning.

`shell/list` (grant `terminal`, or `store.read`) answers `{"shells": [Shell...], "setting", "default"?: Shell, "problem"?, "agents": [{"entry", "harness", "shell", "own", "id"?, "env"?: [names], "notice"?, "problem"?}]}`: the shells found, `[host] shell` as written and the shell it names (or why none), and for each harness entry which setting applies, the variables its harness is told (names only) and why it cannot be told.

## Serving (`canoa_host::serve`)

```rust
pub const MAX_LINE: usize = 16 * 1024 * 1024;
pub const MAX_UNPROVEN: usize = 64;
pub const PROOF_LIMIT: Duration = 15 s;
pub const FIRST_MESSAGE: Duration = 60 s;
pub const ACP_CAPABILITY: &str = "acp/1";
pub const OUTPUT_LIMIT: usize = 1024 * 1024;   // what a provider's process may print per connection
pub const START_LIMIT: Duration = 30 s;        // for a provider's process to connect back

#[non_exhaustive] pub struct ServeOptions { pub provider_program: PathBuf, pub program_args: Vec<OsString>, pub key_limit: Option<u64>, pub rekey: bool, pub nothing_to_serve: bool, pub state_dir: Option<PathBuf> }
pub fn ServeOptions::new(provider_program: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> ServeOptions   // each method's defaults: key limit 2^38, rekey on
pub const fn ServeOptions::with_ask_wait(self, wait: Duration) -> ServeOptions   // how long a form put to the person (a sign-in's, an install's) waits for its answer, and how long its request stays bound; 4 minutes by default, and the application takes no request bound for more than 5
pub const fn ServeOptions::with_key_limit(self, bytes: u64) -> ServeOptions      // no floor; above the default the log lists it as a non-default setting
pub const fn ServeOptions::with_rekey(self, rekey: bool) -> ServeOptions
pub fn ServeOptions::with_program_args(self, args: Vec<OsString>) -> ServeOptions   // before the subcommand on provider_program's command line
pub const fn ServeOptions::with_nothing_to_serve(self, allowed: bool) -> ServeOptions   // a configuration with no entry binds, and serves nothing until stopped
pub const fn ServeOptions::with_local_sources(self, on: bool) -> ServeOptions   // also serve the host's own sources over Local; see "The host's own sources"
pub fn ServeOptions::with_layers(self, layers: Layers) -> ServeOptions   // read canoa.cfg's profile again from these files at each connection to a source of the host's own; `serve` sets it
pub fn ServeOptions::with_state_dir(self, dir: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> ServeOptions   // where a private read of an agent's quota gets its empty working directory, `<dir>/usage-read`; `canoa serve` gives the home; none, and no private agent is started
pub fn ServeOptions::with_config_file(self, file: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> ServeOptions   // the canoa.cfg a key granted `config.write` edits; `canoa serve` gives the file `--config` lists last; none, and `config/*` is refused
pub fn ServeOptions::with_home_dir(self, dir: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> ServeOptions   // the home a key granted `folders` may browse; the environment's (HOME, or USERPROFILE on Windows) when not given
pub fn Serving::restart_flag(&self) -> Arc<AtomicBool>   // raised once a key asked for `host/restart`; `canoa serve` then ends with `cli::STATUS_RESTART`
pub fn ServeOptions::with_account_layers(self, layers: Layers) -> ServeOptions   // the canoa.cfg files the control channel's `accounts/*` read and write (`Layers::own` is written); with the state directory as the home they keep accounts in; `canoa serve` gives the layers it resolved; none, and the control channel manages no accounts

pub fn Serving::bind(config: &Config, identity: coqui_link::auth::Identity, store: TrustStore, options: &ServeOptions) -> Result<Serving, ServeError>
pub fn Serving::method(&self) -> coqui_link::auth::Method   // the identity's: every entry proves by it
pub fn Serving::peer(&self) -> PeerId          // the identity's id, which a client pins
pub fn Serving::listening(&self) -> &[Listening]
pub fn Serving::stopper(&self) -> Stopper      // Clone, Send: for another thread
pub fn Serving::local(&self) -> Local          // Clone, Send: the way into every entry from inside this process; outlives run(self)
pub fn Local::entries(&self) -> Vec<&str>      // the entries it reaches, in the configuration's order
pub fn Local::open(&self, entry: &str) -> Result<Link, LocalError>   // a connection inside this process: no proof, no trust store; the entry's agent starts on the first message. `@control` is the control channel; `@tools` offers this process's tools to the host's sessions
pub fn Local::link_secret(&self) -> Option<Zeroizing<[u8; 32]>>   // the secret this host shares with the application in this process, for secret prompts (link-hmac-sha256); never over a wire or to an agent
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum LocalError { NoEntry, Stopped, Thread }
pub fn Link::send(&self, message: &[u8]) -> Result<(), LinkError>    // waits up to 30 s while 256 are unread
pub fn Link::receive(&self, timeout: Option<Duration>) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, LinkError>   // None: the other end closed and all was read
pub fn Link::close(&self)                      // ends both ends and the agent; dropping a Link does it
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum LinkError { TooLong, Timeout, Ended }
pub fn Serving::run(self)                      // takes connections until Stopper::stop or the process ends; returns with every listener closed and every agent ended
pub fn Stopper::stop(&self)                    // ends every listener, link and agent
pub fn Stopper::stop_agents(&self)             // ends every agent process running, and each later one before it serves; the listeners stay; returns once all have ended, whichever thread ends them
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Listening { pub name: String, pub kind: &'static str, pub url: String, pub harness: Option<String>, pub transport: Option<String>, pub title: Option<String>, pub local: bool, pub missing: Vec<String> }   // local: no address (url is empty); missing: programs a local entry needs that were not found; url: ws://ADDRESS, or pipe:NAME / unix:PATH for an entry on a pipe, or the endpoint the provider says a peer dials for an entry on a transport (transport: its id)
pub fn Listening::on_address(&self) -> bool    // whether a browser can dial it; false for a pipe entry and for one on a transport   // kind: "agent", "provider", "tool" or "control" (the control channel: `/canoa.json` lists it, as ``, only when `[host.control] listen` is an address); harness: a harness entry's provider id; title: an agent entry's title
```

- `stop_agents` ends each agent and each provider process with what it started (job object on Windows, process group on Unix), and turns every later proven connection away before its agent serves it. The listeners stay bound.
- `stop` does that and ends the rest, so an application can start a host again in its own process: each listener closes within 100 ms, when `run` returns, and a fixed address can be bound again; a proven link ends within about a second; a link still in its proof ends by `PROOF_LIMIT` at the latest, holding no address. An entry could one day be served from a stream in this process rather than a program: `run` keeps what serves a connection behind one private enum.

- `bind` binds every entry before any is served, so a failure leaves none listening. An entry that listens on a pipe is bound by `coqui-transport-pipe` with its defaults (see "A pipe or Unix socket"); a failure is `ServeError::Pipe`, and `Listening::url` is then `pipe:NAME` or `unix:PATH`, as `Listen` prints it, not a `ws://` URL. An entry on an address beyond loopback is bound as a plain WebSocket listener with `plain_beyond_loopback` set (the person chose the address) and logged at `info` as `non-default: host.agents.NAME.listen is ADDRESS, default loopback`: every message after the proof is sealed, so the address is the person's to choose.
- Each connection: the WebSocket handshake, then the proof of the identity's method, Noise (`coqui-peer-noise`) or WebCrypto (`coqui-peer-webcrypto`), within `PROOF_LIMIT`; a client of the other method fails it, and the log names both methods. Then the trust store decides (a stranger that proved its key is recorded as waiting), then the entry's `peers`, through a `canoa::Registry` holding one verified `Service` per entry and a grant of `ACP_CAPABILITY` per listed key. Every later message is sealed in the proof's session, which is checked against the store every second: a removed key loses its link.
- On an agent or tool entry, worktree requests (see "Worktrees") are answered by the host before anything else, up to 16 per connection, then the connection ends; once a first other message has come, one is answered `-32600`.
- The first message must be plain ACP: a link of streams (`coqui_link::mux`) is refused. Then a fresh process serves that connection, relayed by `coqui_acp_bridge::relay`, and is stopped with its process tree when either side ends.
- An agent entry runs `program args`. Every message from the client to it is parsed and the host's own serialization forwarded: `params` other than an object is answered `-32602`, since a list would fill a request's fields in order; `_meta` is removed at every depth, except that an `initialize` is given coqui-acp's usage opt-in again, `{"notify": <true whenever the host keeps a usage log, else the client's own wish, false unless it asked>, "account": <the entry's name>}` (the host hides the agent's `_coqui_acp/usage_update` from a client that did not ask, so the log has the spend of a client that never asks or polls) under `dev.boriken.coqui.acp/usage` in the client's capabilities (`clientCapabilities`, or `capabilities` from version 2), and every usage report the agent sends back (the `_coqui_acp/usage_update` notification's, and an answer to `_coqui_acp/usage`) leaves with `account` set to the entry's name whatever the agent named, so a client never reads an account the host did not assign; when the agent's `initialize` answer does not advertise the usage capability, the host stands in for it (`usage_splice`): it adds the capability with `sources: ["inferred"]`, reads each failed `session/prompt` answer (the error's message and data) and each `refusal` (what the agent said in that turn) for the entry's limit phrases, keeps one report per entry shared by its connections (bucket `inferred`, exhausted, `account` the entry's name, `revision` and `observed_at` the host's), answers `_coqui_acp/usage` itself without forwarding it and sends `_coqui_acp/usage_update` to a client that opted in with `notify` when the report changes; an inferred hit ends at its `resets_at`, else at `observed_at` plus its `retry_after`, else when a turn of the agent ends well, and a good turn does not clear one whose end has not come; with `limit_phrases = []` the host adds and answers nothing; an agent that advertises the capability is untouched; before the `initialize` answer shows the agent does not advertise it (and after an `initialize` that failed) the host only forwards, and while it speaks for the agent it drops the agent's own `_coqui_acp/usage_update` and any answer to a usage request it answered; agent text is kept only for a session with a prompt open; a request whose `params.mcpServers` holds anything but `http` or `sse` over `https`, or `acp`, is answered `-32602` and never reaches the agent; with `working_roots`, so is one whose `cwd` or `additionalDirectories` is outside them; what is not one JSON object is answered `-32700` or `-32600`. A refused notification is dropped.
- `usage = "<reader id>"` on an agent entry (`program`, never `harness`) makes the host read the agent's quota as well, through the `usage_reader` provider of that id that a bundled agent's crate offers (`canoa::usage_reader`; `antigravity` is `canoa-antigravity`'s, the one value today; an id is held to what an id is when canoa.cfg is read, and the host looks for it among the bundled agents' readers and those of the libraries and sources of canoa.cfg's entries and extensions when it serves (`serve` stops with `ServeError::Usage` naming the entry when none offers it); an `[agents.NAME]` command that is a bundled preset's own program and arguments, such as `agy acp`, gets its preset's reader). The host owns when to read (`usage_splice/reader.rs`) and the reader owns what a reading is: it describes its id, its bucket prefix and its timings (`ReaderInfo`), and opens a reading for each connection that is told the agent's `session/new` answer (`UsageReading::opened`, which Antigravity's takes the models from) and asked for the figures of a process (`UsageReading::read`). The `initialize` answer's capability then says `sources: ["reported", "inferred"]` and `needs_session: true`, and the entry's report is the buckets read (Antigravity's, from coqui-acp's `AgyQuota`: ids `agy:<first model id>`, permille, `reported`) beside any inferred hit, its `revision` the sum of the two counts. A reading looks at the processes that are the agent's, which the host gives the reader as the `process_members` object: the one the host started and those below it (`usage_splice/members.rs`: on Windows a Toolhelp snapshot taken in the host's process, elsewhere `/bin/ps -A -o pid=,ppid=,pgid=` taking children by parent and the process group the agent leads; a table that cannot be had leaves the agent's own process; on Windows each row carries the process's creation time and a child counts only if created at or after its parent, so an orphan whose dead parent's id was reused is not adopted). The reader tries the agent's own process alone first and uses the tree from the first time that finds no port, which is the case below `canoa launch` for a profile (Antigravity's). The port that answered is kept for the agent's life and asked again with no listing; ports are listed again only after it fails (on Windows the listing is `GetExtendedTcpTable` in the host's process, so no `netstat.exe` runs). A client that asks for figures does not wait for the settle time. Antigravity's models are `session/new`'s `models.availableModels[].{modelId, name}`. A reading runs on a worker thread of the connection, never the relay thread: once the session has been open for the reader's settle time (10 seconds for Antigravity) with no turn running or its first turn has ended, never while `session/new` is being answered or a first turn runs (then at once, and after each of the reader's retry times, 2, 4, 8 and 16 seconds for Antigravity, while the agent shows nothing to read), after each prompt answer, and for `_coqui_acp/usage` from a reading younger than the reader's cache time (15 seconds), else after one; the answer and any `_coqui_acp/usage_update` are written by the connection's receive loop on its next 50 ms tick. With no session open, `_coqui_acp/usage` starts a private agent (the entry's program and args, the host's environment), initializes, opens an empty session in `<state_dir>/usage-read`, reads the whole tree of the process, retrying while the reader says it shows nothing yet, and ends it; one at a time per entry, not within the reader's cache time of the last start, and none while a session of the entry is being opened (the entry's figures as they stand answer then), the reader's limit at most (40 seconds for Antigravity). The private agent is started through the host's record of its agents, so stopping the host ends it and none starts while it stops; it is also ended when its process ends early. A request that comes while one reads waits for it. None starts for an entry with `profiles` (its environment is not applied to the private agent, so it would read the host's account, not the connection's) or when `ServeOptions::state_dir` is none; the answer is then what the entry holds. The entry's buckets are shared by its connections, except for an entry with `profiles`, where each connection keeps its own, so no connection is answered from another's reading. The account's name, email and picture never leave the reader.
- **An agent entry with `profiles`** runs `provider_program launch -- PROGRAM ARGS...` instead, whose first line of standard input the host writes: the profile's environment (see `profile::launch`). The client chooses a profile by name in its first message, `initialize`, under `params._meta["dev.boriken.canoa/profile"]` (`profile::PROFILE_META`); none takes the first. A first message that is not `initialize` (after any worktree requests, which the host answers itself, see "Worktrees") is answered `-32600` (`-32602` when it names a profile), a name the entry does not offer or a value that is not text `-32602`, each logged, and then no reference is read and no process starts; a choice in any later message is answered `-32602` and logged. A reference that cannot be resolved (`from_env` unset, `from_file` unreadable) answers `-32603` and starts nothing. Each overlay the profile names is read from `overlays/NAME` beside canoa.cfg (4096 files, 64 MiB at most), with no link or excluded name, and must have the digest `overlays/NAME.json` records; else it is left out and reported. An overlay is read whole once per process and kept while its record's digest and every file's path, size and modification time stay as they were; the interpreters hooks run with are looked for once per `PATH` and `PATHEXT` value (looked for again when one has gone), and what a killed host left in the temporary directory is swept once per process, on a thread of its own. A skill an overlay names from elsewhere is delivered like a bundled one only from what the app fetched, which the host never fetches itself: `overlays/.skills/LOWERCASE-NAME/` (the files its tree digest covers, 1024 files and 16 MiB at most) and `overlays/.skills/LOWERCASE-NAME.json` (`name`, `source`, `digest`, `overlays`, ...). It is used when the record's `name` is the skill's, its `overlays` lists this overlay, for a source entry its `source` is the entry's and the entry's `pin` (if any) and canoa.cfg's `[skill_pins]` entry for the name (if any) are both its `digest`, the record is a JSON object of at most 64 KiB, neither it, `.skills` nor the skill's directory is a link, the files have exactly that digest, and they pass `coqui_aos::read_fetched_skill` (a `SKILL.md` with YAML front matter naming it). Otherwise it is not delivered and the report says why: `not supported` with `from SOURCE, not fetched` or `a named skill; no catalog resolved it here` when nothing applies, `refused` when a record or the files do not check. The skills of all the profile's overlays are planned together (`coqui_aos::resolve_skills`, no catalogs): a skill from elsewhere never takes a name another overlay's skill holds, whatever its overlay's `priority`; one kept out is reported `refused` under `skills.NAME` with its overlay, a name more than one overlay offers under `skills.NAME.collision` with the overlay delivered, and a delivered one from plain `http`/`git://` or a private network address under `skills.NAME.insecure-transport` or `skills.NAME.private-source`. A delivered skill with no pin, from the overlay or `[skill_pins]`, is also reported under `skills.NAME.unpinned`, and one whose front matter has keys beyond `name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility` and `metadata` under `skills.NAME.skill-keys`. A client may also narrow the overlays one connection starts with, by `initialize`'s `_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/overlays"]` (`profile::OVERLAYS_META`), a list of names (anything else is `-32602` and logged): only the profile's overlays among them are read, each other the profile lists is reported `off` under `overlay` ("not chosen for this session"), and a name the profile does not list adds nothing and is reported `not supported`. The person's own layers and the agent's are never narrowed. The agent's `initialize` answer decides the harness (from `agentInfo.name`, else an entry named `Claude`, `Codex`, `Gemini`, `Antigravity` or `Pi`) and what it takes (`systemPrompt`, `additionalDirectories`, MCP transports, by ACP v1 or v2 capability paths); then every `session/new`, `session/load` and `session/resume`, after the scope above, gets the context as `systemPrompt` (`append`, before a client's own blocks), the MCP servers in `mcpServers` (v1's stdio form untyped; a client's server of a name the profile holds, started or not, is `-32602`), skills written once per connection to a private temporary directory (removed after the agent ends) as the Claude plugin `canoa-profile` in `_meta.claudeCode.options.plugins` or a directory Codex or Antigravity (`agy acp`, `agentInfo.name` `Antigravity ACP Server`) reads (its `skills` folder under the Agent Skills directory inside) in `additionalDirectories`; an agent of either that does not advertise `additionalDirectories` is `not supported`. How an agent is given its skills and its hooks plugin (a local plugin loaded through `_meta`, an additional directory, or not at all), what its own hooks give for the events ACP does not show, and which table of words says what became of the access are the agent's own answers to `agent_vendor` (`skills_delivery`, `hooks_delivery`, `hook_support`, `access_target`); the host holds none of them. A session request before `initialize` is answered `-32600`. A stdio server's `command` goes absolute: as written, from its layer's directory when it holds a separator, else the first match on the host's `PATH` (absolute entries only; `PATHEXT` on Windows), refused when none is found. An overlay's MCP server starts only when `allow_mcp` names it at its digest (`sha256` of `{"layer", "server"}` as the view writes them, and `"program"`, the stdio program found); off, it is logged with that digest. The answer to `initialize` and to each session request carries `_meta[PROFILE_META]` = `{"name", "harness", "summary", "items": [{"key", "layer", "state", "how", "digest"?}]}`, `state` being `applied`, `not supported`, `off` or `refused`: names, never values. No project profile is read.
- **What a harness entry is given is what its provider declares.** `serve::harness_settings` puts the access, `allow_client_access`, `allow_bypass`, `modes` and `setting_sources` into a harness's settings only where the provider's settings schema declares the key (`kind::declares_setting`; `vendors::bundled_declares` for the bundled ones), so a provider that runs a stdio agent as it is gets the program and the limits of a connection and nothing more. The per-agent answers beyond a description (an account's variables, a login's file, a shell word, why skills are not taken, the sessions a program kept) are `AgentVendor` methods asked through `vendors::behave(agent, ..)`, which reaches the bundled agents' providers; an agent a library from canoa.cfg adds is described by `describe` and answers none of them.
- **A harness entry** runs an `acp_harness` provider, chosen by its id, in this process for each connection, over the same channel and scope as an agent entry. `bind` gives each entry a registry of its own: `claude`, `codex`, `pi` and `opencode` (coqui-acp-claude's, coqui-acp-codex's, coqui-acp-pi's and coqui-acp-harness's stdio providers, compiled into the host and registered with `local_providers!`) for an entry with no `library` or `source`, and only what the entry's file offers for one that has it (config refuses an origin on `claude`, `codex`, `pi` or `opencode`), the way a provider entry loads it but in this process, not a process of its own; it then builds each entry's instance from the entry's settings, which the provider checks against its schema (`ServeError::Harness` names the entry and the reason, no value). A connection starts with `StartRequest` (the entry's `working_roots`, which hold a session and a resumed Codex thread; with a profile also the profile's environment and, when the entry has roots, the directory the connection's skills are written under, `Connection::skills`; an entry with no roots gives none), the host's `LocalSpawner` (the provider starts its program as this host's child, ended with everything it started when the connection or the host ends), a sink that hands the provider's messages to the client (no keeper: the harness reports no account's renewal, the account provider's watch does), and the cache lifetime the connection asks: the client's choice for the session in `initialize`'s `_meta` (`CACHE_META`), else the entry's `cache_lifetime`, else the client's default for its kind of session, else the provider's own (`default`). Claude Code's provider sets `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL` and `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL` to `5m` for `short` and `1h` for `long`; Codex's says it ignores it (`canoa options` prints each provider's sentence as `cache_lifetime`, and an entry's own setting as `cache_lifetime_setting`). With a profile the host builds a second instance with `allow_client_plugins = true` where the provider has that setting (Claude Code's), for the one plugin it writes. `stop_agents` closes every connection at once, which makes each provider close its sessions and end each command line with what it started, waits for no client that has stopped reading, and returns once each provider has said `closed` or after 10 s each. A provider that ends the connection with a failure ends it, and the reason is logged at `info`. How the built instance departs from its schema's defaults (Guatiao's `schema::differences`) and the provider's `non_defaults` are logged at `info` as `non-default: host.agents.NAME: SETTING is VALUE, default D`, so `settings = { allow_bypass = true }` is logged as `bypass = true` is. The host sets `max_requests` to 64 for `claude` and `codex` unless `settings` say another. A provider's notices (a version it was not tested with, a failure of its plumbing, the end of its program mid-session, how and never what it last wrote) come through the spawner's `notice` and are logged at `info` with the entry and the kind (`notice`, `exited`, `fault`); so are its own `closed` status and reason. The `mode` option offers every mode the agent has, Bypass included, unless the entry's `settings` name `modes` or `allow_bypass`: a paired key is the person's own, and the access a session runs with is its profile's (the standard access when the entry offers none). A `settings` value for either is logged at `info` as configuration.
- A profile reads an agent's `initialize` answer where its version puts what it says: `capabilities` and `info` in version 2, `agentCapabilities` and `agentInfo` in version 1. `coqui-acp-claude` is Claude Code and `coqui-acp-codex` Codex.
- A tool entry is served as an agent entry whose program is `provider_program` itself: `provider_program tool --name NAME --spec JSON`, the entry as `ToolSpec` serializes it. So the scope above applies to its clients, and the process, with every tool process it starts, ends with the connection.
- A provider entry is not filtered: its facade reads the client's `_meta` launch values (`context`, `model`, `effort`, an opaque `meta`) and hands them to the provider, and refuses an environment name. What a provider does with them is the provider's.
- A provider entry runs `provider_program provider (--library L | --source S) --provider ID --max-sessions N --directory D --settings-on-stdin [--import-search library|path] [--working-root R]... --connect A`, so a library or source is loaded only in that connection's process, which runs in `D`. The command line holds paths, names and the address only. For each connection the host listens on a loopback port of its own, `A`; writes the entry's `source_settings` and `settings` and a one-time token of 32 random bytes as the first line of the process's standard input (`HostLine`), then closes it; and takes the first connection to `A` that sends the token within `START_LIMIT`, up to 8 that do not. ACP runs on that socket alone. What the process writes to standard output or error goes to the host's standard error, `OUTPUT_LIMIT` bytes per connection in all, the rest read and dropped after one warning; none of it reaches the client. The process and what it starts are ended with the connection, by job object on Windows and process group elsewhere. Its facade refuses any `mcpServers` and checks `working_roots` itself.
- On Windows a source entry whose `import_search` is `path` is logged as `bind` binds it: at `info` as `non-default: host.providers.NAME.import_search is path, default library: …` when the file names it, at `warn` as `host.providers.NAME.import_search is path, because import_dirs names no directory: …` (saying to set `import_dirs` or `import_search = "library"`) when the host took it as the default. `Origin::Source::import_search_named` tells the two apart.
- A provider entry whose `source_settings` hold `isolation = "inherit"` is logged at `info` as `bind` binds it: `non-default: host.providers.NAME.source_settings.isolation is inherit, default shipped`. No other source setting is judged.
- `max_peers` connections per entry at once; past it a proven peer is turned away. Past `MAX_UNPROVEN` handshakes at once across entries, a new connection is dropped.

```rust
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum ServeError { NothingToServe, Listen { entry: String, failure: Failure }, Pipe { entry: String, error: pipe::Error }, Transport { entry: String, what: String }, Proof(String), Harness { entry: String, what: String }, Accounts { what: String } }
```

## Shared sessions (`shared = true`)

An agent or harness entry with `shared = true` keeps each session's agent running in the host, held by coqui-acp's `coqui-acp-hub`, so a session outlives the connection that opened it and several clients take part in it. An entry without it is served as before: one agent per connection, ended with it.

- **Opening.** The first client of a session speaks to the agent as it does on an unshared entry (`initialize`, `authenticate`, `session/new` or `session/load`, with the profile and layers it always gets); once the agent has answered the one that opens the session, the host's hub takes over that client and every later message goes through it. One connection serves one session: a second `session/new` on it is refused (open another connection).
- **Attaching.** A connection whose first message is `initialize` while the entry has a running session is answered from what the agent offered (`loadSession` and `sessionCapabilities.resume` on); then `session/load` or `session/resume` naming a running session of that entry attaches to it, an extension request (a method starting `_`, such as the usage an app asks for once connected) sent before that is answered with an error and the connection goes on, and anything else opens a session of its own. `session/load` replays everything the hub retained (from `_meta["dev.boriken.coqui.acp/hub"].cursor`, an opaque value taken from an earlier message's `_meta[...].seq` or an earlier answer's `cursor`, only what follows it), answers `{ _meta: { <key>: { cursor, truncated } } }`, then asks again any permission request still open. Updates that happened before the first client's `session/load` of an agent's own history are not in the replay (they went to that client directly). A key already attached takes over its own earlier connection.
- **Surviving a restart.** With `shared_keep_log` (on by default) and a home, the host writes each session's log to `<home>/shared_sessions/<hash>.jsonl` as it is logged (a header line naming the entry and the session, then one update a line as the hub fanned it out; owner-only on Unix; rewritten trimmed to `shared_log_entries` and `shared_log_bytes` when it has grown to twice that). A session the person closed, the idle rule ended or whose agent exited leaves no file; one the host's stopping or a crash interrupted leaves it, and it is read for as long as `shared_idle_seconds` from its last write (older ones are removed when the host starts, and when read). The agent is not kept: after the restart the first client to `session/load` or `session/resume` the session by name starts a new agent that loads it, and the hub goes on from the kept log with the cursors unchanged. That client's own request is answered by the hub: the kept log is replayed (after the cursor it names, or all of it), the agent's own replay of its history is not sent (the log holds it), and the answer is the agent's result with the hub's `_meta` cursor. A cursor the log cannot continue from (`cursor_ahead`, `cursor_expired` or `bad_cursor` in `error.data.reason`, code -32602) is answered with an error and attaches nothing, to any client; the client loads again with no cursor. With no kept log (`shared_keep_log = false`, no home, or none for that session) the first client back is given the agent's own history as before, its cursor is not consulted, and the log starts past every cursor an earlier log can have issued (the time in milliseconds), so a later client holding an old cursor is refused with `cursor_expired` instead of being replayed from the wrong place. Only the log is kept: the running turn, queued prompts and open permission requests are not.
- **Taking part.** Every attached client gets every update, a prompt is stamped with the client that sent it (`_meta[...] = { client, label, prompt }`) and queued behind a running turn, a permission request goes to every attached client and the first answer wins (the others get `_dev.boriken.coqui.acp/hub_permission_closed`), `session/cancel` from any client cancels the turn, presence is `_dev.boriken.coqui.acp/hub_presence` (each client is sent the list with its own entry marked `"self": true`). A client's id is its key's id, its label the entry's name, its role `operator`, its subject `owner` for this host's own key and `key:<id>` for any other.
- **Who may attach.** The hub's policy is the host's own, asked again at each action: the key must still be paired (`peers` store) and listed by the entry (`peers`), and, to attach, trusted with at least the control grants of the key that opened the session, since the session's tools, secrets and delegation are the opener's. Every key an entry admits and that passes these may read, prompt, answer permissions in and end every session of that entry. A task another agent handed out is never kept.
- **Ending.** `session/close` `{ sessionId }` from an attached client answers `{}` and ends the session. `shared_idle_seconds` (86400; `0` never) ends one nobody is attached to for that long, counted from the hub's `last_activity` (an agent's update, a client's message, an attach or detach); the hub itself never reaps. The agent exiting, or the host stopping, ends it, and the attached clients are told (`_dev.boriken.coqui.acp/hub_ended`); the log of one ended by the host's stopping goes on being kept (see Surviving a restart), that of any other ending is removed. At most `max_peers` sessions of an entry are kept at once (the same bound as connections); a client that stops reading is detached once 256 MiB wait for it.
- **Listing.** `sessions/running` `{}` on the control channel (`ledger.read`) -> `{"sessions": [{"entry", "session", "clients": [{"id", "label", "role"}], "lastActivity", "cursor", "turnRunning", "permissionsWaiting", "title"?, "cwd"?}]}`, newest activity first: the sessions of every shared entry that are open now and that the asking key could attach to under the rules above (an unpaired or unlisted key, or one trusted with less than the opener, sees none of them). `lastActivity` is the host's clock in milliseconds since the Unix epoch, `cursor` the log's head (an attach from it replays nothing), `title` the last `session_info_update` the agent sent and `cwd` the folder its opener named; the app's own link is listed as the host's own key.
- `shared_permission_window_seconds` is the hub's `permission_window_ms`: once a permission request has waited that long with nobody answering, the host logs it at `info` (`has waited for an answer past the entry's window`) and leaves it open for whoever attaches next.
- The hub's notes (a refused action, a permission answered, an unanswered one) are logged at `info` with the entry; nothing is auto-denied. Two sessions of one entry whose agent gave the same id are both kept, but only the older can be attached to.

## A transport provider (`transport = "<id>"`)

An entry listens through a provider of coqui-transport-guatiao's `transport` kind when it names one: `transport = "pipe"` or `"websocket"` (compiled in, registered in a registry of the entry's own) or the id of an outside provider that `transport_library` or `transport_source` loads, into a registry of its own too, so an id is resolved from that entry's own file and no other (`ServeError::Transport` names the entry; its words quote no setting's value). `transport_settings` is the provider's configuration, checked against its schema first. With no `transport` key nothing changes: `listen` is an address, `pipe:NAME` or `unix:PATH` as always.

- `bind` starts the listener, so a refused configuration, an endpoint the provider cannot read or one that is taken stops `serve` there, and `Listening::url` is the endpoint the provider says a peer can dial (a port asked for as `0` is the one the system chose). The provider's own `non_defaults` are logged at `info` as `non-default: host.agents.NAME.transport has SETTING at VALUE, default DEFAULT`, and an outside provider's code loading into the host's process is logged too.
- Each connection the provider hands over is a `coqui_transport::Channel` the host's proof runs on, as on a socket: the same trust store, `peers`, `max_peers` and 64 unproven connections shared with the sockets, every message after the proof sealed. Stopping the host closes the listener and waits (up to 5 s) for the provider to say it has stopped, so the endpoint is free when `run` returns. Peers wait for the accept loop in a queue of 16 and past it are turned away; a connection holds at most 64 messages for the host, and a message over the limit in force (`set_max_message`: the proof's size until the proof is done) ends it. The provider's own message limit applies as well, so an outside provider that takes less than the sealed session's records fails the session.
