- What the transport is, not the host, decides the framing, who can reach it and the wire: the built-in `pipe` frames with a big-endian `u32` (the entry's `listen = "pipe:NAME"` frames little-endian, so a client of one is not a client of the other); the built-in `websocket` sends text messages unless `listen.frames` is `"binary"`, which the proof needs, and it takes the host's `[host] origins` as its `listen.origins` when the entry lists none (logged; an entry's own list replaces them), and listens beyond loopback only with `listen.plain_beyond_loopback` in `transport_settings`, which the host logs, the link being sealed after the proof as on any address. Each upgrade runs on a thread of its own, at most `max_handshakes` (default 64) at once, so a silent peer delays nobody. The built-ins cap a message at 1 MiB (the `channel.max_message` of `pipe`, `listen.limits.max_message` of `websocket`), below the sealed session's 16 MiB records: raise it in `transport_settings` for an entry that carries more. Two entries cannot name one endpoint; endpoints asking for port 0 are not one. A web page is never offered such an entry: `Listening::on_address` is false.
- Only the entries' listening goes through a provider. Dialling (`a2a` sources, delegation), `wss://` and WebTransport do not: they wait for the kind to carry TLS configuration.

## A pipe or Unix socket (`canoa_host::pipe`)

```rust
pub use coqui_transport_pipe::{Error, Listener, PipeName, Stream};
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct PipeChannel;   // Send, Sync; coqui_transport::Channel
impl PipeChannel {
    pub fn new(stream: coqui_transport_pipe::Stream, max_message: usize) -> PipeChannel   // a connected stream
    pub fn connect(name: &PipeName, max_message: usize) -> Result<PipeChannel, Error>     // a client; Error::NotListening / AccessDenied / TimedOut
    pub fn peer(&self) -> io::Result<coqui_transport_pipe::OsIdentity>                    // who the system says is at the other end
}
```

- An entry whose `listen` is `pipe:NAME` or `unix:PATH` is served as one on an address is: a fresh agent per proven connection, the same proof of the host's method, the same trust store (a key that is not paired is turned away and waits for `canoa accept`), `peers`, `max_peers`, worktree requests and the 64 unproven connections at once, shared with the sockets. Nothing differs after the connection opens.
- What differs is the transport: the operating system's access control stands before the proof, and `coqui-transport-pipe`'s defaults are kept (Windows: a security descriptor for the current user alone, plus the integrity label of an elevated host, the first instance of the name taken so nobody else holds it, remote clients refused; Unix: a `0600` socket in a directory only its owner (or root) can write, a stale socket replaced and a live one not). Neither replaces the key proof, and the proof does not trust the access control: the same user's other processes reach the pipe, and so would anyone the descriptor let in. The peer's process and user are logged at `debug`.
- A message is a little-endian `u32` length and then its body (coqui-transport-web's length-prefixed stream format), with no WebSocket handshake and no origin check. `PipeChannel` reads on demand with no read-ahead, so a bound `set_max_message` changes applies to the next message the peer sends. Zero length, over the bound, or a peer that leaves mid-message ends the channel (`Failure::Protocol` or `PathLost`); a clean end between messages is `Ok(None)`. A write that times out ends the channel (a cancelled pipe write on Windows leaves the stream's position unknown).
- Every peer on pipes shares one bucket of the trust store's waiting queue, since there is no address to tell them apart by.
- The served web page never offers or allows a pipe entry: `/canoa.json` and the page's Content-Security-Policy leave it out (`Listening::on_address`), and no browser can reach it.
- A pipe name on Windows is visible to every user: the first instance is taken at bind, so a second host on one name is `ServeError::Pipe` (`NameTaken`), but a client that connects before any host bound reaches whoever bound first, and only the key proof tells it from the host.
## The browser extension's native host (`canoa_host::native`, `[host.native]`, `canoa native`)

```rust
pub const HOST_NAME: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa";            // what the extension passes to connectNative
pub const KNOWN_EXTENSIONS: &[&str];                         // the id manifest.json's key gives, allowed without being named
pub const MAX_PIECE: usize = 768_000;                        // a link's bytes per native message, either way
pub fn door_name(home: &Path) -> Result<PipeName, pipe::Error>        // Windows canoa-native-<16 hex of the home's path>; else <home>/native.sock
pub fn pair_door_name(home: &Path) -> Result<PipeName, pipe::Error>  // beside it, bound only by `canoa pair --native`
pub fn relay(home: &Home, origin: &str, input: &mut impl Read, output: impl Write + Send + 'static, ended: impl Fn() + Send + Sync + 'static) -> io::Result<()>
pub fn install(home: &Home, program: &Path, options: &Install) -> Result<Vec<String>, Error>
pub fn uninstall(home: &Home, name: Option<&str>, profiles: &[PathBuf]) -> Result<(), Error>
pub fn status(home: &Home, name: Option<&str>, profiles: &[PathBuf]) -> Result<Status, Error>
pub struct Launch { pub program: PathBuf, pub args: Vec<String> }     // <home>/native/launch.json; Launch::read, Launch::start
pub fn record_origin(home: &Path, peer: &str, origin: &str) -> io::Result<()>
pub fn origin_allows(home: &Path, peer: &str, origin: &str) -> bool
```

- The browser starts the native host with the extension's origin as its first argument (`chrome-extension://ID/`, then `--parent-window=N` on Windows): `main` turns that into the hidden `canoa native-host ORIGIN`, which is `relay` on standard input and output. On Windows the browser runs it through `cmd.exe /d /s /c` with named pipes for standard streams. One port is one link: the relay ends when the browser closes its input or the door closes.
- **Native messages** (a `u32` in native byte order, then JSON; to the browser at most 1 MiB, from it at most 1 MiB plus a little): `{"op":"hello"}` answers `{"reply":"hello","running":true,peer,method,name,version,entries:[{name,label,kind,local}],control,hostname?,addresses}` from the host's door (`hostname` this computer's name in lower case, `hostnames` that and its DNS name when the system gives one, `addresses` up to 16 of its network addresses, never loopback or link-local: what the extension counts as "on this computer", beside loopback; a claim that only narrows what a page may do, and given by the stopped relay's `hello` too), or `{"reply":"hello","running":false}` (`"door":false` when a host holds the home with its door shut). `{"op":"open","entry":NAME}` (an entry, `@control`, or `@pair`) answers `{"reply":"open","opened":true}` or `{"reply":"open","error":...}`; after it only `{"d":BASE64}` pieces go either way, and `{"closed":true}` ends the link. `{"op":"discover","seconds":N}` searches itself, whether or not a host runs, and answers `{"reply":"discover","candidates":[...]}` as `discover/nearby` does (below), or `{"reply":"discover","error":...}`; it needs no key, since the browser that may start the program is the one the install allowed, and what it returns is claims. `{"op":"start"}` runs `launch.json`'s program, detached (`DETACHED_PROCESS`, plus `CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` where the browser's job allows it; a new process group on Unix) and only when no host holds the home; it answers `{"reply":"start","started":PID}` or an error. Anything else is `{"error":"unknown op"}`.
- **The door** (`serve::door`) is bound by `Serving::bind` in the home (`ServeOptions::state_dir`), unless `[host.native] enabled = false`, a portal, or no state directory. A connection's first message is `{"canoa_native":1,"open":NAME,"origin":ORIGIN}` (at most 64 KiB, within 5 s); `@hello` is answered and closed, anything else is answered `{"opened":true}` and then served as `connection` serves any link: the proof of the host's method, the trust store, the entry's `peers`, scope and `max_peers`. `@control` is the control channel with `[host.native] control` for every paired key: through the door that set replaces whatever grants `[host.control]` gives the key. `@tools` is where a program offers its tools to the host's sessions (**Tools a program offers**, below): only a key the extension's PIN pairing bound to its origin, and holding `tools.provide` in that same set, is let in, and one without the grant is refused with `{"type":"refused"}` without it. A door that cannot be bound is logged and left out, and why is written to `<home>/native/door-error.txt`, which a relay's `hello` (`"door":false,"why"`) and `canoa native status` (`door_error`) say. Unproven connections count against `MAX_UNPROVEN`.
- **`[host.native]`**: `enabled` (default true) and `control`, a list of grant names (default `ledger.read`, `ledger.write`, `worktree`, `store.read`, `folders`, `config.read`, `config.write`, `discover`, `tools.provide`). `config.write.sessions`, `overlay.write`, `host.restart`, `upload`, `terminal`, `voice.setup` and `hosting` are refused there. `store/index` is served by `overlay.read` (the catalog entries) and `store.programs` (the program entries), neither in the default list: the extension reads the catalog index only when `control` names `overlay.read`, and a `refresh` it asks for is refused, since it needs `overlay.write`.
- **The door's other end:** a relay speaks to the door only when the system says the server end is held by the user it runs as (the pipe's owner on Windows, the peer's credentials on Unix); one it cannot place is not spoken to. A record of origins (`origins.json`) that exists and cannot be read allows no key through the door, and `record_origin` will not write over it; a pairing that cannot record its origin takes its key back out of the trust store.
- **Pairing**: `canoa pair --native [--until-stdin-closes] [--method M]` binds `pair_door_name`, prints `{"pin","peer","method","native":true}`, takes one connection at a time that asks for `@pair`, runs the PIN pairing on it, records the origin the browser reported beside the key (`<home>/native/origins.json`) and prints `{"paired":PEER}`. The door then turns that key away from any other origin; a key with no record (`canoa accept`) is bound to none. The host never holds a PIN, so pairing works with the host stopped.
- **Registration** (`canoa native install [--name NAME] [--extension-id ID]... [--browser chrome|edge|chromium|chrome-for-testing]... [--profile DIR]... [--launch PROGRAM [-- ARGS...]]`, `uninstall`, `status`): `dev.boriken.canoa` (`--name` registers another name the extension does not look for, for trying a registration), `type stdio`, `path` the running executable, `allowed_origins` `KNOWN_EXTENSIONS` plus the given ids. Windows: the manifest at `<home>/native/NAME.json` and `HKCU\Software\{Google\Chrome,Chromium,Microsoft\Edge}\NativeMessagingHosts\NAME`. Linux and macOS: `NativeMessagingHosts/NAME.json` in each browser's profile directory that exists, and in each `--profile`. `--launch` (default this program's `serve`) is recorded in `<home>/native/launch.json`; `status` prints `{name,registered,launch,running,holder}`. Flatpak and Snap browsers cannot start a native host: not handled.

## The web app (`canoa_host::web`)

```rust
pub const DEFAULT_LISTEN: SocketAddr = 127.0.0.1:7800;
pub const HOST_INDEX: &str = "/canoa.json";
pub const BUILT_IN: bool;                       // cfg!(feature = "web")

#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Limits { pub max_connections: usize, pub max_connections_per_address: usize, pub connection_time: Duration, pub max_request_bytes: usize, pub max_requests: usize, pub request_time: Duration, pub idle_time: Duration, pub send_time: Duration, pub immutable_age: Duration }   // Copy, Eq, Default
// Default: 64, 16, 600 s, 16 KiB, 1000, 10 s, 30 s, 30 s, 365 days; each with a const builder:
pub const fn Limits::with_max_connections(self, count: usize) -> Limits   // and with_max_connections_per_address,
    // with_connection_time, with_max_request_bytes, with_max_requests, with_request_time, with_idle_time, with_send_time,
    // with_immutable_age. A time too long for the clock to add counts as a year.

pub fn WebServer::bind(web: &WebConfig, serving: &Serving) -> Result<WebServer, WebError>
pub const fn WebServer::local_addr(&self) -> SocketAddr
pub fn WebServer::url(&self) -> String          // http(s)://ADDRESS, or http(s)://hosts[0]:PORT when bound to every interface
pub fn WebServer::run(self)                     // takes connections until WebStopper::stop or the process ends; returns with the address free
pub fn WebServer::stopper(&self) -> WebStopper  // Clone, for another thread
pub fn WebStopper::stop(&self)                  // a connection already open ends after the answer it is sending, or when it next goes quiet

#[non_exhaustive] pub enum WebError { NoSite, Site { dir: PathBuf, why: &'static str }, PlainBeyondLoopback(SocketAddr), Limit(&'static str), Connect, OfficeFrame, Tls { file: PathBuf, why: String }, Listen { address: SocketAddr, source: io::Error } }
```

- `bind` refuses plain HTTP beyond loopback (`PlainBeyondLoopback`), a zero limit but `immutable_age` (`Limit`, naming the canoa.cfg setting), a `connect` source of another form than the file allows (`Connect`), an `office_frames` source that is not one `https` origin (`OfficeFrame`), a directory that cannot be resolved, holds no `index.html`, or is or holds one of `keep_out` once resolved (`Site`), and no directory in a build without `web` (`NoSite`); reads `cert` (every PEM certificate, in order) and `key` (one PEM private key) into a rustls server configuration with its safe default versions (TLS 1.2 and 1.3), the `ring` provider, ALPN `http/1.1` and no client certificate (`Tls`, naming the file, never its contents); and logs at `info` each limit above its default as `non-default: host.web.SETTING is V, default D`, a `listen` beyond loopback, and each `connect` source as `non-default: host.web.connect lets the page connect to SOURCE…`; on Unix a `key` file others may read is logged at `warn`, since nothing in the file asked for it. Plain HTTP beyond loopback stays refused: the page is not sealed like an entry's link, so whoever is on the path could rewrite the code that holds the person's key. Then it binds (`Listen`). The site's directory is resolved once, at `bind`.
- HTTP/1.1 and 1.0, `GET` and `HEAD` only (else 405 with `Allow: GET, HEAD`). Refused with 400: a request with a body (`Transfer-Encoding`, more than one `Content-Length`, or one other than 0), no `Host` or two (HTTP/1.0 included), a target that does not start with `/` (absolute-form included), anything `httparse` refuses. A `Host` that is not `localhost`, an IP address or one of `hosts`, with any port, is refused with 421, which stops a page of another site reaching this one by DNS rebinding. More than 64 headers or `max_request_bytes` is 431; a head not complete within `request_time` is 408. Every refusal closes the connection.
- **Paths.** The target's path (the query left out) is split at `/` and each segment percent-decoded; refused with 404 is any segment that is empty (but a last one), `.` or `..`, starts or ends with a dot, ends with a space, holds a control character, `/`, `\`, `:`, `*`, `?`, `"`, `<`, `>` or `|` once decoded, is not UTF-8, or is a Windows device name with any extension (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `CONIN$`, `CONOUT$`, `CLOCK$`, `COM` or `LPT` and a digit or `¹`, `²`, `³`). The file is the resolved path (every link followed) under the resolved root, each segment of which, once resolved, must pass the same rules: one that resolves outside the root, or to a name a request could not give (an NTFS short name such as `GIT~1` resolving to `.git`, a link to a dot-name), is refused with 404 and logged, whatever it is, and a directory is never listed. From the site built in, the path is looked up as written.
- **The app's routes.** A path naming nothing, or a directory, whose last segment has no `.` is answered with the root's `index.html` (200, `no-cache`); any other missing file is 404.
- **The Office add-in's routes** (`[host.web] office = true`; with it off `/office/...` is an ordinary path and nothing below applies). The host carries the add-in's task pane (the page that loads Office.js and frames the chat-only app), its dialog page and their scripts, and answers exactly these paths, each with the policy it needs and no other route changes: `/office/taskpane.html` (`script-src` adds `https://officeapis.public.onecdn.static.microsoft`, Office.js's own origin; `frame-src 'self'`; `frame-ancestors` is `https://outlook.office.com https://outlook.office365.com https://outlook.live.com https://*.office.com https://*.cloud.microsoft` and `office_frames`; no `X-Frame-Options`), `/office/dialog.html` (`script-src` adds the same origin; still `frame-ancestors 'none'` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`; no `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`, so Office's dialog keeps its link to the window that opened it), `/office/taskpane.js`, `/office/relation.js` and `/office/dialog.js` (the usual headers), `/office/app/` (the site's `index.html` under the usual policy, `script-src` unchanged, with `frame-ancestors 'self'` and the same origins and no `X-Frame-Options`: the page the task pane frames, `?mode=chat&relation=<kind>:<key>&label=` as the app reads them) and `/office/icon-16.png`, `-32`, `-64` and `-80` (the site's `icons/Icon-192.png` with `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin`, as Office draws it from its own origin). Every other path, `/office/app/deeper` and `/office/other` included, is the site's and has today's headers. Microsoft publishes no list of the origins that frame a task pane: the built-in ones are a starting list to check against each client, and Word, Excel and PowerPoint on the web (SharePoint, `officeapps.live.com`) are named only through `office_frames`. `/office/app/canoa.json` is `/canoa.json` for the app page that asks for it beside itself. The add-in's manifests are rendered by `clients/office/render.mjs`. The portal answers the same routes itself with `office = true` in its own settings (below); with it off it answers none of this.
- **The add-in on this computer** (`[host.web] office = true` and `office_local = true`, `listen` on loopback, normally with a `cert` and `key` the person names, since Office wants HTTPS). The page the task pane frames needs no PIN and no `canoa accept`: after its key has connected once and been turned away, it sends `POST /office/admit?peer=<id>` (empty body) and the host accepts that waiting key (`TrustStore::accept`), then holds it as a page's key for this run: on the control channel it holds `[host.native] control` besides its own grants (and the sessions it starts are given the tools its grants allow: the same set plus `mail` when `[host.mail]` names an account and its tools are on), and `restricted` no longer turns it away from `@control`. `/canoa.json` names the path as `admit` only to a request whose `Host` is a loopback name. The request is refused (`403`, `Cache-Control: no-store`) unless it comes from a loopback address, its `Host` is a loopback name, its `Origin` is exactly the scheme and `Host` of the request and `Sec-Fetch-Site`, when sent, is `same-origin`; `404` for a key that never connected, `403` for one the trust store knows connected from another address, `400` for no id or one that is not an id. Without `office_local`, `POST` is `405` as ever and `/canoa.json` has no `admit`. What stops others: a page of another origin cannot send that `Origin`, a name pointed at this computer fails the `Host` check, and a key the host never saw connect is not accepted; a program on this computer, or another account's on a shared computer, can send any header and so can ask for a key it has just connected with (see `clients/office/README.md`). The admitted keys are kept in `<home>/office/admitted.json` (`{"secret": bool, "keys": [ids]}`, written on each admission) and read when the host starts: a key the trust store still accepts is held as a page's key again at once, with its control grants, so a restart needs no new request; a file that cannot be read holds none, and one written without the secret is not read once `office_secret` is on. **`office_secret`**: `<home>/office/secret` (64 hexadecimal digits, made on first use, mode 0600 on Unix) must be sent as `X-Canoa-Office-Key` on `POST /office/admit`, every `/office/tools/` request and `POST /office/launch`, compared without branching on how much matched; a request without it is `403` saying the add-in's secret is needed. `web::Secret` is the file's reader; the manifest carries it as `?office_key=` (`clients/office/render.mjs --secret-file`), the pane passes it on in the frame's address and the app sends it with its admission request. **The pane's tools** (`web::Tools`, `/office/tools/`): with `office_local` the web server registers the open Office application as a provider of the host's `@tools` (`outlook`, `word` or `excel`, so `canoa-outlook`, `canoa-word`, `canoa-excel`; see "Tools a program offers") on a `Local::open("@tools")` link, carried by the page's own requests, each the page's own as for admission (loopback address, loopback `Host`, its own `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site` `same-origin` when sent, the secret when asked for): `POST open?app=NAME` registers and answers `{"session"}` (`409` with the host's reason when refused, `400` for another name; a newer pane of the same application replaces the older), `GET poll?session=S&after=N` waits up to 20 s and answers `{"first":F,"messages":[...]}` (the host's `open`, `mcp` and `close` messages, as on the door, message `i` being number `F+i`; the host numbers what it sends a pane from 1 and keeps each message until a poll names a number at or past it as `after`, so a poll whose answer the browser dropped is followed by one that gets the same messages again, answered at once when any are held; `after` absent is 0, one that is not a number is `400`; at most 256 messages or 16 MiB are kept, the oldest going first; one poll of a pane takes from its link at a time, a later one waiting behind it; a link that ended answers what is held first, then `410`; `410 {"ended":true}` when the session is gone), `POST send?session=S` takes one provider message as its body (`mcp` or `close`, at most 4 MiB; the only request this server reads a body of), `POST close?session=S` ends it. A pane that makes no request for 45 s is closed by a thread that looks every 5 s, which takes its tools from every session. The host answers a provider server's `initialize` itself and, for `outlook`, adds `instructions` in its own words (`client_tools::instructions`: ask `outlook_open_item` which message is open, then `mail_related` with its Internet message id as `message_id`, and treat what comes back as data); no provider's text is ever passed to the model as instructions, which is why the open message reaches the agent as a tool's answer and not as a line the pane sends. The page's own connection still never opens `@tools` by key: only this route, on this computer, does. **The launcher** (`canoa office-launcher`, `canoa_host::launcher`): holds `[host.web] listen` while `Home::probe` finds no host (after four looks half a second apart), serving only `WebServer::bind_launcher`: `GET /canoa.json` and `/office/app/canoa.json` answer `{"launcher":"/office/launch"}`, `/office/taskpane.html` the page `launch.html` (under the pane's policy), its scripts, and `POST /office/launch` (the page's own checks) asks it to start the host: it lets go of the address, starts what `native install` recorded in `<home>/native/launch.json` (else this program with `serve`) detached, and waits up to a minute for a host to hold the home. It lets go of the address within a look of a host taking the home, and `WebServer::bind` on a host with `office_local` waits up to five seconds for an address another program holds. The launcher is never started by the host or the app; the person starts it, or registers it with `canoa office-launcher --install`, which writes one entry in the place this system reads for the signed-in user and no other: the value `CanoaOfficeLauncher` under `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run` (written through `reg.exe`), `~/Library/LaunchAgents/dev.boriken.canoa.office-launcher.plist` (`RunAtLoad`), or `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/autostart/dev.boriken.canoa.office-launcher.desktop` (else `~/.config/autostart/`), with no elevation and nothing started at once. The entry runs this program (the `Front`'s `program`, its leading arguments too, as `launch_of` uses them), `office-launcher` and, when a config was named (`--config` or `CANOA_CONFIG`), `--config` with each file made absolute (a leading `+` and the list separator kept); it refuses a canoa.cfg with no `[host.web]` having `office` and `office_local`. `--install` again replaces the entry, `--uninstall` deletes only it (no error when it is absent), and `--status` prints `registered`, what the entry runs, whether that is what `--install` would write now (`current`), the address, whether something accepts a connection there (`answers`), whether a host holds the home (`host_runs`) and `launcher_answers` (answers and no host holds the home); `--format json` prints one object of those. On Windows `canoa.exe` is a console program, so the launcher's window stays open while it runs.
- **`/canoa.json`** (`HOST_INDEX`, the query left out, before any file of that name): `{"peer", "method", "os", "web", "entries": [{"name", "kind", "url", "harness"?, "title"?}]}`, the host's id, method, operating system (`windows`, `linux`, `macos`), `web` (`local` when the person sits at this computer, `remote` when the browser is only the interface: `[host.web] mode`, else by the request's `Host`, loopback being local; an editor or file manager is started on the host in local mode, and offered as a Remote-SSH link in remote) and each entry as `serve` prints it (the control channel too, kind `control`, when it listens on an address; a page needs its address to reach the ledger, folders and uploads), `Cache-Control: no-store`. It names the host for a page to fill in; it proves nothing, and connecting still runs the proof both ways and needs the page's key accepted.
- **Headers on every answer**: `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; font-src 'self' data:; media-src 'self' blob:; connect-src 'self' URL...; worker-src 'self' blob:; manifest-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`, the `connect-src` naming each entry's `url`, then each `connect` source; over HTTPS, to a request naming one of `hosts`, `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000` (never to `localhost` or an address, which other servers share); `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`, `Date`, `Connection`. A file carries `Content-Type` by its extension (`application/octet-stream` when unknown), a strong `ETag` (the first 128 bits of the contents' SHA-256, read once while the file's length and modification time stay as they were; the built-in site's are made at build), `Last-Modified` (a directory's files only), and `Cache-Control: public, max-age=IMMUTABLE_AGE, immutable` when its name holds a hash (a part of 8 or more hex digits after the first, split at `.`, `-`, `_`: `main.3f9a1b2c.js`) or a folder on its path is named for a revision (12 or more hex digits and nothing else), else `no-cache`: a folder such as `canvaskit/` carries no version, so it is asked about each time. `If-None-Match` naming its tag is answered 304 with no body and no `Content-Length`; without one, `If-Modified-Since` no earlier than the file's change is too. No range, no compression. The portal answers the same way for the signed-in site, with `private` in place of `public` (`private, no-cache`, `private, max-age=IMMUTABLE_AGE, immutable`).
- **Connections**: one thread each, `max_connections` at once and `max_connections_per_address` from one address (past either a new one is closed unanswered), each ended after `connection_time` whatever it is doing; kept alive in HTTP/1.1 unless `Connection: close`, for `max_requests` requests; the first head must arrive within `request_time` of the connection (TLS handshake included), each later one within `idle_time` to start and `request_time` from its first byte; a write that makes no progress for `send_time` ends the connection.

## One provider on standard streams (`canoa_host::provider`)

```rust
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct ProviderOptions { pub origin: Origin, pub provider: String, pub settings: Option<Settings>, pub working_roots: Vec<PathBuf>, pub max_sessions: usize }
pub fn ProviderOptions::new(origin: Origin, provider: impl Into<String>) -> ProviderOptions   // no settings, any cwd, the facade's default max_sessions
pub fn serve_stdio(options: &ProviderOptions) -> Result<(), ProviderError>
pub fn serve_connected(options: &ProviderOptions, address: SocketAddr, token: &str) -> Result<(), ProviderError>   // loopback only

#[non_exhaustive] pub struct HostLine { pub source_settings: Option<Settings>, pub settings: Option<Settings>, pub token: Option<String> }   // Default, Eq; Debug shows no value or token
pub fn HostLine::to_line(&self) -> Vec<u8>                                           // one line of JSON, no line break
pub fn HostLine::read(input: &mut impl BufRead, max: usize) -> Result<HostLine, ProviderError>   // one line of at most max bytes
```

`serve_stdio` loads the code, takes the provider, and runs `coqui_acp_provider::AcpFacade` on standard input and output until the client closes them. There, loaded code shares both with the transport: a Python `print()`, or a subprocess that inherits them, writes into the client's ACP stream or takes its messages. `serve_connected` connects to `address`, sends `token` and a line break, then loads the code and runs the facade on that socket, so the process's own standard streams are the provider's alone; it is what `serve` runs.

- **A library** is mapped with Guatiao's default import search. On Windows: the library's own directory, the executable's, the system's; not the working directory or `PATH`. Elsewhere the platform's: `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, the file's run path (its own directory only through `$ORIGIN`), the system's.
- **A source gets the kinds' descriptions.** Every source is added with its `kinds` setting holding, besides any the entry lists, a file of the descriptions of the kinds a script library may serve (`descriptions.rs`: vendor, store extension and program store, `usage_reader` with its reading and process table, the speech kinds, `acp_harness`, the account kinds, `agent_provider`, `transport` and the objects they name), made from the build's own kinds and kept content-named under the temporary directory. A source built from descriptions (JavaScript) refuses a library with `no description of the kind X was given` without it; Python builds its tables from its own typed modules and ignores it. The Canoa SDKs (`bindings/{python,js}/canoa-sdk`) write libraries against them.
- **A source** is added with `settings` as a Guatiao map, then the registry is refreshed once; a library the source refuses is logged and passed over. On Windows the import search is the entry's `import_search`: an import the source names itself that the default search leaves missing (`python3.dll`, for Python) is mapped from the first of `import_dirs` holding it, and with `ImportSearch::Path` else the Windows directory, else **the working directory (canoa.cfg's, under `serve`) or a directory on `PATH`**, in that order; the source is then opened with the default search, so its other imports come from where they did. An import of an import is not looked for. Elsewhere the search is the platform's (`LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, the file's run path, the system's).
- **The provider**: with no `settings`, its own instance; a provider built only from a configuration is `NeedsConfiguration`. With `settings`, an instance built from them: Guatiao checks them against the schema the provider declares, then the provider may refuse them; a provider that is its own one instance is `TakesNoSettings`.

**Loading runs code** with this process's rights, before any check, and trusts whoever can write any file it reads or the directories holding them. For a library, that is the file. For `guatiao_source_py` it is also: the Python whose shared library the loader finds (on Windows the first `python3.dll` in `import_dirs` or the search above, then the `python3X.dll` it names); `GUATIAO_LIBRARY` in this process's environment, which picks the native library Guatiao's Python binding loads; every directory in `paths`; every manifest in `dirs` and `manifests` and every directory its own `paths` names; every module those import; and what the source's `isolation` lets the interpreter read:

| `isolation` | The interpreter also reads |
| --- | --- |
| `shipped` (the source's default) | the standard library and `site-packages` of `home` or the Python found, with their `.pth` files; a `canoa._pth` or `pyvenv.cfg` beside the `canoa` executable (`CanoaUI._pth` beside the app's, when it hosts), and on Windows that executable's directory, which CPython puts on `sys.path`, so whoever can write that directory chooses where modules come from. Not the `PYTHON*` variables or the user's own site-packages. |
| `isolated` | the standard library of `home` or the Python found, and nothing beside the executable, in `site-packages` or in the environment |
| `inherit` | all of `shipped`'s, the user's own site-packages, and the `PYTHON*` variables this process inherits from the host (`PYTHONPATH`, `PYTHONHOME`). `serve` logs it at `info` as configuration. |

`HostLine::read` reads up to the line break and leaves the rest to the transport. The line is `{"canoa_host": 1, "source_settings": {...}, "settings": {...}, "token": "..."}`; anything else, a client's ACP message included, is `ProviderError::HostLine`.

## A tool entry (`canoa_host::tool`)

A command-line tool with no ACP, served as an ACP agent (version 1) by the rules of its entry. No tool has code here.

```rust
pub const NOTICES_META_KEY: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/notices";
pub const CANCEL_GRACE: Duration = 10 s;
pub const MAX_TEXT: usize = 1024 * 1024;      // text per update; longer is split, a tool_done output is cut
pub const MAX_LABEL: usize = 4096;            // a title, an id, a failure, after control and format characters go
pub const MAX_ASKS: usize = 16;               // permission requests one process may have waiting
pub const MAX_SPEC: usize = 16 * 1024;        // an entry as JSON, since it goes on a command line

#[non_exhaustive] pub struct ToolSpec { pub program: String, pub args: Vec<String>, pub runs: Runs, pub unasked: String, pub prompt: Option<Value>, pub cancel: Option<Value>, pub resume_args: Vec<String>, pub kinds: BTreeMap<String, String>, pub max_sessions: usize, pub events: Vec<Rule> }   // Clone, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize (deny_unknown_fields)
pub fn ToolSpec::check(&self) -> Result<(), String>          // what serde cannot check; names a setting, never a value. Also refuses an entry over MAX_SPEC or holding a NUL, and in `message` mode an argument that is `{prompt}` alone not right after `--`
pub fn ToolSpec::notices(&self, name: &str) -> Vec<String>   // what session/new lists under NOTICES_META_KEY
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Runs { Session, Message }
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Rule { pub when: Map<String, Value>, pub send: Action, pub text, id, title, kind, input, output, error: Option<String>, pub status: Option<ToolStatus>, pub stop: Option<Stop>, pub allow, reject, line: Option<Value> }
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Action { Text, Thought, Tool, ToolDone, Failure, End, Session, Ask, Reply, Ignore }   // TOML: snake_case
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum ToolStatus { Completed, Failed }
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Stop { EndTurn, Cancelled, MaxTokens, Refusal }
pub fn serve(spec: ToolSpec, name: &str, input: impl Read, output: impl Write + Send + 'static) -> Result<(), ToolError>
pub fn serve_stdio(spec: ToolSpec, name: &str) -> Result<(), ToolError>
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum ToolError { Spec(String), Transport(io::Error) }
```

- **Rules.** Each JSON line the tool writes is taken by the first rule whose `when` paths all hold their values (JSON equality; an empty `when` takes every line). A path is dotted steps of letters, digits, `_` and `-`; a number indexes a list. A line that is not JSON, or that no rule takes, is passed over, and so is every line of a `message` process that a later prompt replaced.
- **Fields per `send`**, each other field refused: `text`, `thought`, `failure`: `text`. `tool`: `id`, `title`; optional `kind`, `input` (a path, its value as `rawInput`, left out over `MAX_TEXT`). `tool_done`: `id`; optional `status` (`completed`), `output`. `end`: optional `stop` (`end_turn`) or `error`, not both. `session`: `id`. `ask`: `title`, `allow`, `reject`. `reply`: `line`. `ignore`: none.
- **Templates.** In text fields, `{path}` is the line's value there: a string as it is, null or missing as nothing, anything else as compact JSON; a `{` not opening a path is kept. In `prompt`, `cancel`, `allow`, `reject` and `line`, every string value is a template, a string that is one placeholder alone takes the value with its type (null when missing), and keys are kept as written. The prompt's context is `{"prompt": text, "session": the tool's session or ""}`; `args` and `resume_args` (`message` only) take the same context; `allow`, `reject` and `line` take the line that matched. Every line written, to the tool or the client, is one line: U+2028 and U+2029 are escaped as ` ` and ` `.
- **`kind`**: the rendered text when it is one of ACP's tool kinds (`read`, `edit`, `delete`, `move`, `search`, `execute`, `think`, `fetch`, `switch_mode`, `other`), else its value in `kinds`, else `other`. `check` refuses a `kinds` value that is not one.
- **ACP.** `initialize` answers version 1, no `loadSession`, no image, audio or embedded context. `session/new` needs an absolute `cwd` on this machine (on Windows a drive path; a share or device path is refused before anything looks at it) that is a directory, and `mcpServers` empty or absent (`-32602`), at most `max_sessions` per connection (`-32600`); it answers `{"sessionId", "_meta": {NOTICES_META_KEY: notices}}`. In `session` mode it starts the process there, `-32603` naming the entry when it cannot. `session/prompt` takes text and `resource_link` blocks (a link as its `uri`), joined by line breaks, anything else `-32602`; one turn at a time per session (`-32600`); it is answered `{"stopReason"}` when the turn ends, or `-32603` with the failure's text as `message`. `session/cancel` as below. Everything else is `-32601`; a notification other than `session/cancel` is dropped.
- **A turn ends** at an `end` rule; a `failure` before an `end_turn` makes it fail with that text. A `session` process that exits ends the turn with `-32603` and the session with it: every later prompt is `-32603`. A `message` process that exits ends its turn: `end_turn` (or its failure) on a success status, `-32603` with the status otherwise. A process that closes its output and has not exited within 2 s counts as failed.
- **Cancel.** A cancelled turn is answered `cancelled` however it ends. `session` with `cancel`: the line is written, and a turn still running `CANCEL_GRACE` later has its process ended and the session with it. Otherwise the process is ended at once; in `session` mode the session with it.
- **`ask`** sends `session/request_permission` (v1: `toolCall` `{"toolCallId": "canoa-ask-N", "title", "status": "pending", "rawInput": {"text": the whole question, cut to MAX_TEXT}}`, options `allow`/`allow_once` and `reject`/`reject_once`) and writes `allow` only for an answer selecting `allow` while the turn that asked runs uncancelled; any other answer and an error write `reject`. A request that comes with no turn running, cancelled, or `MAX_ASKS` waiting is refused at once, without asking. When a turn is cancelled or ends, every question of its session still waiting gets `reject` then and is forgotten, so a later answer reaches nothing; so is one whose process was replaced or ended.
- **Processes** run `program` (with `args`; in `message` mode `resume_args` first once a `session` rule has recorded one) in the session's `cwd`, with this process's environment. A line to one goes through a queue of 64; a full queue ends it. Its standard error goes to this process's, `OUTPUT_LIMIT` bytes per process. A line of output over `serve::MAX_LINE` ends it. On Windows each process is in a job object of its own, ended with it, and runs with `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath` set, so a `.cmd` tool does not take a program it names bare from the client's `cwd`; on Unix it stays in this process's group, and ending it ends only it. When `input` ends, each process's input is closed, and it is ended 1 s later; under `serve` that does not happen, since the host ends the whole connection's process tree at once.

## Profiles (`canoa_host::profile`)

The schema of a profile, what an agent is launched with, and its merge in layers. The app reads the same schema; `tests/vectors/profile.json` holds both to the same views and refusals. `serve` applies a `[host.profiles]` profile to an agent entry that offers it (see Serving).

```rust
pub const PROFILE_META: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/profile";
/// `initialize`'s `_meta` key naming, as a list of names, the overlays this connection starts with: only a narrowing of what the profile lists.
pub const OVERLAYS_META: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/overlays";
/// `initialize`'s `_meta` key naming, as a loadout's name, the `[loadouts]` entry this connection starts with.
pub const LOADOUT_META: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/loadout";
/// `initialize`'s `_meta` key naming, as text, the kind of client (`browser`, `outlook`, `word`, `excel`, `powerpoint`): a connection that names no loadout, and whose preset names none, starts with `[defaults.clients]`'s loadout for that kind, else `[defaults] loadout`. A kind the file gives nothing is only a label; anything but text is refused. The profile report carries the `loadout` the connection got.
pub const CLIENT_META: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/client";
pub fn launch(program: &OsStr, args: &[OsString], max: usize) -> io::Error
```

`launch` reads one line of at most `max` bytes, `{"canoa_host_launch": 1, "env": [[NAME, VALUE], ...], "args": [ARG, ...]}` (`args` optional: arguments for the program after those of `launch`'s own command line, which carry the access for Antigravity), runs `program` with those variables added, passes the rest of standard input to it, and exits this process with its status (1 for a signal); it returns only an error: `InvalidData` for a line of another form, or why the program did not start. It is `canoa launch`.

`DEFAULT_PRESET` (`"default"`) is `[profiles.default]`: always there (`HostProfile::default_preset` is an empty `Preset` when the file has none, `resolve_preset("default")` never fails, and a `config/set` of `profiles` without `default` is refused), and the preset a session that names none starts with. `Preset::items` holds what a profile keeps for each `profile_item` (`[profiles.NAME.items.ID]`, values by field name); `Preset::item_values` is everything the profile holds by item id, the session's own settings (`model`, `effort`, `mode`, `context`, `access`) included, and `Preset::session_layer` the layer the built-in items make of those. `ItemSet` is the items a host offers (the built-in and bundled providers and those its entries' and extensions' libraries add, opened when `serve` binds); a started session applies each item its profile holds through it and reports `items.ID` with what it became. The proxy a `system` or `pac` network runs for a session (`network::session`) listens on loopback only, answers only a request that carries the session's secret as the proxy password, and ends with the connection.

```toml
model = "m"                      # effort, mode likewise: text, not empty, no control character
context = { text = "..." }       # or { file = "CONTEXT.md" }: exactly one
unset = ["model"]                # model, effort, mode or context of a lower layer; not beside that key
[env.NAME]                       # NAME: a letter or _, then letters, digits or _, up to 128
value = "v"                      # exactly one of value, from_env (a variable's name), from_file (a path), remove = true
[skills.NAME]                    # NAME, and mcp's and headers': 1 to 64 letters, digits, - or _
path = "skills/review"           # or remove = true
[mcp.NAME]
type = "stdio"                   # stdio: command, args, [mcp.NAME.env.X]; http or sse: url (http:// or https://), [mcp.NAME.headers.X]
command = "npx"                  # or remove = true alone
```

```rust
pub const MAX_NAME: usize = 64;
pub const MAX_ENV_NAME: usize = 128;
pub const MAX_SECRET_FILE: u64 = 1024 * 1024;
pub fn full_commit_id(reference: &str) -> bool   // 40 or 64 lower-case hex digits

#[non_exhaustive] pub enum LayerKind { BuiltIn, Overlay, User, Project, Agent, Session }   // Copy, Ord: lowest first
pub const fn LayerKind::as_str(self) -> &'static str      // "built-in", "overlay", "user", "project", "agent", "session"
pub const fn LayerKind::is_confined(self) -> bool         // Overlay and Project
pub struct LayerId { pub kind: LayerKind, pub name: String }   // Display: "user", "overlay:team", "agent:Claude"
pub fn LayerId::new(kind: LayerKind, name: impl Into<String>) -> LayerId

#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Delegate { pub default: Option<String>, pub sources: Option<Vec<String>>, pub max_running: Option<u32> }   // Default; a layer's `[delegate]` (an overlay's `[harness.ID.delegate]` for the harness it is read for): suggestions the host applies only where its own settings allow. `max_running` is 0..=i32::MAX, sources unique and plain
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct EffectiveDelegate { pub default: Option<Sourced<String>>, pub sources: Option<Sourced<Vec<String>>>, pub max_running: Option<Sourced<u32>> }   // Default; a later layer wins per key; the view's `delegate` holds each with its layer
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Profile { pub model, effort, mode: Option<String>, pub context: Option<Context>, pub delegate: Delegate, pub unset: Vec<Scalar>, pub env: IndexMap<String, Change<EnvValue>>, pub skills: IndexMap<String, Change<Skill>>, pub mcp: IndexMap<String, Change<McpServer>>, pub contexts: Vec<Context> }   // Default; contexts only from an AOS overlay
pub fn Profile::from_str(text: &str, kind: LayerKind) -> Result<Profile, ProfileError>    // touches no file, reads no variable
pub fn Profile::from_table(table: toml::Table, kind: LayerKind) -> Result<Profile, ProfileError>
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum EnvValue { Value(String), FromEnv(String), FromFile(String), Text(String), Path(String), Reference { reference: String, optional: bool }, Paths(Vec<(String, bool)>) }   // Debug never shows a Value's or Text's text; the last four only from an AOS overlay
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Context { Text(String), File(String), Template(String) }   // Template: an overlay's context file, `${NAME}` replaced when the context is assembled; rules stay `File`
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Skill { pub path: String }   pub fn Skill::new(path) -> Skill
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum McpServer { Stdio { command, args, env }, Http { url, headers }, Sse { url, headers } }
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Change<T> { Set(T), Remove }
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Scalar { Model, Effort, Mode, Context }

#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Layer { pub id: LayerId, pub dir: Option<PathBuf>, pub profile: Profile, pub aos: Option<Box<AosLayer>> }
pub fn Layer::new(id: LayerId, dir: Option<PathBuf>, profile: Profile) -> Layer
pub fn merge(layers: &[Layer]) -> Effective
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Effective { pub model, effort, mode: Option<Sourced<String>>, pub context: Option<Sourced<Context>>, pub delegate: EffectiveDelegate, pub env, skills, mcp: IndexMap<String, Sourced<_>>, pub removed: Vec<Removed>, pub contexts: Vec<Sourced<Context>>, pub overlays: Vec<OverlayReport>, pub aos: Vec<AosLayer> }
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Refused { pub key: String, pub layer: LayerId, pub reads: String }
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Sourced<T> { pub value: T, pub layer: LayerId, pub dir: Option<PathBuf> }
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Removed { pub key: String, pub layer: LayerId, pub was: LayerId }
pub fn Effective::view(&self) -> serde_json::Value          // references as written, never resolved
pub fn Effective::environment(&self, lookup: impl Fn(&str) -> Option<String>) -> Result<Vec<(String, Secret)>, ProfileError>
pub fn resolve(key: &str, value: &EnvValue, dir: Option<&Path>, confined: bool, lookup: impl Fn(&str) -> Option<String>) -> Result<Secret, ProfileError>
pub struct Secret(..)   pub fn Secret::expose(&self) -> &str   // Debug "Secret(..)", Display "(hidden)"

#[non_exhaustive] pub enum OverlaySource { Git { repo: String, reference: String, path: String }, Dir(PathBuf) }
pub fn OverlaySource::parse_git(source: &str) -> Result<OverlaySource, ProfileError>   // [git+]<repository>@<ref>[#<path>], an AOS source string

#[non_exhaustive] pub struct OverlayInput<'a> { pub identity, root, os: &'a str, pub harness: Option<&'a str>, pub pinned: bool, pub tree: &'a [aos::TreeEntry] }
pub fn OverlayInput::new(identity, root, os, tree) -> OverlayInput   // no harness, not pinned
pub fn overlay_layer(input: &OverlayInput<'_>) -> Result<Layer, OverlayRefusal>   // reads nothing but input.tree
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct AosLayer { pub identity, root, os, digest: String, pub pinned: bool, pub manifest: Map<String, Value>, pub files: BTreeSet<String>, pub texts: BTreeMap<String, Vec<u8>>, pub references: Vec<(String, String, bool)>, pub not_applied: Vec<String>, pub elsewhere: Vec<coqui_aos::SkillEntry>, pub allowed: Option<String>, pub packages: BTreeMap<String, String>, pub state: Option<String> }
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct OverlayReport { pub layer: LayerId, pub digest: String, pub not_applied: Vec<String>, pub non_defaults: Vec<coqui_aos::NonDefaultSetting> }   pub fn OverlayReport::view(&self) -> Value
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct OverlayRefusal { pub code: &'static str, pub subject: String }   // code: name, digest, manifest, invalid, env-file, reserved

#[non_exhaustive] pub enum ProfileError { Parse { line, column }, Invalid { key: String, rule: Rule }, Resolve { key: String, what: &'static str } }
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Rule { Name, Path, Form, Value, Reserved }
pub const fn ProfileError::code(&self) -> &'static str     // "parse", "name", "path", "form", "value", "reserved", "resolve"
```

- **Merge**: lowest first. A scalar a later layer sets replaces the lower one; `unset` takes it away first. A named entry a later layer sets replaces the lower one whole and keeps its place; `Change::Remove` takes it out, and set again later it comes last. `removed` lists only what was there to remove, in order.
- **Layers from elsewhere** (`is_confined`: overlay, project) read any variable of this computer, as the person's own do. A confined layer that sets a variable the AOS specification reserves for the host (`coqui_aos::is_reserved_variable`: `AOS_*`, `*_OVERLAY_ROOT`, `*_OVERLAY_STATE`), in `env` or a stdio server's `env`, is refused at parse time (`Rule::Reserved`, key `env.NAME` or `mcp.NAME.env.NAME`); no other variable is refused, and removing one is allowed. There is no `share_env`: a profile that names it is a parse error.
- **The view** has `model`, `effort`, `mode`, `context` when set, and always `env`, `skills`, `mcp`, and `removed`.
- **Paths** are judged on the text, the same on every platform. In a confined layer (overlay, project) a path is relative and stays below its directory: no leading `/`, `\` or `~`, no `:`, no `..` segment. Elsewhere any non-empty path without NUL.
- **Errors** never quote a value: `Invalid.key` names the setting (`env.A`, `mcp.a.headers.T`), or for a refused name the table holding it (`env`, `mcp.a.env`). The first refusal in a document is, in order: anything serde refuses (an unknown key, a wrong type, a key twice) as `Parse`; then `model`, `effort`, `mode`, `context`, `unset`, then `env`, `skills` and `mcp` entries in the document's order.
- **`resolve`** reads `FromEnv` through `lookup` and `FromFile` from `dir` (an absolute path only outside a confined layer), less one final `\n` or `\r\n`. The path is canonicalized and that path is what is read; for a confined layer it must be under `dir` canonicalized, so a link out of the project is refused. It must be a regular file of at most `MAX_SECRET_FILE` bytes, UTF-8. Call it only when a session starts.
- **`parse_git`** reads an AOS source string (`coqui_aos::Source::parse`): the path follows the first `#`, without leading or trailing `/`, and must be an AOS relative path; the ref follows the last `@` when it holds no `/`, `:` or `\`. Refused besides what AOS refuses: a source that is not Git, or names no ref; a repository holding a space or control character, or a scheme other than `https`, `http`, `ssh`, `git`, `file` in lowercase; `file://` with a host, or a path opening with `\\` or `//` (another host's share); `http://` or `git://` at a ref that is not a `full_commit_id`, since nothing else checks what a plaintext transport carries; a ref `git check-ref-format` refuses, or one opening with `-`. `Dir` is never parsed here.
- **`overlay_layer`** makes the layer of an AOS overlay from its tree, whose digest it computes: the root variable `<NAME>_OVERLAY_ROOT` (a `Value`), then each env file's and `env`'s variables as written, then path additions per variable (the overlay's own `bin/` on `PATH`, `lib/python/` on `PYTHONPATH` and `lib/node/` on `NODE_PATH` come first, as the specification's defaults say; each is read over the host's own value of the variable, which `build_each` names so a default addition never starts from empty), merged as any layer's; bundled skills `skills/<name>` (a skill the overlay names by source or by a name for a catalog is not bundled: it is in `AosLayer::elsewhere`, never in `profile.skills` from this function); MCP servers (a command with a separator made absolute under the root; `env_passthrough` and `headers_env` as optional `env:` references; no header over plain `http` to another host); `contexts` from rules, routing lines, `context` and the harness's `context`; `model` and `effort` from `[harness.<input.harness>]`. Mapping entries are taken in key byte order. Refused whole: a name AOS refuses, a tree it cannot digest, an unreadable or invalid `overlay.cfg` (problems under `/about` alone never refuse), a missing or unreadable env file, a path addition that leaves the overlay's folder (`path`, or `invalid` where the manifest check says so first), a variable the specification reserves for the host (`reserved`; every other variable, sensitive or not, applies, and `report` lists the sensitive ones as `env-sensitive`). The agent is found on the host's own `PATH` before the overlay's is added: `canoa launch` and, on Unix, the host's process starter look a bare program name up there when the profile sets `PATH` (`profile::found_on_own_path`); on Windows an in-process Claude, Codex or Pi is started by `coqui-acp-harness`'s own std `Command`, which looks the bare name up on the child's `PATH`. What it needs of the computer (`runtime`, and the interpreters its scripts name) is checked by `profile::needs` when a session starts, against the session's `PATH` and then the Store's runtime folders, once per overlay: each need is a report item `runtime.python`, `runtime.node`, `runtime.interpreter: sh` or `runtime.commands: git` (`runtime.os` when `runtime.os` leaves this OS out), `applied` when found and `off` with why when not, with `offer` naming the Store runtime provider (`python_store`, `node_store`, `git_bash_store`, `powershell_store`) that installs it; a version is read by running the program's `--version` (5 seconds, once while its file is as it is). The first program of a name on the `PATH` is the one that counts. What preparing made (`profile::prepare`) and the commands `scripts` expose (`profile::commands`) are items of the same report: `dependencies: FILE` and `setup: PATH` are `applied` when the record under `overlays/.prepared/NAME.json` names the overlay's digest (`off`, saying to install or sync the overlay, when it does not; the packages then go on `PYTHONPATH` and `NODE_PATH` after `lib/`, `<NAME>_OVERLAY_STATE` is the state directory, and `AosLayer::packages` and `state` hold them), and `scripts.NAME` is `applied` when its launcher was made in `overlays/.environments/NAME/commands/` (first on `PATH`; a name two overlays share without regard to ASCII case is `refused` for both). A stdio MCP server's bare `command` is also looked for in its overlay's own `PATH` additions (`overlay::path_additions`: `bin/` by default, those that go first before the host's `PATH`, those that go last after it) and given to the agent as an absolute path; a name no folder holds is `refused`, as before. The agent itself is found on this process's `PATH` and the Store's runtime folders when its environment sets `PATH`: `Serving::bind` gives `coqui_acp_harness::set_program_dirs` the runtime folders, and the harness's own start (every system, an in-process Claude, Codex or Pi) looks the name up there. Not applied, and listed in `not_applied`: `mcp.skills`, an MCP `timeout` and working directory, other harnesses' hints and the harness's own keys.
- **Templating** (`profile::hosted::template`, the specification's "Templating" through `coqui_aos::{template_values, context_text, skill_text}`): an overlay's `Context::Template` files and the body of its bundled `SKILL.md` have `${NAME}` replaced when `parts_for` hands them out, never in the installed bytes (so the digest, a pin and a move are unchanged). `AOS_OVERLAY_ROOT` is the private copy the overlay's hooks run from, made when a text names it; `AOS_OVERLAY_NAME` is the normalized identity; `AOS_PROJECT_ROOT` is the `cwd` of each `session/new|load|resume` (a context naming it is replaced again per session by `Parts::system_prompt_for`; a skill's body is written once per connection, so it keeps the name as written); `AOS_OVERLAY_STATE` and `AOS_OVERLAY_SCOPE` have no value (no state directory, no scope label). Option values come from `[overlays.NAME] options` (`HostProfile::options`), else the manifest's default; a sensitive option is never replaced. A value of the wrong type refuses each text of that overlay that is templated, reported `refused` under `context` or `skills.NAME` with the option's name and no value. `rules`, `routing` and a skill fetched from elsewhere are not templated. The bound on the context counts the replaced text.
- **An AOS layer in a merge** adds an `OverlayReport` to `overlays`, and its `AosLayer` to `aos`. `unset = ["context"]` also takes back every `contexts` entry below. `Effective::environment` builds the overlays' environment first, lowest first, over this process's environment through `lookup`: every root variable, then each overlay's env files, `env` and path additions with substitution. A placeholder reads what overlays set and any variable of this process its text names. A variable an overlay won takes the built value (an optional reference not resolved leaves it out); an error is `Resolve` with key `overlay:<identity>.env.<subject>`. The view adds `contexts` and `overlays` when there are any.

## Overlay hooks (`canoa_host::profile`)

A hook is a program an overlay runs at a named moment of a session (`hook.<ext>` and `[hooks]` in the overlay's manifest, the specification's `hooks.md`). Canoa fires each event it sees over ACP, for the overlays of a profile (a `[host.profiles]` profile, or canoa.cfg's top level for a source of the host's own). It fires nothing for an overlay it does not read. A hook may allow, as a harness's own may: a combined `allow` on a `pre-tool` answers the permission question the person would have been asked (below); nothing else a hook prints approves a prompt or a session.

- **Off until allowed, by digest.** An overlay's hooks run only when canoa.cfg names the overlay's tree digest (`allow_hooks = { team = "sha256:..." }` in `[host.profiles.NAME]`, `[agents.NAME.hooks]` team = "sha256:..." for a source of the host's own), or when the person, asked by the host, allows them for one session (`_canoa/profile/approve`: the question carries `hooks`, one entry per overlay with `overlay`, `digest`, `changed`, `failClosed` and its `events`, each `{event, run, timeout, onFailure, match?, sets?, fires}`; the answer's `allowHooks` lists overlay names, and a name the question did not list is ignored). Both allowances name the files' digest, so a changed overlay is off again (`changed` says it was allowed at another version) and never allowed by itself. An overlay with a hook that has `on_failure = "refuse"` is not activated while its hooks are off (the report says `left out`): a guard that is off would fail open. Only a client that is this process's own link and says it answers (`APPROVE_META`) is asked; every other client gets canoa.cfg's allowances only.
- **What runs.** Each hook runs from a private copy of the overlay's files made from the bytes the digest covered (removed with the connection), as the agent's own hooks do: in the session's working folder (the `cwd` of its payload; a folder that is gone makes the run `not-run`) and with the session's environment, as a child process of the host, with no process group or job object. The environment is the host's own, the variables of the person's own layers, and the environment the specification gives its event (`hook_environment`): every overlay's root variable (each the path of that overlay's copy), every overlay's declarations stacked in composition order (the top layer's value wins, and a secret granted to one overlay reaches every overlay's hooks), the `pre-env` output of the connection, what the earlier hooks of the event set (`env`, whether or not Canoa can apply it), and the hook's own overlay's `AOS_OVERLAY_ROOT`, `AOS_OVERLAY_NAME`, `AOS_OVERLAY_STATE` and `AOS_HOOK_EVENT`; a `pre-env` hook gets the host's environment, the root variables and what the `pre-env` hooks before it in composition order set, run in the directory canoa.cfg is in. At its time limit the hook's own process is stopped and nothing it started is. A hook also gets what the overlays' env scripts set (applied after every overlay's declarations, in run order). Canoa runs no overlay's setup, and the agent starts the MCP servers in its own environment, so those are the session's too. The hooks of an event run in the specification's order (`hook_order`: composition order; Canoa has no hook of the user's or the project's). The event is the first argument; the payload, exactly the keys the specification gives the event, is one JSON object on standard input. Interpreters are the first of `python3`/`python` (`python`/`python3` on Windows), `node`, `pwsh`/`powershell`, `bash`, `sh` found on the host's `PATH`; a hook whose interpreter is missing fails as `not-run`.
- **Bounds** are `coqui_aos::Settings`' defaults: 60 seconds a run (or the entry's `timeout`, if shorter), 120 seconds an event, 64 KiB of output (read no further), 1 MiB of payload (the hook is not run), 1024 characters of `reason`. Standard error is read and dropped. A run that fails (`exit`, `timeout`, `too-large`, `output`, `not-run`, `payload-too-large`, `event-timeout`) applies nothing and is logged by cause, never quoting its output; with `on_failure = "refuse"` it also refuses the event (a prompt is blocked, a tool call denied, a session or the agent's start refused).
- **The session's base environment** (`profile::Session`, built in `Connection::prepare_with` from `Starting { who, host }`): the identity variables dotagents stamps (`AGENTS_HARNESS` the harness id, `AGENTS_VENDOR`, `AGENT`, `AGENTS_MODEL`) and Canoa's own (`AGENTS_HOST=canoa`, `AGENTS_HARNESS_VERSION`, `AGENTS_EFFORT`, `AGENTS_ACCOUNT`, `AGENTS_ACCOUNT_KIND`, `AGENTS_ACCOUNT_PROVIDER`, `AGENTS_PROFILE`, `AGENTS_LOADOUT`, `AGENTS_ROLE`, `AGENTS_PROJECT_ROOT`, and the session's access as `AGENTS_SANDBOX`, `AGENTS_NETWORK` and `AGENTS_APPROVALS`), each only when it has a value and never empty; a value the host's own environment already holds is kept. `merge_with` makes every name readable by every layer (a `built-in:session` entry in `share_env`), so a layer's `${...}`, `from_env` and `if_unset` see them; the agent's environment starts with the identity variables and a layer's own value for one replaces it. `Starting::host` holds what the host sets for the session itself (read, never handed on again). The session's folder and id (`AGENTS_PROJECT_ROOT`, `AGENTS_SESSION_ID`) are added to the environment a hook runs in, from its working directory and the session in its payload. The harness id comes from the provider an entry runs (`claude`, `codex`, `pi`) or the entry's name (`harness_identity`; Grok is `grok`/`xai`, not yet in dotagents' registry). The account's kind and provider are read from its kind id and provided standards (`account_words`).
- **The access of a program the host starts** (Grok, Antigravity, OpenCode, whose `OPENCODE_PERMISSION` is one JSON variable; `Connection::launched_as`, chosen by the harness id the entry gives, so an entry that is only reported as one by its `initialize` answer is started as its entry says): `Connection::prepare_with` asks `Access::translate` (coqui-acp-harness) for the target's variables, arguments and JSON. The variables join the agent's environment unless a layer already sets the name (reported under `access.env.NAME`); the arguments go on `canoa launch`'s first line as `args`; the JSON (Grok's `yoloMode`) is merged into each session request's `_meta`. The Profile line's `access.*` items read the same translation, with `Part::said`.
- **The account's variables** (`Starting::account`, from `kind::Harness::account_environment`): before the account is held, `Accounts::preview` makes the grant it would be given (settings, provided standards and the state folder's path, no credential, nothing created) and the harness's own provider (`coqui_acp_claude::accounts::environment`, `coqui_acp_codex::accounts::environment`) lists the variables that grant sets that hold no credential; the names the specification reserves are dropped. They are read by every layer, ahead of the host's own variables, and are not handed on again, since the provider applies them after the profile's environment: a layer that sets the same name is reported `not supported` under `env.NAME`. Pi lists none yet.
- **Output** goes where the specification says and no further: `context` is added as a pair of content blocks (a label Canoa wrote, then the hook's text alone, so nothing it prints can pass for the label), `decision = "block"` stops a prompt, `"deny"` a tool call, `"ask"` leaves it to the person, `"allow"` answers for them (below). A decision an event does not take is an `output` failure. `context` for a new session goes into `systemPrompt` when the agent says it takes one; otherwise, and for a resumed session and for `post-tool`, it goes after the user's own blocks in that session's next prompt, once. `env` is applied only for `pre-env` (the agent's process environment; a name the specification reserves is dropped by `coqui_aos::fire_event`, a sensitive one is applied and listed as `env-sensitive`); for every other event it is `hook-not-applied`, since ACP has no way to set a variable for the agent's later commands.
- **Session identity.** `session` in a payload is Canoa's own id for the session (random, per connection), never the agent's.
- **The report** (`_meta[PROFILE_META]`, and the profile dialog) lists `hooks.NAME` (off, with `hook-not-allowed`) or `hooks.NAME.EVENT` per hook: `applied`, `applied` with `hook-partial`, or `not supported` with `hook-unsupported`; a failure appears as `hooks.NAME.EVENT` refused with `hook-failed: CAUSE` on the answer to the next session request. Context a hook prints that would take the session past the bound (UTF-8 bytes, together with the hook context the session already holds) is left out whole and reported `hooks.NAME.EVENT` `hook-not-applied`; a later part that fits is still added. `env_scripts.NAME` (off, `env-script-not-allowed`) says an overlay with env scripts has hooks canoa.cfg does not allow at its digest: the overlay still applies, and its env scripts do not run (Canoa runs none either way).

- **Env scripts** run under the same allowance as hooks (`allow_hooks`; an overlay at another digest, or with none, reports `env-script-not-allowed` and runs none, and the person's one-session allow never reaches them, since they run before anyone can be asked). `Hooks::env_scripts` runs them once when a connection starts, through `coqui_aos::composed_environment_running`: after each overlay's `env_files`, `env` and `paths`, in composition order, each script gets the environment the stack has at its turn (the host's own and the session's variables, every root, the `pre-env` output, the earlier overlays' declarations and scripts, its own overlay's declarations, and its `AOS_OVERLAY_*`) and prints JSON objects that go on top, unsubstituted. The script is its overlay's file from the private copy of the bytes the digest covered, run by its `interpreter` (found on the host's `PATH` as the hooks' are, never through a shell) in that copy, with its `args`, its `timeout` (60 s; `0` runs to the end) and `Settings::hook_output_bytes` as the bound on its output. A script that does not start, times out, exits non-zero, prints too much or prints anything but the specification's objects applies nothing: the session starts, and `env_scripts.NAME.PATH` is `refused` saying which and ending with the last 300 characters of its standard error (never its output). Each that ran is `applied` and names the variables it set; a sensitive one is also listed as `env-sensitive`. What each gave is kept (`overlay::ScriptRuns`), so every environment built later (the MCP servers' text, hooks' `hook_environment`) reads the same output, and no script runs twice. A default script no interpreter here can run is `script-not-run`. Not done: a script sees the host's process environment and the session's variables, not the person's own layers' variables; a hook sees script output applied after every overlay's declarations, not between them; the `python` interpreter is the first `python3`/`python` on the `PATH`, not the overlay's prepared runtime.

What fires, per event and harness. Every harness is reached through ACP, so the table is the same for all of them except where it says; `cd`, `pre-compact` and the `compact` and `clear` reasons of `session-start` are what ACP does not show, and the table after this one says, harness by harness, where they fire and where they cannot:

| Event | Canoa fires it | Output applied | Cannot |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `pre-env` | Before the agent starts, once per connection, for hooks canoa.cfg allows | `env` (the agent's environment), a failed guard starts no agent | the person's allowance (nobody can be asked yet) |
| `session-start` | `session/new` as `new`; `session/load` and `session/resume` as `resume`; Claude Code's own start after a compaction as `compact` (see Native hooks) | `context`; a failed guard answers the request `-32000` (not for `compact`, which has happened; its `context` goes in the next prompt) | `clear`, `fork` (never sent: a hook matching only those is `hook-unsupported`); `compact` for every harness but Claude Code; `env` (`hook-not-applied`) |
| `session-end` | `session/close`, and for each session still open when the connection ends | none | a killed host, an agent that exits without closing |
| `prompt` | Each `session/prompt`, before the agent has it (declared only) | `context`, `block` (the client gets `stopReason: "refusal"` and a message naming the overlay and the reason) | `env` |
| `turn-end` | The agent's answer to a prompt, after it was passed on | none | continuing the turn |
| `pre-tool` | Each `session/request_permission` (declared only) | `deny` (the host rejects it itself with the request's `reject_once`, else `reject_always`, else cancels, and the client is never asked), `allow` (the host answers with the request's `allow_once`, see below), `ask` and no decision (the client is asked as it would be) | A call the agent's own rules allow, which asks nothing (`hook-partial`); `context` and `env`, and an `allow` that carries an `input` (`hook-not-applied`: ACP's answer names an option and cannot carry a rewritten call, so the allow is not applied either) |
| `post-tool` | The `tool_call_update` that ends a call, `completed` or `failed` (declared only) | `context` (next prompt) | none; the call has run |
| `cd`, `pre-compact` | Claude Code only, through its own hooks (see Native hooks) | `pre-compact`: `block`; `cd`: none | every other harness (`hook-unsupported`, the reason in the report); `pre-compact`'s `context`, `cd`'s `env` (`hook-not-applied`) |

**An allow.** Hooks combine to the tightest: a deny beats an ask, an ask beats an allow, and a deny ends the event (an ask or an allow leaves the next hook to decide). A combined `allow` answers `session/request_permission` itself with the request's `allow_once` option, never `allow_always` (the agent asks about the next call again), and the client is never asked; it is dropped as a deny is. It does not lift the person's refusals. The agent asks only for a call its own deny rules and mode have left to the person, so those are not overridden; the host adds the person's list of protected paths (`[permissions] protected`, carried in `HostProfile::protected`; unset, the app's recommended list, which is `src/protected_default.txt`: the app's `recommendedProtected` must equal it, and `protected_paths_test.dart` fails when they differ): a call whose `locations` or `rawInput` path keys (`file_path`, `path`, `notebook_path`, `filePath`, `paths`) name a protected place, resolved against the session's folder with `..` worked out and links followed, or a path that cannot be placed (empty, `~`, climbing above its root), goes to the person as it would have, with `hook-not-applied: allow` in the report; so does a request with no `allow_once` option. The call is read at `toolCall` (ACP v1) or `subject.toolCall` (v2); a question with neither (a v2 command subject, an unknown shape), a request to widen the sandbox (a `fileSystem` in its input, Codex's "Grant more permissions"), a path with a `..` component, on Windows a part ending in a dot or space or holding a `:`, and an `allow_once` option whose id another option shares are never answered by an allow. On a call of kind `other` (an MCP tool's), a hook whose `match` does not name `other` still runs and may deny or ask, but its allow does not count (`hook-not-applied`). **The text of a shell command is not read for paths, so allowing an `execute` call can mean writes to protected paths**: a hook that allows commands answers for everything they do. The native forwarder carries no `PreToolUse` (only `start`, `pre-compact` and `batch`), so no allow becomes a plugin hook's approval.

By harness (`harness` in a payload is the AOS id, from the agent's `initialize` or an entry named `Claude`, `Codex`, `Gemini` or `Pi`; any other entry is `canoa` for `pre-env`): `session-start` context reaches the system prompt of an agent that says it takes one (the Claude and Codex adapters when they announce it) and the first prompt of one that does not (Pi, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, and any agent that says nothing). A tool's `kind` is the agent's own ACP `kind`, `other` when it sends none, and an `other` call runs every `pre-tool` and `post-tool` hook whatever its `match`; `tool.name` is Claude's `_meta.claudeCode.toolName`, else the call's `title`. What only a harness's own hooks could see and Canoa does not fire (a call its own rules allow, an `env` for later commands) is not fired; compaction, a `cd` and a compacted session's start are fired for Claude Code only (below).

### Native hooks (`profile::hosted::hooks::{forward, native}`, `canoa hook-forward`)

For Claude Code the host asks the harness's own hooks to report what ACP does not show. When an overlay with hooks the person or canoa.cfg allowed listens to `cd`, `pre-compact`, or `session-start` for `compact` or `clear` (a hook with no `match` listens to every reason), each `session/new`, `load` and `resume` of a Claude Code session (`claude-code` by `initialize`'s agent name or an entry named `Claude`) carries a second plugin in `_meta.claudeCode.options.plugins`: `canoa-hooks`, in a directory made for that request under the connection's own private directory (see below), removed with the session, or when the request ends in an error, or with the connection. Its `hooks/hooks.json` has one command hook (`"shell": "bash"`, which Claude Code runs the command through whatever the person's default) per report the overlays need: `SessionStart` (every source: `startup` and `resume` only say where the session is, `compact` and `clear` run the overlays' `session-start`), `PreCompact` (`pre-compact`, with `trigger` `manual` or `auto`), `PostToolBatch` (`cd`: the host compares the working directory Claude Code reports after each batch of tool calls with the one it has, spelled as the host holds it ignoring separators and, on Windows, case; Claude Code's own `CwdChanged` is not given to a plugin's hooks, and `options.settings` is ignored by the adapter). The command is `PROGRAM [program_args] hook-forward --channel DIR/channel.json KIND`, the host's own program quoted for the harness's shell; a guard's `pre-compact` command carries `--refuse` (not a secret), so a forwarder that cannot read its channel still stops the compaction. No hook is written when the program's or the directory's path holds a character that cannot be quoted safely (`"`, `$`, backtick, `%`, `!`, a control character; backslashes are written as `/`; `'`, `&` and `;` are fine inside double quotes). When the plugin cannot be written (a path that cannot be quoted, a directory that is not the host's own, no room for another secret, a failed write) a session on a harness that needs it for an overlay hook with `on_failure = "refuse"` (`cd`, `pre-compact`, or `session-start` for `compact`/`clear`) is refused (`-32000`) as a `session-start` guard's is; otherwise the session opens and the report says `hook-failed` for the events that needed it. The adapter must be started with `--allow-client-plugins`, as it must for a profile's skills; without it `session/new` answers `-32602`.

`canoa hook-forward [--refuse] KIND --channel FILE` (hidden; it is one of the host's own commands, so an application runs it as `--canoa-child hook-forward`) reads the harness's payload on standard input, keeps only `source`, `trigger` and `cwd` (each a string of at most 2 KiB; nothing else of it, a tool's input or output and a prompt included, leaves the process), reports them on the channel and answers the harness with its exit status alone: 0, or 2 when the host says the compaction is blocked.
