**Usage records.** The host keeps what its agents spend in its home, `usage/` (files only this user reads): one line a turn and
model appended to `YYYY-MM.jsonl` with `time` (Unix seconds), `account`, `harness`, `model`?, `session_id`, `parent_session_id`?,
`input`?, `output`?, `cache_read`?, `cache_write`?, `cost_micro_usd`?. An agent reports running totals for a session; the host
keeps the last total it saw for each account, session and model (`seen.json`) and writes the difference when the turn ends (a
report with no turn open, at once), so a reconnect, a session loaded again or a restart counts nothing twice. A total that falls
is a counter that started over. A counter the agent never moved, a cost it never gave and an agent that reports no spend leave no
field and no line. The model is the report's own per-model split, else the model the session opened or was set to. Only what
reaches the host from an agent that reports usage is seen, whether or not the client asked notices (the host asks the agent for them whenever it keeps the log and hides them from a client that did not ask). `parent_session_id` is
set on what a delegated task's own session spends: the id of the session that started it, so a parent and its children are each their own lines and a sum over the log counts each once (the parent's figures never hold a Canoa child's).
The allowances an agent reports are kept as the latest for each account (`limits.json`), not appended. Answered by
the `usage` grant (above).

```toml
[host.usage]
keep_days = 400                     # a month's file is removed at start once all of it is older; at least 1
```

**Session history.** The host records each session it serves in the session-history format (`coqui-acp-history`) in its home,
`history/`, one `<sha256 of entry and session id>.jsonl` a session in a directory and files only this user reads (`0700`/`0600`
on Unix). A turn is written when it ends, and a connection that ends inside one writes it as ended with an error, so a stop
loses at most the turn in flight. It records what reaches the agent and what the agent says, after the host's own checks, and
never a message over the limit: a session that passes a limit keeps what it had and nothing more.

```toml
[host.history]
record = true                       # false records nothing
max_session_bytes = 16777216        # one session's history; at most 64 MiB, since a history is read whole to be moved
max_sessions = 1000                 # sessions with a history; a new one past it is not recorded
max_total_bytes = 1073741824        # every history together
```

| Method | Params | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `history/export` | `entry`, `session`, `max_bytes`? (1 to 4 MiB, default 1 MiB), `after`? | `{"id", "text", "next"?}`: one page of whole lines of the history as it is, the first with the header; `next` is the `after` of the following page |
| `history/move` | `entry`, `session`, `to` (an entry that runs a harness), `cwd`? (absolute; the history's own by default, and under the target's working roots; needed when the host has no record of the session) | `{"session", "entry", "cwd", "replayed", "compacted", "seededFrom"?, "recorded", "via": "record" or "provider", "losses": [{"kind", "count", "detail"?}]}`: the history this host recorded, else the one `entry`'s own agent exports for `session` in `cwd`; a provider that can do neither is refused with its reason |
| `history/portable` | none | `{"entries": {NAME: {"export", "import", "replay", "why"?}}}` for every entry that runs a harness: whether its provider exports a session in the standard format and imports one (`replay`: only as context in the first prompt, where `replay_max_bytes` is set). Found by asking the provider about a session that cannot exist, so a provider that is not local to this machine, or Pi's where the spawner is remote, answers its own refusal in `why` |
| `history/account` | `entry`, `session`, `cwd` (absolute), `from`? (an account of the entry's provider, listed by the entry or not; the entry's first by default, else the login the program has where it runs), `to` (another; `null` for either is that login) | `{"moved": false, "session", "cwd"}` when neither account has a folder of its own (they keep conversations in one place, so the session resumes as it is), else `{"moved": true, "session" (a new id), "cwd", "via": "provider" or "record", "replayed", "losses"}`: the provider's `export_session` from `from`'s folder, imported by `import_session` into `to`'s at a new id; where the provider cannot export, the history this host recorded stands in, and where neither exists the answer is `-32002` saying why. `from` and `to` are held for the call, so a connection still running on one makes it wait up to 5 seconds |
| `history/importable` | `harness` (an agent whose crate's `agent_vendor` provider finds its program's sessions: `providers/list` says `imports`), `entry` | `{"harness", "entry", "found", "sessions": [{"id", "title"?, "cwd"?, "bytes", "updated", "imported"}]}`: the sessions in that program's own folder, newest first, at most 500; titles and folders only, never what was said |
| `history/import` | `harness`, `entry`, `ids` (at most 200 UUIDs from a listing) | `{"imported": [{"id", "session", "title"?, "turns", "notCarried": {"lines"}}], "refused": [{"id", "why"}]}` |
| `history/imported` | `entry` | `{"entry", "sessions": [{"session", "title"?, "cwd"?, "at"?}]}`: what `history/import` brought in under that entry, newest first |

- `entry` and `session` are the source entry's name and the session id its client used. A session with no history, an
  entry that is not a harness and a host that records nothing answer `-32003` or `-32002` with the reason.
- A move imports the history into the target at a **new** UUID the host makes (never an existing session), by the provider's
  `import_session`: Claude and Codex write their own session files; OpenCode writes it into the target account's folder, or with no account into the store its environment names (`opencode import`; the session then has the host's UUID); an entry whose provider replays (`replay_max_bytes` set on
  its settings) carries the history as context in the first prompt and says so (`replayed`, and `replayed_as_prompt` among the
  losses); any other provider refuses with its reason. The new session then has a history of its own (`recorded`), which can be
  exported or moved again. A client opens it by `session/resume` or `session/load` of that id on the target's entry.
- `losses` is what did not carry (`thinking`, `tool_as_note`, `attachment_by_digest`, `replayed_as_prompt`, `history_cut`,
  `item_cut`, `non_text_omitted`, `summary_missing`), counts only, never the lost content. The history is context, never
  commands: nothing is run again.
- **Importing earlier sessions.** The person's own Claude Code and Codex sessions are read from where those programs keep them
  (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `.claude`, `CODEX_HOME` or `.codex`, in the user's home) and only when asked: nothing is read at start-up
  or by any other method, and the folders are never written. A listing walks the folders by their layout (a link is not
  followed; a name that is not a UUID is no session) and reads the first stretch of each file for its title and folder. An import
  reads each chosen file whole, converts it with the harness's own codec (`coqui-acp-claude` and `coqui-acp-codex`, `history`
  feature, under the format's rules for hostile histories) and keeps it as a history of the host's own under `entry` and the
  harness's session id, which `history/export` and `history/move` then serve like any recorded session. A file that holds no
  conversation, is larger than the codec's bound or does not fit `[host.history]` is `refused` with the reason and the rest still
  come; lines of a file that are not JSON are skipped and counted in `notCarried`. A session imported already is refused, never
  replaced. `title` and `cwd` are shown text: control and bidirectional characters are removed and they are cut at 120
  characters. The three methods are the owner's alone, with no grant, and a host that records nothing refuses them.
- Every history method is the owner's alone, over the app's own link: a recorded session holds what the person and the agent said,
  tool output included, which a paired key has no grant to read.

**`worktree/*`** takes the params and answers the results of `_canoa/worktree/*` ("Worktrees" below), with every check of
theirs unchanged (branch names, repositories led out by links or share paths, no hooks, `MAX_WORKTREES`). The owner (the app)
may name any absolute path on this computer; a key with the `worktree` grant only paths under the `working_roots` of the entries
whose `peers` allow it (all paired keys when an entry has none) and that have `worktrees` on, else `-32602`. The entry-level
`_canoa/worktree/*` stays for a client that reaches an agent over the network and holds no control grant.

**The program Store** (`canoa_host::store`, `Programs`) is carried the same way. `provider` names a store (`claude_store`, `codex_store`,
`grok_store`, `agy_store`, `pi_store`, `opencode_store`; `ServeOptions::with_programs` offers others), a program kept under the user's programs folder.
The params and results are the JSON of `canoa::store`'s models:

| Method | Params | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `store/list` | `kind`? (`agents`, the default, or `runtimes`) | `{"stores": [StoreInfo + "provider"]}`: the agents' programs, or the Runtimes type's (`node_store`, `python_store`, `whisper_store`, `piper_store`, `git_bash_store` on Windows and `powershell_store` on Windows and Linux, each with its `summary`, `icon` and `commands`); every other `store/*` method takes either kind's provider |
| `store/list` | `kind`: `extension`, `type`: a type's id | the same shape for the stores of a type an extension adds; an unknown type is `-32003` |
| `store/extensions` | none | `{"types": [{"id", "title", "icon"?, "stores": [provider ids], "extension", "extension_title", "publisher"}]}`: the types the `[host.extensions]` add, in canoa.cfg's order; needs `store.read` |
| `store/catalogs` | `{"type"?}` | for each Store type that reads catalogs (`overlays`, `skills`, `mcps`, `extensions` and the types an extension gives `catalogs` words) `{"type", "title", "words": {noun, plural, lists}, "default"?: {label, source}, "catalogs": [{label, provider, fields, source?, default, problem?}], "sources": [{id, title, help?, extension?, types, schema?, form?}]}`: the catalogs of `[store.TYPE.catalogs]` (the default first unless the table names its label or sets it `false`) and the `catalog_source` providers that serve the type, `read` first; `type` answers one, an unknown one is `-32003`; needs `overlay.read` |
| `store/catalog` | `{"type": "skills" or "mcps", "label", "search"?, "cursor"?}` | one catalog of that type read through its `catalog_source` provider: Skills `{"type", "label", "provider", "commit"?, "skills": [{name, description, source, path, ref?, problem?}], "skipped", "truncated"}`, MCP servers `{"type", "label", "provider", "servers": [...], "metadata": {"nextCursor"}}`; an unknown label is `-32003`, a provider that fails or answers in another form `-32002`; needs `overlay.write` |
| `extension/catalogs` | `{"refresh"?}` | the extensions catalogs of `[store.extensions.catalogs]`, as `store/index` answers for `[store.overlays.catalogs]`; needs `overlay.read` (`refresh` fetches, and takes `overlay.write`) |
| `extension/plan` | `{"catalog", "name"}` | the plan to install the extension `name` of the catalog labelled `catalog`, shaped as `store/plan`'s (`action: "install"`, `downloads` with the digest as its check, `changes` naming the folder and the `[host.extensions.NAME]` it would write, `notices`, `token`) plus `extension: {catalog, name, provider}`; nothing is fetched. The host takes the entry from its own index of the extensions catalogs canoa.cfg names, so a client names neither a source nor a digest; the entry must name a `load` file for a tree, fit this host (`needs`: kind `store_extension` at version `1`, this OS and architecture) and be one `canoa_host` reads (`-32002` in words otherwise); needs `hosting` |
| `extension/apply` | `{"plan"}` | fetches the plan's source (a Git tree, with the person's own git settings when it is in the catalog's own repository and through the isolated fetch skills use otherwise, or one `http` or `https` file, any origin and redirect since the digest decides), computes the tree digest or the SHA-256 **before anything is kept**, refuses a mismatch (`-32002`, nothing left), keeps the files in `<home>/extensions/NAME/` and writes `[host.extensions.NAME]` (`library`, `provider`, `pin` and `root` or `sha256`) through `[host]`'s own check and its digest, so another page's change is never overwritten; `{"installed": true, "name", "path", "restart": true}`. A plan whose `token` is not the one the host would make now (the catalog's entry changed) is refused: plan again. Files of an extension of that name the running host has loaded cannot be replaced on Windows: the refusal says to remove it, restart and install again; needs `hosting` |
| `extension/remove` | `{"name"}` | takes `[host.extensions.NAME]` out of canoa.cfg and, when its library is in `<home>/extensions/NAME/`, that folder; `{"removed": true, "files_removed": bool, "restart": true}` (a library the running host has loaded may stay until it restarts); needs `hosting` |
| `foundry/models` | none | `{"installed": bool, "program"?, "models": [{"id", "alias"?, "task"?, "device"?, "size_mb"?, "description", "cached", "loaded", "window": {"context_length", "max_length", "chosen"}}]}`: Foundry Local's catalog (its variants) with what is downloaded and what is in memory marked, loaded first, read by running its command line (`model list --variants [--cached\|--loaded] -o json`; what is loaded is asked only while `~/.foundry/daemon.json` names a running service, so listing starts none); without Foundry Local `{"installed": false, "models": []}`. Needs `store.programs`: it runs a program as this user |
| `foundry/model` | `model` (a variant id), `action` (`download`, `load` or `unload`) | `{"ok": true, "model", "action", "note"?}`: runs `foundry model <action> <model> -o json` and ends it after 4 hours (download), 15 minutes (load) or 2 minutes (unload); a refusal is the program's own words (`-32002`), a name that could be an option or an unknown action `-32602`. A load keeps the model in memory until `foundry server stop`, which `note` says. Needs `store.programs` |
| `foundry/window` | `model` (a variant id), `window` (tokens, at least 1, or null) | `{"ok": true, "model", "window", "note"?, "applies"}`: keeps the window the model is run with in canoa.cfg's `[foundry.windows]` (a key per variant id), lowered to the model's own `context_length` when it is downloaded and above it (`note` says so), or takes the setting away with null. Used when an account runs the model with `engine = "sdk"`, from the next gateway; the service engine ignores it. Needs `store.programs` and a canoa.cfg the host may write |
| `store/report` | `provider`, `probe`? (bool), `pick`? | `ProgramReport`: the copies the host finds; no network |
| `store/latest` | `provider`, `channel`? | `LatestReport` |
| `store/plan` | `provider`, `action` (`install`, `update`, `uninstall`), `channel`?, `version`? | `InstallPlan`, with its `token`: what an apply would do, nothing written |
| `store/apply` | `provider`, `plan` (as handed back) | `{"applied": bool, "version", "dir", "lines"}`; `applied` is false when an update has nothing to do |
| `store/rollback` | `provider` | `{"provider", "version", "dir"}` |
| `store/remove` | `provider` | `{"provider", "removed": true}` |

- The host applies **its own copy** of the plan it made (found by provider and `token`, used once, good for 15 minutes), so a plan a
  client forged or kept is refused `-32002`.
- **Accounts** (the owner's, or a key with the `accounts` grant; see "Accounts"): `accounts/catalog` `{"harness"}` -> `{"harness", "kinds": [{"id", "title", "description", "settings", "endpoints": [{"key", "purpose", "default"}], "rotates", "history_portable", "state_dir", "renews", "notices"}], "logins": [{"id", "title", "description", "produces", "inputs", "steps", "remote"}]}` (`settings` and `inputs` are schemas; an unknown harness is `-32003`); `accounts/list` `{}` -> `{"accounts": [{"name", "harness", "kind", "id", "state", "revision", "expires_at", "provides"}]}` (`provides`: the standards the account's kind provides, from the catalog of the provider that made it), the objects `canoa accounts list --format json` prints, read from the canoa.cfg files `serve` was started with each time, so an account another process added shows; `accounts/remove` `{"name"}` -> `{"removed": true}`, refused as the command is (held, offered by an entry). `accounts/add` `{"name", "harness", "method", "inputs"?}` and `accounts/login` `{"name", "method"?, "inputs"?}` are long requests that carry their progress like `store/apply`: `accounts/progress` notifications `{"id": the request's id, "say": text}` and `{"id", "open_url": {"url", "user_code", "expires_ms"}}`, and, when the sign-in asks for a typed value, the request `_coqui_acp/secret_request` (id `canoa-secret-N-HEX`, session `accounts`, the form's title as its message) whose key is bound by the link's secret (a key's connection: by that key's own, as an agent's secret prompt is), so the answer's secret fields come back sealed to a key made for that request and the host opens them itself; a declined form, an answer bound to anything else, the ask wait (four minutes) with no answer, or the link closing refuses or cancels the sign-in (the provider kills what it started and the sign-in's directory is removed). The result is `{"account": {"name", "harness", "kind", "id", "label", "checked"}}`; a bad request is `-32602` and a failed sign-in `-32002` with a reason that holds no typed value. The accounts are written to the person's own canoa.cfg, the file `canoa accounts` writes. A host with no secret to bind with refuses both sign-ins. The other requests on the link are answered meanwhile. `accounts/entries` `{}` -> `{"entries": [{"name", "harness", "accounts": [names, the first the default], "open", "accepts": [standards, most preferred first], "usable": [names]}]}` (`usable`: every account in canoa.cfg that can run a session on the entry, the provider's own and the others' whose `provides` meets `accepts`, offered by the entry or not): the agent entries that run a provider with accounts (canoa.cfg's, then the built-in sources Claude and Codex it has not named; a built-in source is `open` only on a portal's host, which only the person's browser reaches). `accounts/offer` `{"entry", "accounts": [names], "harness"?}` -> `{"entry", "accounts", "restart": true}` makes the entry offer exactly those accounts, in that order (none takes the key out), in the person's own canoa.cfg, in its own format; every name must be an account that can run on the entry's harness: its provider's, or another's whose kind provides a standard the harness accepts (`-32003` for an unknown account or entry, `-32002` for one that cannot, the reason naming the standards each side says, `-32602` for a repeat). An entry canoa.cfg does not have is made: a built-in source keeps its `title` and `bypass`, and on a host that is not a portal's gets `peers = []` and `own_profile = true`, so that it reaches no paired key; any other name needs `harness` and is made private (`peers = []`), titled by its name. An entry an earlier version of the application made for accounts (`claude-accounts` titled "Claude accounts", `peers = []`, no other key) is folded into the provider's own entry the first time any accounts request reads canoa.cfg: its accounts are appended to that entry's and it is taken out; an entry of the person's that merely shares the name, or sets anything else, is left alone. Changes to canoa.cfg by an offer, an account added or removed and `config/set` are made one at a time in a host, each from the file the one before wrote. The host reads offers when it starts, so the application asks for `host/restart`. **An `accounts` key can attach any account to any harness entry, including an entry whose `peers` leave that key out.**
- An apply takes long and says where it is: the host sends `store/progress` notifications `{"id": the apply's request id, "text",
  "fraction"?}` on the same link before the answer, and a question the install asks the person comes as the
  request `_coqui_acp/secret_request`, put exactly as an `accounts/add` sign-in's form is (same asker: bound by the in-process link's
  secret, or by the paired key's own for a key's connection; id `canoa-secret-N-HEX`, session `store:PROVIDER` (a sign-in's is
  `accounts`) so the application can name the store whose install receives the value, the form's title as its message and its schema
  as the schema; the request is bound for the ask wait), so a typed secret comes back sealed to a key made for that request and the host opens it itself, and the plain
  fields come back as plain text. A host with no secret to bind with cannot ask: the install is told so. A declined form, an answer
  bound to anything else, the link closing, or the ask wait with no answer takes the question back and cancels the install (the
  provider's next call is told to stop). The ask wait is four minutes, the time the request stays bound and the longest the
  application accepts (`ServeOptions::with_ask_wait`, below), the same for a sign-in. Other requests on the link are still answered meanwhile.
- `store/list`, `store/report`, `store/latest` and `store/plan` need `store.read`; `store/report` with `probe` or `pick`, which runs a program of the user's, needs `store.programs`, and a plan a key asks for is not kept unless it holds that grant. `store/apply`, `store/rollback` and
  `store/remove` need `store.programs` (the owner holds every grant): there is no `store.write` grant and `[host.control]` refuses the name, since an install
  runs code as the user. A store that is not one of the host's is `-32003`; a plan or install the host refuses `-32002`.
- The overlay store's git half is `overlay/*` and `catalog/*` (see "Overlays for a page" above).

**Store extensions** (`[host.extensions.NAME]`, `canoa::store::extension`): a `store_extension` provider, loaded from a library or a
source like any other, whose `describe` names the types it adds (`id`: 1 to 32 lower-case letters and digits, not `overlays`,
`skills`, `agents`, `mcps` or `runtimes`; `title`; `icon`?; `stores`: the `program_store` provider ids the same library offers). The
host loads each into a registry of its own, lists its types in `store/extensions`, and plans, applies, reports and removes through the
same `store/*` methods and the same plan the person confirms, so an extension writes no installer. One that does not load, names a
type already taken, or a store it does not offer (or one of the built-in ones) is skipped and logged; the others stay. The app lists
Runtimes and each type after the built-in ones; an installed item the host holds its own copy of has a Remove that shows the plan for
`store/remove`'s uninstall as an install's. The app's Settings, Extensions (for a key that holds `hosting`) lists `[host.extensions]` from
`config/get` `host`, adds, edits and removes one by writing `[host]` back with `config/check` then `config/set` (the host reads it only when
it starts, so the page offers to restart it). `icon` is an app icon's name or a small `data:image/png;base64,` or `data:image/webp;base64,`
picture. `examples/sample_extension.rs` is a stand-in library; `tests/extension_stores.rs` runs it.

**Extensions catalogs** (`[store.extensions.catalogs]`, `store/extension_catalog.rs`, `control/extension_install.rs`). A table of label to git source (`<repository>@<ref>#<file>`), none until the person adds one and never mixed with `[store.overlays.catalogs]`; the file is Canoa's own TOML format, written down in `docs/configuration.md`: `[extensions.NAME]` entries, each pinned (a git or relative `source` by the `pin` tree digest, an `http` or `https` URL by its `sha256`), with `provider`, `load` (or `runtime`), `needs` and informational fields. The reader is `store::extension_catalog` (an entry is refused alone, with a class: `invalid-name`, `duplicate-name`, `invalid-entry`, `invalid-source`, `invalid-pin`, `unpinned`, `ref-not-allowed`, `leaves-repository`; where a source is, and what it needs to reach it (a path, `file:`, `ssh`, credentials, a query, `http`), is never a reason), and `StoreIndex` keeps each catalog under `extensions:LABEL`, a key no overlay catalog's label can take, fetched into `.catalogs/.extensions/LABEL.git`. The catalog is fetched with the person's own git settings; a source an entry names is fetched with none of them unless it is in the catalog's own repository. `extension/catalogs` answers like `store/index` (`kind: "extension_catalog"` sources and `kind: "extension"` entries with `extension: {provider, pin | sha256, load?, needs}` beside `origin`). This host loads one library file: a tree entry names it with `load`, a URL is the library; or a package of scripts: `runtime = "js"` (or `python`) names no file, the folder is the library, and the install writes `source = "bundled:js"`, `source_settings = { dirs }`, `pin` and `root` instead of `library` (`runtime = "python"` is refused at the plan, the host carries no Python source). `extension/catalogs` entries carry `runtime` in `extension`. The same bytes install on every platform; `tests/extension_install.rs` installs a package from a local catalog on two hosts and lists it after a restart. Nothing of an extension is kept before its digest has been computed over what was fetched; the host records `pin` and `root` (or `sha256`) in canoa.cfg and checks them again, before the file is opened, each time it loads the extension, and an extension whose bytes changed is skipped and logged. An extension named by hand has no digest and is loaded as it always was. `tests/extension_install.rs` installs the sample extension from a local catalog and loads it after a restart.

**Installed runtimes** (Node, Python; Store type Runtimes) are put on the `PATH` of every program the host starts through `spawn::Command`
(agents, their MCP servers, tools), after the person's own `PATH` entries so their own copy wins; a program started with no `PATH` gets none.

**The catalog index** (`store/index`, `store::index`) lists every `[store.overlays.catalogs]` source of the host's canoa.cfg (and, first, `boriken` = `https://github.com/Boriken-Dev/agent-overlays.git@main#catalog.toml` unless `[store.overlays.catalogs] boriken = false` leaves it out or a source of that label replaces it) and every program store as
one normalized list, so a client reads cards from one call and not one fetch an entry. `store/index` `{"refresh"?: bool, "icons"?: bool}`
-> `{"schema": 1, "sources": [...], "entries": [...]}`. A source is `{"id": "catalog:LABEL" | "programs", "kind", "label"?, "spec"?,
"commit"?, "checked" (ms), "stale", "refreshing", "name"?, "description"?, "problem"?, "entries"}`; an entry is `{"id", "kind": "overlay" |
"skill" | "program", "catalog", "name", "summary", "source", "origin": {"kind", "location", "ref", "path"} | null (the source in parts, null for a refused entry), "version", "tags", "requirements", "pin", "icon", "contents": {"skills",
"hooks", "env_scripts", "mcp_servers"} | null, "problem"?}` plus `homepage`, `repository`, `license` and `authors` where the catalog
says them (a program entry also carries `program`, `provider` and `channels`). The sources are canoa.cfg's, never a key's: a key names
none, so the host fetches only places its owner chose. `contents` and `requirements` are read from the manifest of an entry that lives in
the catalog's own repository; one elsewhere is the catalog's claim only. Nothing runs: no hook, setup or install.

- Kept as `<home>/cache/store/s1-catalog-LABEL/<commit>/index.json` (and `icons/`), written beside and renamed; the `s1` is the form's
  version, the label a valid overlay name, and only the newest commit of a source stays. A different `spec` for a label is another source.
  A host without a state folder keeps the index in memory.
- **Fresh** (checked less than `index_ttl` seconds ago): answered with no process started. **Stale**: answered at once, `stale: true`, and
  refreshed on a thread (one per source): `git ls-remote` for the ref, and a fetch only when it moved. `refresh: true`, or a source with
  no index yet, waits for that. A source that fails keeps its last index with `stale: true` and `problem`, and is tried again no sooner
  than `index_ttl` later. An entry the catalog refused or whose manifest does not read is a card with `problem`; the others are listed.
- `icons: true` adds the icon's bytes (`icon.data`, base64) for entries whose icon file passed AOS's checks and fit `index_cache_bytes`.
- Programs come from `store/list`: no network, no cache.
- Runtimes (`node_store`: nodejs.org builds checked against `SHASUMS256.txt`, channels `lts` and `current`; `python_store`: the standalone CPython builds checked against the `SHA256SUMS` of the newest release, channels `3.11` to `3.14`) are kept on the programs folder as `node` and `python`, installed and removed with `store/plan`, `store/apply` and `store/remove` like an agent's. A signature is not checked, and the plan says so. An archive's links are left out, so a Unix copy gets `npm`, `npx`, `python3` and `python` written beside the interpreter.
- `bitwarden_store` (the **Secret providers** type, listed by `store/extensions` as `secrets` with `extension` `built-in`, and by `store/list` `{"kind": "extension", "type": "secrets"}`) keeps Bitwarden's command line as `bw`: the `cli-vYYYY.M.P` release of github.com/bitwarden/clients (the newest of the latest hundred releases of that repository, which also publishes the other clients), the zip of this system (`bw-windows-`, `bw-linux-`, `bw-linux-arm64-`, `bw-macos-`, `bw-macos-arm64-VERSION.zip`; Windows has one build, which ARM64 runs through emulation) holding the one program, checked against the SHA-256 GitHub lists for it, whose publisher signature is not checked. It is on no search path of what the host starts (`commands` is empty): only the spawner a secret provider is handed finds it, by its bare name (`store::runtimes::SECRET_PROGRAMS`), so an agent does not find it by name. Installing it reads no vault; the Bitwarden provider (see "Secret providers") is set up with `secrets/configure` afterwards.
- `whisper_store` (also Runtimes) keeps whisper.cpp's speech-to-text program and one model as `whisper` (MIT, ggml-org). The channel is the model (`base`, the default, `base.en`, `tiny`, `tiny.en`, `small-q5_1`, `small`, `small.en`, `large-v3-turbo-q5_0`): the `ggml-*.bin` file of the publisher's Hugging Face repository at one pinned revision, checked against the SHA-256 that revision lists. The program is the Windows or Linux build (x86-64 and ARM64; none for macOS) from the release's own files, or, for a `vX.Y.Z` release that carries none, from the `bNNNN` build release published up to six hours before it (the program reports the version of that commit, which the install checks); each is checked against the SHA-256 GitHub lists. The copy is `bin/whisper-cli` and `models/ggml-<model>.bin` in the version's folder, on no `PATH`; a plan names its model in its downloads, and the engine asks the recipe's `channel_of` for it again at apply. Another model is another install (remove, then install with the channel). `speech/enable` makes the speech provider of the installed copy.
- `foundry_store` (the program of the Store type `foundry` that `canoa-foundry`'s `foundry_extension` adds, which `store/extensions` lists as built in) keeps Foundry Local's command line, service and libraries as `foundry`: the `cli-preview-X.Y.Z` release of github.com/microsoft/foundry-local, the Windows MSIX unpacked as a zip (never installed), the Linux `tar.gz` or, for Apple silicon Macs only, the `osx-arm64.zip` (whose `libonnxruntime.1.dylib` link is written as a copy), checked against the SHA-256 GitHub lists for that file. Its folder is not on any program's `PATH`: `foundry/models` and `foundry/model` find the copy by its launch path, else the first `foundry` on the `PATH`.
- `git_bash_store` (Runtimes, Windows only) keeps Git for Windows' portable release as `git-bash` (`git/bin/bash.exe`, `sh.exe` and `git.exe`, which are on the `PATH` of what the host starts like Node's): the `PortableGit-VERSION-64-bit` or `-arm64` `.7z.exe` of the newest non-prerelease `vX.Y.Z.windows.N` release (version `X.Y.Z`, or `X.Y.Z.N` for N above 1, as the files spell it), checked against the SHA-256 GitHub lists for that file. MinGit is not used: it holds no `bash.exe` and a thin set of tools. The archive is a self-extracting program of the publisher's, which the plan says the host runs once with `-y -o<folder>` (the step is the recipe's `finish`, through the guarded spawner: an absolute program in the version's folder) and deletes; the version read back is `git --version`'s, not bash's. A `bash` found elsewhere is listed with who put it there (Git for Windows, MSYS2, WSL); its version is the `git` it finds, when it finds one.
- `powershell_store` (Runtimes, Windows and Linux) keeps PowerShell 7 as `powershell` (`pwsh` on the `PATH`): the `PowerShell-VERSION-win-x64|arm64.zip` or `powershell-VERSION-linux-x64|arm64.tar.gz` of the newest release that is no prerelease, checked against the SHA-256 GitHub lists for that file; its own update check and telemetry are off in a copy the host keeps (`POWERSHELL_UPDATECHECK=Off`, `POWERSHELL_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT=1`). A Mac lists neither store (`store::runtimes::runtimes_here`).
- `piper_store` (also Runtimes) keeps Piper, a local neural text-to-speech engine, as `piper`: the `piper/` folder of its last binary release (`2023.11.14-2`, which prints `1.2.0`; the project is archived and the release publishes no checksums, so each digest is the SHA-256 Canoa measured from the published file) and a `voices/` folder with `en_US-ljspeech-medium` (public domain) and `es_MX-claude-high` (Apache 2.0), each pinned to one commit of `rhasspy/piper-voices`. Channels `standard` (both voices), `english` and `spanish`. Windows on ARM runs the x64 build under emulation (measured); a Linux archive's libraries' short names, which an unpack leaves out as links, are written as copies by the install. Its folder is on no `PATH`: the `piper` voice provider finds it (see **Speech output**). `store_piper`'s `--ignored` test downloads the real release and speaks with both voices.
- `[store.mcps.catalogs]` and `[store.skills.catalogs]` are not in `store/index`: they are read when asked, through `store/catalog` (below).

**Catalogs by Store type and catalog sources** (`store/catalogs.rs`, `store/sources.rs`, `control/catalog_types.rs`). Every type's catalogs are `[store.TYPE.catalogs.LABEL]`, one reader (`store::catalogs::read`): a bare string is `{ provider = "read", source = string }`, a table is `{ provider?, ...fields }` with `read` the default provider, `false` takes a default away. The reader never mixes types: `table_of(store, "skills")` is the Skills table only. A catalog is read by a `catalog_source` provider (`canoa::store::source`): `read` (the type's own reader; for overlays and extensions the git path of `store/index`) and `command` (`fields`: `command` list, `cwd`, `file`, `timeout` seconds, default 60) are JavaScript libraries that ship in the host (`extensions/catalog_sources/`, written with the Canoa SDK, kept as files by `scripts::Bundle`) found through a registry as an extension's would be, and the `catalog_source` providers of each `[host.extensions]` library are added beside them (`Sources::add`; an id already offered refuses the whole extension). A catalog whose provider is not `read` is asked for its text, by `StoreIndex::refresh_read`, each time its index is refreshed (when the time to live ends or a refresh is asked): the provider's text is indexed as a fetched catalog is (no repository, so a relative source or icon in it is refused), keyed by its SHA-256, and a failure keeps the last good index with the provider's reason as `problem`. `command` starts its program through the spawner each `read` is handed (`Reach`: this machine's, with the person's own reach, whose `read_file` reads any absolute path and which keeps and removes no file) and closes its standard input, keeps the end (2 KiB) of what it wrote on standard error for the reason, reads at most 4 MiB of standard output and stops after `timeout` seconds, ending the program's tree; it serves every type (`SourceInfo::types` empty; an extension's source lists the types it serves). Writing a `[store]` that names a provider other than `read` it did not hold takes `hosting` (`config/set` and `config/check`, `Control::guard_sources`).

**Skills and MCP servers catalogs through the same sources** (`control/catalog_read.rs`, `store/listings.rs`). `store/catalog` `{"type": "skills" | "mcps", "label", "search"?, "cursor"?}` reads one catalog of canoa.cfg (the label names it; a key never names a place) and answers `{"type", "label", "provider", ...}`; it needs `overlay.write`, as `skill/scan` and `registry/fetch` do, and `overlays` and `extensions` are refused (they are read through `store/index`). `store/catalogs` lists the MCP servers type's official registry first as `{"label": "official", "default": true, "fixed": true}`: it is not in canoa.cfg, cannot be removed, and a table row of that label is a `problem`. For the `read` provider the host reads the type itself:
- Skills: the `source` is parsed as `npx skills add` takes one (`store::listings::SkillSpec`: `owner/repo[/folder]`, `github:`/`gitlab:`, a tree address, a git address, a folder, `#ref` or `@version`, `@name` for one skill) and scanned as `skill/scan` scans (`own` unless the label is `anthropics` with Canoa's own default source), answering `{"commit", "skills": [{"name", "description", "source", "path", "problem"?}], "skipped", "truncated"}`.
- MCP servers: `source` is a registry's address (read as `registry/fetch` reads one, `<address>/v0.1/servers?limit=30` with `cursor` and `search` percent-encoded) or `<repository>@<ref>#<file>` (a file in the list shape, fetched into `.catalogs/registry-<16 hex of the source's SHA-256>.git`), answering the registry's list shape `{"servers": [...], "metadata": {"nextCursor"}}`.
Any other provider is asked for its text (`ReadRequest` with the type's id and the label) every time, and the host reads the text as data: Skills as a listing `{"skills": [{"name", "description"?, "source", "path"?, "ref"?}]}` (answered as above with `commit: null` and `ref`; an entry with no name or source or a repeated name is counted in `skipped`, one with an invalid name or source carries a `problem`, at most 1024), MCP servers as one page of the list shape (items with no `server.name` or marked not latest dropped, `search` words matched against name, title and description, at most 2000). A provider that fails or answers in another form is `-32002` with its reason. What a listed skill's `source` names is fetched with none of the person's git settings, as a `skill/search` hit's is.

## Worktrees

No public API: what `serve` answers on an agent or tool entry with `working_roots` and `worktrees` on (the default), before its agent starts. Off, or with no root, each is `-32602`.

| Method | Params | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `_canoa/worktree/new` | `cwd`, `branch` | `{"path", "branch", "repo"}`: `path` and `repo` written under the root as configured |
| `_canoa/worktree/status` | `path` | `{"exists": false}`, or `{"exists": true, "branch" (null when detached), "detached", "changed", "commits", "listing"}` |
| `_canoa/worktree/remove` | `path`, `discard` (bool, default false) | `{"removed": true, "branch", "branchKept", "commits"}` |
| `_canoa/worktree/diff` | `path`, `base` (a commit's hex id; HEAD when absent) | `{"exists": false}`, or `{"exists": true, "head", "branch", "base", "added", "removed", "files", "more"}`; a file is `{"path", "status" (`A`, `M`, `D`), "added", "removed", "binary", "patch", "cut", "print"}` |

- **A branch** is `canoa/` then runs of `a-z0-9` joined by single hyphens, 70 bytes at most; any other is `-32602` before git runs.
- **A path** (`cwd`, `path`) must be plain absolute, on a drive on Windows (a share or device path is refused unlooked at), under a root as written, and, resolved by `canonicalize`, under a root resolved the same way. A missing `path` is `{"exists": false}` for `status`.
- **Every request** first refuses a repository (`-32602`) when a `.git` file from the directory up to the roots, or its `commondir`, names a share or device; when `git config --local` or `--worktree` holds a key matching `^(filter|include|includeif)\.|^core\.attributesfile$`; when `rev-parse --git-common-dir` resolved is not the main worktree's `.git` (a submodule, `--separate-git-dir`) or is outside the roots; or when it has `objects/info/alternates`.
- **`new`**: the repository's main worktree (`git worktree list --porcelain -z`, its first entry; a bare one refused) resolved must be under a resolved root, and so must its parent; the worktree goes in `<parent>/<name>.worktrees/<branch less canoa/>`. An existing `.worktrees` that is a link or not a directory, a worktree already there, or `MAX_WORKTREES` (32) worktrees of the repository already in it, is refused. Then `git worktree add -b BRANCH -- PATH HEAD` from `cwd`; a result whose resolved path is not itself is removed again and refused.
- **`status` and `remove`** take only a worktree `new` would have made: its own top level (`rev-parse --show-toplevel`), not the main worktree, where `new` puts the branch `canoa/<its directory's name>`, whatever its HEAD is now; else `-32602`. `changed` counts what `git status --porcelain=v1 -z --untracked-files=all --ignored=matching --ignore-submodules=all` lists, and `listing` is the SHA-256 of that output in hexadecimal; `commits` counts `rev-list HEAD [refs/heads/BRANCH] --not --exclude=BRANCH --branches --remotes --tags`.
- **`diff`** is read only and takes any repository the checks above accept, not only a worktree `new` made. It lists what differs from `base` in the folder `path` names (paths relative to it): tracked files by `git diff` with no external diff program and no text conversion, and untracked files that are small text, shown as added lines. `patch` holds the hunks with no file header, cut at 96 KiB (`cut`); a binary file, or one over 20,000 changed lines, has none. `print` stands for the file's change, so two reads with one print show it changed the same way. At most 300 files are listed (`more`), the totals count all. Git runs with `--no-optional-locks`, so it never rewrites the index. A `base` that is not a commit of the repository is `-32602`.
- **`remove`** refuses a detached HEAD reaching any commit no branch, tag or remote has, whatever `discard` says, and anything `changed` counts without `discard` (git itself would remove ignored files). Then `git worktree remove [--force] -- PATH` (`--force` only with `discard`), and, when the branch exists and holds no commit of its own, `git update-ref -d refs/heads/BRANCH TIP` at the tip counted: a branch that moved meanwhile stays (`branchKept`).
- **Git** is `git` on the host's `PATH`, run as `git -C DIR -c core.hooksPath=NUL|/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false ...` with every `GIT_` variable of the host's removed, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` the roots' parents, ended after 120 s; a failure is `-32603` `git failed: ` and up to 1 KiB of git's own words, control characters made spaces.
- A request is refused, never forwarded, once the agent runs (`-32600`), and a notification of one is dropped.

## Pairing (`canoa_host::peers`)

```rust
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Peer { pub id: PeerId, pub state: PeerState }
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum PeerState { Accepted, Waiting { source: Option<SocketAddr> } }
pub fn list(store: &TrustStore) -> Result<Vec<Peer>, PeersError>     // accepted, then waiting, each by id
pub fn accept(store: &TrustStore, id: &str) -> Result<PeerId, PeersError>
pub fn revoke(store: &TrustStore, id: &str) -> Result<bool, PeersError>   // false: the store did not know it
```

A key is paired when the store accepts it. `accept` takes only a key that is waiting, so a person pairs a client by connecting with it once, then accepting the id the client shows.

## The portal (`canoa_host::portal`, feature `portal`, Unix; `canoa_host::portal_link`)

`canoa portal` serves the web app to people who sign in with their account on this machine, and gives each a `canoa serve --portal` of their own, started as them. The guide is `docs/portal.md`; this is the contract.

```rust
pub const COOKIE: &str = "__Host-canoa";
pub const LOGIN: &str = "/portal/login"; pub const METHODS: &str = "/portal/methods"; pub const CERTIFICATE: &str = "/portal/certificate"; pub const FACTOR: &str = "/portal/factor";
pub const MAX_PASSWORD: usize = 1024;

#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Settings { pub socket: Option<PathBuf>, pub socket_group: Option<String>, pub listen: Option<SocketAddr>, pub site: Option<PathBuf>, pub socket_dir: PathBuf, pub unit: String, pub systemctl: PathBuf, pub elevate: Vec<String>, pub pam_service: String, pub min_uid: u32, pub groups: Vec<String>, pub authenticators: Vec<Entry>, pub sign_in_policy: Option<Entry>, pub certificate_header: String, pub libraries_any_owner: bool, pub enrol: Duration, pub hosts: Vec<String>, pub trusted_proxies: Vec<IpAddr>, pub client_address_header: String, pub session: Duration, pub idle: Duration, pub start: Duration, pub pair: Duration, pub max_failures_per_pair: usize, pub max_failures_per_address: usize, pub slow_after_user_failures: usize, pub slow: Duration, pub failure_window: Duration, pub failure_floor: Duration, pub max_connections: usize, pub max_sign_ins: usize, pub max_sessions: usize, pub max_sessions_per_user: usize, pub max_hosts: usize, pub max_relays_per_user: usize, pub max_message: usize, pub request: Duration, pub keep_alive: Duration, pub max_requests: usize, pub office: bool, pub office_frames: Vec<String>, pub office_handoff: Duration }   // Default, Clone, Eq
pub fn Settings::from_toml(text: &str) -> Result<Settings, String>   // deny_unknown_fields; the keys are the fields' names, durations as `NAME_seconds`, `max_message` as `max_message_bytes`
pub fn Settings::check(&self) -> Result<(), String>   // a `socket` or a `listen`, loopback `listen`, absolute `socket`, `{user}` in `unit`, a plain `pam_service`, an absolute `socket_dir`, `min_uid` of 1 or more, no zero limit

pub trait Accounts: Send + Sync { fn sign_in(&self, user: &str, password: &str, providers: &[String]) -> SignIn /* only these providers, in this order; empty is every one that takes a password */; fn methods(&self) -> Vec<Method> /* default [Password] */; fn carries_certificate(&self, headers: &[(String, String)]) -> bool /* any forwarded certificate, readable or not; a certificate sign-in without one is neither counted nor asked of a provider; default true */; fn sign_in_certificate(&self, headers: &[(String, String)], client: IpAddr, providers: &[String]) -> SignIn /* default Refused */; fn certificate_status(&self, user: &str, uid: u32, headers: &[(String, String)]) -> Option<CertificateStatus> /* default None */; fn pin(&self, user: &str, uid: u32, headers: &[(String, String)]) -> Result<(), PinError> /* default Unsupported */; fn unpin(&self, user: &str) -> Result<bool, PinError> /* default Unsupported */; fn still(&self, user: &str, uid: u32) -> Standing /* default Present(vec![]) */; }   // blocks; `still` is asked each time a host is wanted, and a transient directory failure is retried once, then `Unknown`, which ends nothing
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Standing { Present(Vec<String>) /* the account's groups now */, Gone, Unknown }
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum SignIn { Admitted { user: String, uid: u32, groups: Vec<String>, provider: String }, Refused, NoCertificate, Expired, Unpinned, Failed(String) }   // NoCertificate, Expired and Unpinned are what a certificate sign-in can say beyond Refused (no certificate sent, past its dates, known to no provider); the page and the log tell them apart, and a password sign-in never answers them. Admitted: the account's name as the system spells it, its user id and groups (what the sign-in policy's `subjects` read) and the id of the provider that accepted the credential
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Method { Password, Certificate, Totp, RecoveryCode }   // Method::id() is "password" | "certificate" | "totp" | "recovery_code"; Method::from_id(word) is the reverse, None for a word of the policy's the portal has no use for; Method::needs_account() is true for the last two, which are checked for an account an earlier step named and are never offered first
// Accounts also has check_code(user, uid, method, code, providers) -> CodeCheck { Valid { provider } | Refused | Failed(why) }, factor_record(user, uid) -> (FactorStanding { Enrolled | Disabled | Reset | NotEnrolled | Unsupported | Unknown }, Option<i64> made)` (one read: an error is `Unknown`, never `NotEnrolled`; `factor_standing` is its first half), `unlock_stamp(user, uid) -> Option<i64>`, begin_factor(user, uid, proved_current) -> Result<Enrolment { uri, codes }, FactorError> (an account that has a code is refused a new one unless `proved_current`; the portal sets it only when the setup's shown steps hold a `totp` or `recovery_code`, `canoa portal factor enrol` never), confirm_factor(user, uid, code) -> Result<provider id, FactorError>, reset_factor(user, uid) -> Result<bool, FactorError>; the defaults refuse / say Unsupported. Access implements them over the one authenticator that takes `otp` (`coqui_access_totp`, loaded from a `library`, which must name a `source` file: the authenticator and its account kind are two instances that share it), and has admitted(name) and list_factors() -> Vec<FactorEntry { id, user, enrolled, disabled, waiting, reset }>, reset_by_id(key), clear_reset(user, uid) for `canoa portal factor`. Enrolment's Debug shows no link or code.
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Entry { pub provider: String, pub library: Option<PathBuf>, pub config: serde_json::Map<String, Value>, pub enrol: bool }   // one authenticator of `authenticators`; Entry::new(provider); Debug shows the config's keys, never its values
pub struct Presented { pub subject: String, pub fingerprint: String, pub expired: bool }   // a request's certificate: subject text (show escaped) and `sha256:` + lower-case hex
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum PinState { Yours, Another, Unpinned }
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct CertificateStatus { pub presented: Option<(Presented, PinState)>, pub pinned: bool, pub disabled: bool }   // `pinned` ignores a pin made for another user id and a disabled one
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum PinError { NoCertificate, Taken, Expired, Disabled, Unsupported, Failed(String) }
pub struct Access;   // Accounts over `Settings::authenticators`: Access::new(&Settings) -> Result<Access, String>. `coqui_access_system` is built in (its absent keys come from `pam_service`, `min_uid`, `groups`); any other provider is a Guatiao library named by `library`. A password goes, in order, to the providers that take a password (`Unknown` passes it on, `Invalid` ends the try, an error is `Failed`); a certificate (base64 DER, comma-separated, in `certificate_header`: one header, at most 16 KiB and 8 certificates) goes to those that take a request, as `Attempt::certificates` with `secure` and the browser's address as the peer; a leaf outside its validity dates is refused before any provider is asked. A `library` is refused unless the file and every directory above it are root-owned and not group- or other-writable (`libraries_any_owner` lifts it, with a `warn`), and a `source`/`waiting` file a group or others can read is warned about at start. A bearer-key provider is skipped. Whoever a provider names is looked up by name in the system directory under `min_uid` and `groups`. `enrol = true` on the one certificate entry makes its account kind the store of `pin`/`unpin` (one pinned certificate per account name, recorded with the group `uid:N` so a recreated name does not inherit it; a disabled record can be neither replaced nor removed from here; its directory says whose a fingerprint is); it must take a request and offer both kinds
pub const NAME_PATTERN: &str = "[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,31}";   // what the polkit rule and the root helper check, written out
pub fn valid_user(name: &str) -> bool   // exactly NAME_PATTERN: a leading digit is a user id to systemd, so never a name

pub trait Controller: Send + Sync { fn start(&self, user: &str) -> io::Result<()>; fn stop(&self, user: &str) -> io::Result<()>; fn running(&self) -> io::Result<Vec<String>>; }
pub struct Systemd { pub systemctl: PathBuf, pub elevate: Vec<OsString>, pub unit: String, pub list: PathBuf, pub timeout: Duration }   // Controller over `[elevate] systemctl start|stop --no-ask-password UNIT` (`systemctl` may be the helper) and `list` (`systemctl`, no privilege) `list-units`; each call is ended after `timeout` (60 s); start and stop refuse a name `valid_user` refuses, before any program runs
pub struct Sessions; pub struct Session { pub user: String, pub uid: u32, pub id: u64, pub password_at: Option<Instant>, pub method: Option<Method>, pub fingerprint: Option<String>, pub done: Vec<coqui_access::Factor> }; pub struct Throttle; pub struct Attempt { pub wait: Duration }; pub struct Instances; pub struct Lease; pub enum Conn { Tcp(..), Pipe(..) }
pub fn Sessions::new(lifetime: Duration, limit: usize, per_user: usize) -> Sessions
pub fn Sessions::open_with(&self, user: &str, uid: u32, method: Option<Method>, fingerprint: Option<String>, done: Vec<coqui_access::Factor>) -> Option<(String, Session)>   // `done` is the steps that signed it in, which resume asks the policy about; `open` is `open_with(.., None, None, vec![])`; a password method stamps `password_at`; `fingerprint` is the certificate the sign-in request carried
pub fn Sessions::close_certificates(&self, user: &str, keep: Option<&str>)   // ends the account's certificate sessions, except those of the fingerprint `keep`
pub fn Sessions::open(&self, user: &str, uid: u32) -> Option<(String, Session)>   // evicts the account's oldest at `per_user`; None when the table is full of others
pub fn Sessions::get(&self, token: &str) -> Option<Session>; pub fn Sessions::alive(&self, id: u64) -> bool; pub fn Sessions::close(&self, token: &str); pub fn Sessions::close_user(&self, user: &str)
pub fn Throttle::new(window: Duration, per_pair: usize, per_address: usize, slow_after: usize, slow: Duration) -> Throttle   // an address is an IPv4 address (IPv4-mapped IPv6 included) or an IPv6 /64; a success takes back its own try by id
pub fn Instances::new(controller: Arc<dyn Controller>, dir: impl Into<PathBuf>, idle: Duration, start_wait: Duration, max_relays: usize, max_hosts: usize) -> Arc<Instances>
pub fn Instances::dir_of(&self, user: &str) -> PathBuf; pub fn Instances::entry_socket(&self, user: &str, name: &str) -> PathBuf
pub fn Instances::adopt(&self)   // counts the hosts that already run (the controller's `running`) as used just now, so a portal that started again stops them when idle; `Portal::bind` calls it
pub fn Instances::touch(&self, user: &str); pub fn Instances::sweep(&self) -> Vec<String>   // stops each idle host; `max_hosts` counts hosts up or being started, in one count
pub fn dial(path: &Path, uid: u32) -> io::Result<Stream>   // connects and requires the socket's maker to be `uid`; PermissionDenied otherwise
pub enum Subject { Account(String), Certificate(IpAddr) }   // whose failures a try counts toward; never a string, so no name can collide
pub fn Throttle::begin_certificate(&self, address: IpAddr) -> Option<Attempt>   // `begin` for a certificate sign-in
pub fn Throttle::begin(&self, address: IpAddr, user: &str) -> Option<Attempt>   // checks the limits and counts the try as a failure in one step; None is a refusal
pub fn Throttle::succeeded(&self, attempt: Attempt)   // takes the count back
pub fn Instances::acquire(&self, user: &str, uid: u32) -> io::Result<Lease>   // starts the unit if no host answers; PermissionDenied when the socket was made by another user
pub fn Instances::ask(&self, user: &str, uid: u32, request: &str) -> io::Result<serde_json::Value>   // one deadline of 5 s in all

pub fn Portal::bind(settings: Settings, accounts: Arc<dyn Accounts>, controller: Arc<dyn Controller>) -> Result<Portal, PortalError>   // binds `socket` (mode 0660, group `socket_group`) and `listen`
pub fn Portal::local_addr(&self) -> Option<SocketAddr>   // the loopback address taken, when `listen` asked for one
pub fn Portal::stopper(&self) -> Stopper   // Clone; Stopper::stop ends run and every relay within a second
pub fn Portal::run(self)
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum PortalError { Settings(String), Site(String), Listen(io::Error) }
```

- **The Office add-in** (portal settings `office = false`, `office_frames`, `office_handoff_seconds = 60`; `office_frames` is checked as `[host.web] office_frames` is, `Settings::check` naming it; each non-default is logged at `warn`). With `office` the portal answers, under the add-in's own policies (`web::Office`, the same as the standalone server's), `GET /office/taskpane.html`, `dialog.html`, `taskpane.js`, `relation.js`, `dialog.js` and `icon-16|32|64|80.png` to anyone, and `GET /office/app/` (the site's `index.html`, `frame-ancestors 'self'` and the Office origins, no `X-Frame-Options`) to a browser with a session, else `401`; any other `/office/...` path is the site's, behind the sign-in, with today's headers. `GET /office/session` answers JSON, `200 {"signed_in": true, "user"}` or `401 {"signed_in": false}`. `GET /office/signin` (`Sec-Fetch-Site` absent or `same-origin`, else `403`) sets the marker `__Host-canoa-office=1; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Max-Age=600` and answers `303 /portal/login`. While the marker is in a request, every answer drops `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` (a header value that is empty is never sent), and a sign-in that completes (`steps::advance`, any method, any number of steps) opens no session: it keeps a `session::Handoff` (account, uid, method, `password_at`, fingerprint, steps, the time of its code) in `Handoffs` (memory only, `office_handoff` long, `max_sessions` in all and `max_sessions_per_user` for one account, the oldest of an account going first), clears the marker and the pending cookie and answers `303 /office/dialog.html#done=STRING` (43 characters of URL-safe base64, only its SHA-256 kept). `POST /office/session` `{"handoff": STRING}` (`from_own_page`, else `403`; no JSON string `400`; an unknown, used or old string `401`, one claim in any case) opens a session with `open_after` from the handoff, runs `resume` (the account's standing and the sign-in policy again, from this request's address) and answers `200 {"signed_in": true, "user"}` with `__Host-canoa-pane=TOKEN; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=None; Partitioned; Max-Age=SESSION`. `Request::token` is `__Host-canoa`, else `__Host-canoa-pane`; `POST /portal/logout` ends both sessions and clears both cookies. Nothing else about a session changes: a `POST` and an upgrade still check `Origin` and `Sec-Fetch-Site`, `/canoa.json` still refuses a request that crossed sites.
- Requests: `GET|POST /portal/login` (form `method` = `password` with `user` and `password`, or `certificate`; a method the policy does not offer this connection is `403` before the throttle and before any provider, a method word it does not know `400`), `GET /portal/methods` (no cookie; `{"methods": [...]}`; `certificate` only on `socket`), `GET /portal/login.css` and `/portal/login.js`, `GET|POST /portal/certificate` (signed in; `action` = `enrol` or `remove`; enrolling needs a certificate on `socket` and the password: the session's own, when a password signed it in within `enrol` with this same certificate, else typed again in the form through the throttle; refusals are `403`; a pin or removal is logged at `warn` with the fingerprint and address, and ends the account's certificate sessions that used the old pin), `POST /portal/logout`, `GET /portal/session`, `GET /canoa.json`, `POST /portal/pair`, `GET /portal/ws/entry/NAME` and `/portal/ws/control` (WebSocket), and any other `GET` or `HEAD` is the site, to a signed-in browser (else `303 /portal/login`), except `/manifest.json`, `/favicon.ico` and `/icons/NAME`, which anyone is given (a browser fetches the manifest with no cookie; they hold nothing private). Every request carries one `Host` that is `localhost`, an address or one of `hosts` (else 421). A `POST` and a WebSocket upgrade need an `Origin` equal to `Host` and no `Sec-Fetch-Site` but `same-origin` (else 403). A body is at most 4096 bytes. HTTP/1.1, one thread a connection. It listens on `socket`, a Unix socket only the proxy's group can reach, and optionally a loopback `listen` for development (beyond loopback is refused: the cookie is `Secure`, so a proxy speaks TLS). `GET /canoa.json` starts a host, so a browser that says `Sec-Fetch-Site` is not `same-origin` or `none` is refused (403). An `Origin` is the page's own `scheme://host`: `https` when the proxy (on `socket`) says `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` or the host is not `localhost` or an address, else `http`; `null` is never one. The sign-in page sends `Referrer-Policy: same-origin`, every other answer `no-referrer`. The sign-in and certificate pages have the policy `default-src 'none'; style-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'`, no inline script or style, and are server-rendered from the same method list `/portal/methods` answers; `login.js` only counts a `429`'s `Retry-After` down. The sign-in policy decides which methods a request may use (below). A certificate sign-in whose request carries no certificate header is `401` with its own notice, is not counted by the throttle and asks no provider; an expired certificate and one pinned to no one are `401` with their own notices, counted, naming no account, and the log says which of the three (never the certificate). A password sign-in on `socket` that carries a certificate pinned to no one is redirected to `/portal/certificate`.
- **Sign-in.** `Throttle::begin` is asked before the accounts, in one step with counting the try: an address (IPv6 by its /64) with `max_failures_per_pair` failures for this account, or `max_failures_per_address` for any, in `failure_window` is `429` and PAM is not called; an account with more than `slow_after_user_failures` failures from anywhere makes the try wait `slow` per failure over, up to 30 times, and is never shut out. A wrong password, an unknown name, an account the provider does not admit and root or a user id below `min_uid` are all `401`; accounts that cannot be asked are `503`, and count as a failure; only a success (or a try never made, a busy portal) takes its count back. Every failed sign-in, `429` included, is answered no sooner than `failure_floor` after the request began. The session is a random 256-bit token; the cookie is `__Host-canoa=TOKEN; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Max-Age=session_seconds`; only the SHA-256 of the token is kept; an account holds `max_sessions_per_user`, the oldest ending when another comes. A cookie named twice is no session. The address counted is the TCP peer's on `listen`, which never believes a header; on `socket` it is the last address in `client_address_header` that is not in `trusted_proxies`, or one shared address when there is none.
- **Sign-in policy.** `sign_in_policy` (an `Entry`; `provider` defaults to `coqui_signin_rules`, built in, any other from `library`; `enrol` is not read) is a coqui-access `coqui_sign_in_policy` provider, built when the portal binds (a provider that is missing, does not load or refuses its configuration is `PortalError::Settings`); with none, `SignInRules::single_factor` over the methods `Accounts::methods` lists. It is asked, with `service` `portal`, `client` `browser` and the time: (1) for the page and `GET /portal/methods`, `purpose` `sign_in`, no person, no steps, the address the proxy reported or `None` (never `0.0.0.0`: that stays the throttle's key for an unknown address) - the offered methods are the policy's, in its order, that this portal has (`Method::from_id`; another word is ignored) and, for `certificate`, only on `socket`; (2) for a `POST /portal/login` before the throttle and before any provider: the method not offered is `403` (a word that is not a method of the portal's is `400`), and only the `providers` of its `Offer` are asked, in its order (`Accounts::sign_in*` take them; a name no provider has asks nobody); (3) once a provider accepts, with the account (`Person { id: uid as text, name, groups }`, the groups the system directory gives) and `done` = the one step (`Factor { method, provider, person }`): `complete` opens the session, which keeps `done`; anything else, and an error from the policy (logged), is not a success - the try stays counted, and the answer is `401` and the floor, so a policy that cannot decide reads as one that decides against; (4) `purpose` `resume` in `resume`, which `host_for` (`GET /canoa.json`, `POST /portal/pair`, each WebSocket upgrade) and `GET /portal/session` and `GET|POST /portal/certificate` all go through, with the session's steps, the address now and the groups `Accounts::still` returns: not `complete` closes the session (`401`; `303` to the sign-in page for the certificate page), an error is `503`, and so is a directory that cannot say the groups (the session stands). The address is compared to `trusted_proxies` as an IPv4 address when it is IPv4-mapped. The log says at bind which policy is in force. A password retyped at `/portal/certificate` is first checked for `password` among the policy's offers for the client. The log names the method, the address and the rule, never a name typed. `coqui_access_proxy` in `authenticators` stops `Access::new`. A provider that answers by `claims` goes to the matcher (`matcher`: a list of `MatcherRule { by, template, domain, pins }`, coqui-access's `MatchRule`; none is its defaults, the UPN as a name then the email; it asks the operating system's accounts only): exactly one account is a match and is then admitted by `Access::admit` under `min_uid` and `groups`; none or several is `SignIn::Refused` (logged without the claims); a directory error is `SignIn::Failed`. `local` without `domain` makes `Access::new` fail; a value with more than one `@` makes no candidate for a rule that strips or takes the realm (the matcher's own rule), and a match whose `Person::id` is not the admitted account's numeric user id is refused. Only a certificate on `socket` is ever asked of a provider, so none reaches the matcher from `listen`.
- **Second step.** A valid step the policy finds not enough, with methods still offered, opens a pending sign-in (`session::Pendings`, memory only; the cookie `__Host-canoa-pending=TOKEN; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict`, only the SHA-256 kept): the account, the steps passed, a count of tries, the first method, when a password was typed and the certificate its request carried. `pending_seconds` (300) and `max_pending_tries` (5, right or wrong; the try after the last ends it unchecked) bound it; `POST /portal/login` with `method=cancel` ends it. While it lives, `GET /portal/login` and `GET /portal/methods` (`{"step":"next","methods":[...]}`) answer from the policy's offer for its steps and the account's groups now, a method not offered is `403` with nothing asked or counted, and a code (`method=totp|recovery_code`, field `code`) is checked for the pending account only (`Accounts::check_code`): the browser never names whose it is. A password or certificate step must name the pending account, else it fails and counts. Each try passes `guard_try`: the pending try count, `Throttle::begin` under the account's lower-case name (the pair and address limits, `429`), then `Throttle::begin_person` (`max_factor_failures_per_person`, default 5, by user id, from any address, sliding over `failure_window`, and `max_factor_failures_per_day`, default 20 over `factor_day_seconds`; `begin_person` says which limit; a refused try is not counted for the person but stays counted for its address, `401`, and is logged: at `warn` with the account for the first in a window, at `info` after). A right step takes its counts back. When the offer after a step includes `certificate` and the request is on `socket` and carries one that `certificate_status` says is `PinState::Yours` for the pending account, it is tried in the same request; any other certificate is not tried (it would count a failure against someone who only sent a password). The session opened records every step; its `method` is `password` when one was typed (with `password_at` and the fingerprint of that request's certificate), else the first, and `close_certificates` ends sessions that have a certificate step. `resume` ends a session whose `done` holds `totp` or `recovery_code` when `factor_standing` is no longer `Enrolled` (disabled, reset, none), or when the record's made time is not the value the session recorded (`Session::code_made`, taken when it opened: a code set up again), and answers `503` when the codes cannot be read; a channel's liveness check does the same every `CODE_LOOK` (3 s) for such a session and keeps the channel when the read fails. A code record is keyed `UID:NAME` (`code_key`), so a user id made again does not find the earlier account's code; `reset_factor` writes the account's mark in `<source>.reset` (one key a line, `0600`, under the flock of `<source>.reset.lock`) before it removes the code, and `FactorStanding::Reset` (a mark, or a record or mark of the same user id under another name) blocks every self-setup until `canoa portal factor enrol NAME --code` (`Access::clear_reset`, which also lifts the marks of that user id under other names) lifts it; `Access::unlock` writes `KEY MILLIS` to `<source>.unlock` and ends the provider's own delay, and the portal's `guard_try` clears the account's counts (`Throttle::clear_person`) when it sees a later time than it acted on; `check_code_files_owned(settings)` runs first in that command, over the files the code provider's entry names (`source`, `pending`, `waiting`; found by `totp` in its id or library file name) and their lock, reset and unlock files and folders, no other provider's; `Access::unlock` passes the record's current `disabled` back, so an administrator's disable stands.
- **`/portal/factor`.** `GET` shows whether the account has a code and what to do; `POST` (own page only) `action` is `step` (a signed-in person shows a factor the policy's `enrol` decision asks again, `method` and its fields; the steps are kept in a pending state of purpose `enrol` bound to that session, `enrol_seconds` long), `begin` (only when `enrol` is `complete` for the steps shown, and, for an account that already has a code, only when those steps include its `totp` or a `recovery_code`: a rule for `enrol` that asks for a password alone does not replace a code; for a sign-in's pending state the purpose is `enrol_first`, which only a rule that names it allows, and also only when the account has no code, is not `Reset` and the sign-in offers `totp`; logged at `warn` there; answers the link, a QR code as inline SVG and the recovery codes, once), `confirm` (`code`; passes `guard_try`; confirms the waiting setup, which in a sign-in is also its `totp` step) or `cancel`. No cookie is `401`; with no `enrol` rule nobody gets past `Nobody`. A portal with no code provider answers `403` to every action.
- **`canoa portal [--config FILE] factor list | enrol NAME [--code CODE] | reset NAME | reset --id UID:NAME | unlock NAME`**, run as the portal's account: `list` prints a JSON line per account (`id`, `user`, `enrolled`, `disabled`, `waiting`, `reset`), `enrol` without `--code` prints `{"enrol": {user, uri, recovery_codes}}` once and with it confirms the setup waiting, `reset` prints `{"reset": {account, had_one}}`; status 2 for a portal with no code provider or an account it does not sign in, 1 for a failure.
- **Channels.** The socket's path is `socket_dir/USER/entries/NAME.sock` or `socket_dir/USER/control.sock`, made from the session's account name and a `NAME` of letters, digits, `_` and `-` checked here, and nothing else the browser sends. Messages are relayed between the WebSocket (binary only; text ends the channel) and the socket's length-prefixed frames, at most `max_message` bytes each. A channel ends when either side does, when its session ends (sign-out or expiry, checked twice a second) or when the portal stops. A user may hold `max_relays_per_user` open. A write that makes no progress for 30 s ends the channel, and the socket is shut before the writer's lock is wanted.
- **Hosts.** `Accounts::still` is asked at each use: an account that is gone loses its sessions (401). `acquire` asks the introduction socket and, when it does not answer, starts the unit and asks again for `start_seconds`, never above `max_hosts` running. Each user has a state of their own, a lock held across only that user's own start or stop; `ask` has one 5 s deadline for the whole answer (at most 64 KiB). A host with no channel and no use for `idle` is stopped (checked every tenth of that, between 0.2 s and 30 s); one with a channel open never is. `Systemd` runs `[elevate] systemctl start|stop --no-ask-password UNIT`, which may be `dist/canoa-instance`, a root helper that takes only those words, a `NAME_PATTERN` name and an account in `ALLOWED_GROUPS` with a user id of 1000 or more outside the system ranges (it sets its own `PATH` and `LC_ALL`). `POST /portal/pair` `{"peer"}` asks the host to accept that key every 250 ms for `pair_seconds`, `200` once it is, `404` when it never connected.

```rust
pub struct PortalSockets { pub dir: PathBuf, pub uid: u32 }   // canoa_host::portal_link, non_exhaustive; Clone, Eq
pub fn check_account(dir: &Path, min_uid: u32, groups: &[String]) -> Result<(), String>   // what `serve --portal` runs first: not root, not `system_uid`, not below `min_uid`, in one of `groups` (at least one must be named; read with `id -Gn`), `USER` the last part of `dir`, `dir` owned by this account
pub const fn system_uid(uid: u32) -> bool   // 0, 61184 to 65535 (systemd's dynamic users, `nobody`) and u32::MAX
pub fn PortalSockets::new(dir: impl Into<PathBuf>, uid: u32) -> PortalSockets
pub fn uid_of(user: &str) -> io::Result<u32>   // a number, or a name in /etc/passwd
pub fn entry_socket(dir: &Path, entry: &str) -> PathBuf
pub const ENTRIES: &str = "entries"; pub const CONTROL: &str = "control.sock"; pub const INTRODUCTION: &str = "portal.sock";
pub fn ServeOptions::with_portal(self, sockets: PortalSockets) -> ServeOptions
```

- **`serve --portal DIR --portal-user USER`** (`ServeOptions::with_portal`): `check_account` first refuses to run as root, in `system_uid` ranges, below `--portal-min-uid` (1000), as an account in none of the `--portal-group` groups (one at least is required), as a name (`USER`) that is not the last part of `DIR`, or with a `DIR` this account does not own (status 2); then, as with `--allow-empty`, a canoa.cfg that does not exist yet reads as empty (the person's own file is where settings are written, and the control channel creates it on the first one); `DIR/entries` is made (`0755`); every entry that is not a host source, and the control channel (made if canoa.cfg has none, with canoa.cfg's limits when it has), listen on a Unix socket there, mode `0666`, replacing any `listen` canoa.cfg gives; `[host.web]` and `--web` are ignored. Every connection is checked with the system's own word for who is at the other end (`SO_PEERCRED`): one whose user is not `USER` is closed before a byte is read and logged at `warn`. A paired key is the person's own browser, so on the control channel it holds `Grant::all()` with no entry under `grants`; an entry's `peers`, when it lists keys, still applies. `own_profile` still needs `peers` (config refuses it without), so an instance that offers it lists the keys it knows; the portal's introduced keys are not known in advance.
- **The introduction socket**, `DIR/portal.sock`, takes one line of JSON a connection from `USER` only: `{"op":"index"}` answers `{"peer", "method", "os", "entries": [{"name", "kind", "harness"?, "title"?}]}` with no `url` (the control channel is there, `kind` `control`); `{"op":"accept","peer"}` is `peers::accept` (only a key that connected and waits) and answers `{"ok":true,"peer"}` or `{"error"}`; `{"op":"revoke","peer"}` answers `{"ok": bool}`; any other request `{"error"}`. A request is at most 4096 bytes and 5 s.
- The sockets are `coqui-transport-pipe` listeners at mode `0666` with no group, so the pipe crate's own checks stand: a parent that others can write, or that another user owns, is refused. The unit makes `DIR` as root, owned by the person, under a `/run/canoa` only root writes.

## One host on a home (`canoa_host::home`)

```rust
pub fn Home::at(dir: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> Home
pub fn Home::of_user() -> Result<Home, coqui_link::Error>   // COQUI_AGENTIC_PEER_HOME, else ~/.canoa (%USERPROFILE%\.canoa on Windows)
pub fn Home::dir(&self) -> &Path
pub fn Home::identity(&self, method: Option<Method>) -> Result<Identity, coqui_link::Error>   // as coqui_agentic_peer::home, in this directory
pub fn Home::store(&self) -> Result<TrustStore, coqui_link::Error>
pub fn Home::lock(&self, name: &str) -> Result<HomeLock, LockError>
pub fn Home::holder(&self) -> Option<Holder>   // by what it wrote; proves nothing about the lock
#[non_exhaustive] pub struct Holder { pub name: String, pub pid: u32 }
#[non_exhaustive] pub enum LockError { Held { dir: PathBuf, holder: Option<Holder> }, Io { dir: PathBuf, source: io::Error } }
```

- `lock` takes the operating system's lock, made with its parents when absent: on Unix on the directory itself, on Windows on `host.lock` in it, held open so that nobody can delete or rename it meanwhile; removing a file cannot let a second host in. It writes `{"pid", "name"}` to `host.holder` through a file of a name nobody could know in advance, made only where nothing is. So a holder that dies gives it up however it died, and a process id used again proves nothing. `Held` says who, or `None` when the holder has not said within half a second. Two locks in one process conflict too.
- Dropping a `HomeLock` removes `host.holder` and gives the lock up; `host.lock`, on Windows, stays.
- The lock is only as good as the filesystem's: a home on WSL's `/mnt/c` (9p) is not locked against a host on Windows, and a network filesystem is untested.
- What `Display` says of `Held` names the holder, its process and the home. `canoa serve` and the app show it.
- A file system that has no such lock is `Io`: that host does not start.

## Delegation (`[host.delegate]`)

An agent session hands a task to another source. Each task is an A2A task (`coqui-a2a`, `tasks` feature) of the host's one delegation agent, run as a normal host session of that source opened in process with `Local`, whose only prompt is the task text.

