  agy: SHA-512 from the manifest, only the newest build (a pinned version is `UNSUPPORTED`). Pi: Node's standalone build (SHASUMS256.txt, its signature is not
  checked) then that Node's npm with `--ignore-scripts`; npm fetches the packages itself, so the host does not see those bytes.
- **Pi's launch** is a Node and a script, not one file: `Launch.path` is the absolute Node, `Launch.settings` holds `node` (the same path) and
  `script` (the absolute path of Pi's `dist/bundle/cli.js`), and `Launch.environment` sets `PI_SKIP_VERSION_CHECK=1`. A provider that serves
  Pi takes `node` and `script` as settings and starts the Node with the script, never a `.cmd` shim.
- **Known gaps, stated in each plan's notices:** Claude's manifest signature (`manifest.json.sig`, key published at downloads.claude.ai/keys) and Node's
  `SHASUMS256.txt` signature are not checked, only the digests over TLS; a digest fetched from the origin that serves the file does not
  catch a compromised publisher. npm's own downloads for Pi are not seen by the host.
- `contract::run(store, fixture)`: the suite (`describe.*`, `program.*`, `latest.*`, `plan.*`, `apply.*`, `uninstall.*`), strict and offline, on a `World` of
  documents and a stand-in program built by `cargo test` (`examples/version_standin.rs`, with a trailer appended per version). `echo` is the
  good example; `tests/store_suite.rs` has stores that break one rule each. The registry keys are the real ones and are restored.
- A store reaches a harness only through the host: `Launch.settings` (`{"executable": path}`) merged under an entry's own, and `Launch.environment`
  added to its requests; only the built-in Pi source reads a managed copy so far (its `node` and `script`; "The host's own sources"), and wiring it into `[host.agents]` is not here yet.

**What the host holds a store to, and what it does not (a security review, 2026-11)**

- A store is code running as the user. The host's checks catch a store's mistakes and an install's side effects; they are not a sandbox.
- The host applies the plan it made, found by its token (`Programs::plan` keeps it); a client's copy only names it. The token covers the
  version, platform, launch, every download and its check, and every declared change. A change that declares a place of its own is the only
  kind accepted: a root, the home itself or an ancestor of it, all of HKCU, is refused (`the plan declares ...`). `USER_PATH` covers
  the registry `PATH` value gaining exactly the declared directory at its end, and a profile whose only change is a line that is exactly a `PATH`
  assignment adding it (no whitespace or `\` in a POSIX directory, quotes balanced); a `REGISTRY` change never covers the user `PATH`.
- An install is measured on every outcome, a failure, a cancel and a timeout included: the machine is snapshotted before and after, the
  registry values put back, and the folder removed. Nothing is exempted for moving: a profile line, a `PATH` directory or a `Run` key that
  changes is undeclared, and the error asks the person to try again. During an install the other programs' folders under the programs root are watched too.
- The programs root is `Shelf::default_root()` or below (`Shelf::configured` refuses another) until an access-list check exists.
- Downloads nothing checks (Grok's) are refused unless `Options::allow_unverified`; `current.json` records each file's `verified`. Endpoints may be a prefix
  (`https://storage.googleapis.com/antigravity-public/antigravity-cli/`), which a URL and every redirect has to start with.
- `Programs::launch` reads `current.json` against a digest the host keeps in `<programs>/.digests/`, outside the program's folder, refuses a launch
  outside the copy or a copy outside the shelf, and checks every file of the copy (the files an archive unpacked too) against what was recorded
  at install: a changed, missing or added file refuses. Records survive a rollback. A person with write access to the programs folder can rewrite
  both `current.json` and the digest: the check is against a stray edit, not against the owner's own account.
- A plan is kept by the host per (provider, token) for `Options::plan_ttl` (15 minutes) and `plan_limit` (32), and applied once. The token covers the
  action and the version being replaced. A plan with a registry key, a state folder or a profile file is checked by name: the home's sensitive
  trees and their ancestors are refused, a state folder must be named for the program (or under such a folder), a registry key must be the user's
  `Path` or the program's own `Software\<vendor>\<program>` or `Uninstall` entry. `USER_PATH` names the directory it adds; it covers a profile line
  that is exactly `export PATH=<dir>:$PATH`, `fish_add_path <dir>` or a PowerShell `$env:Path = "<dir>;$env:Path"`, or the registry value gaining
  exactly that directory at its end. An endpoint path with a dot segment or an encoded slash (after one decoding) is refused.
  `Shelf::configured` resolves `..` before it checks the root is the default's; `Shelf::unchecked` is for tests.
- A launch is recorded only when its variables are not `PATH`, `NODE_*`, `LD_*`, `DYLD_*` or credentials, and its `executable`, `node` and `script` settings lie in the version's folder.
- An install is given an environment without the person's `npm_config_*`, `NODE_*` and `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`; Pi's npm is told its registry. Pi's packages
  are not pinned by a shrinkwrap yet (`npm ci` from a per-version lockfile is a later step).
- `Programs::report(probe)` runs the host's own copy only, and of the outside ones the one the person picked (`pick`).
- A downgrade (an update to an older version) is planned with a notice that says so.
- Install, update and remove are for the person who owns the host: nothing here serves them to a paired peer, and a later phase that does has to ask the person.
- Not checked yet, by decision: Claude's `manifest.json.sig` and Node's `SHASUMS256.txt.sig`. A pure-Rust OpenPGP verifier (`pgp`, rpgp) is the way; it adds
  its RustCrypto tree to this crate and the pinned keys (Anthropic's signing key, and the Node release key of the pinned Node) to the source.

Tests: `cargo build -p canoa-host --examples` first, then `cargo test -p canoa-host --all-features --test store_tools --test store_shelf
--test store_programs --test store_suite --test store_extensions`. `tests/store_live.rs` (ignored) installs the real programs:
`CANOA_STORE_LIVE=claude,codex,grok,agy,pi cargo test -p canoa-host --test store_live -- --ignored --nocapture`.

## The knowledge tools (`[host.kb]`, `[overlays.NAME] kb`)

```rust
pub const KB_META: &str = "dev.boriken.canoa/kb";   // the `_meta` key of `initialize` that asks for the tools
```

**Tools a program offers** (`client_tools`, `@tools`). A program on this computer, such as the browser extension (or an Office pane, through the web server's `/office/tools/` routes under `office_local`), opens `@tools` on a proven link and registers a name (`{"type":"register","provider":"browser"}`, a lowercase word of at most 32). From then on every session the host starts for any connection (every key that reaches the host is the person's own; a task another agent handed out is the one exception) is given one MCP server for that provider, `canoa-<name>` (`canoa-browser`), the way `canoa-kb` is given: over loopback HTTP behind a bearer minted for that session, or for an agent that takes only stdio through `PROGRAM mcp-bridge`; no `_meta` is needed from the client. The host opens a stream on the provider's link for each session and forwards that session's MCP requests (`tools/list`, `tools/call`) on it, so the provider's grants and windows belong to that session alone and end with it (its connection, a `session/close`, or the provider's link). `initialize` and `ping` are answered by the host, which says the tool list may change. A `GET /mcp` with the bearer is the session's stream of server messages (`text/event-stream`), where the provider's `notifications/...` and the host's own `notifications/tools/list_changed` (a provider connected or left) reach an agent that takes HTTP servers; a stdio agent hears none and lists again when it chooses. Wire, one JSON object a message. Provider to host: `register`, `{"type":"mcp","stream":S,"message":M}` (an answer, or a `notifications/` message; a message for a stream this connection was not given is ignored) and `{"type":"close","stream":S}`. Host to provider: `registered` or `{"type":"refused","why"}`, `{"type":"open","stream":S,"session":{"agent":ENTRY,"via":"app"|"paired","peer"?}}` (what the extension's window tells the person: the entry the session runs on and whether it came from the app in this process or from a paired key), `{"type":"mcp","stream":S,"message":M}` and `{"type":"close","stream":S}`. A session gets servers for the providers connected when it starts and for those seen since the host started, so one outlives a browser restart: with no provider connected `tools/list` is empty and a call says the provider is not connected, and a provider that returns is opened a new stream, with none of the old grants. A name held by one key is refused to another; the same key replaces its own connection. A session request a web page made (`_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/site"]` naming a site and not `own`) is given none: the connection's raw messages are read before the scope removes that mark (`ToolsPeek`), and `ToolsLayer` adds the servers after the scope. An answer waits up to 190 seconds for the provider, which may ask the person. Limits: 256 sessions a connection, 1024 streams and 16 waiting calls a stream, messages of at most 4 MiB, 16 provider names.

An agent can search and read the knowledge of the overlays its session has through an MCP server the host adds to its sessions, `canoa-kb`, offered the way `canoa-store` is: the client sets `_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/kb"] = true` in `initialize`, and each session request gets one server over loopback HTTP behind a bearer of that session (or, for an agent that takes only stdio, through `PROGRAM mcp-bridge`), which ends with the connection. A session that gets it also gets a built-in skill, `canoa-kb`, that says when to search. A session sees the overlays its connection applies (what its loadout gave it) and nothing else. The tools only read.

The tools, their arguments, answers, error classes, templates and limits are the Agent Overlay Specification's (`spec/v0/knowledge.md`) and coqui-aos's `KnowledgeSession` answers them; see its `rust/AGENTS.md`. The host adds:

- **Tools.** `kb_list`, `kb_read {path, from?, to?, overlay?, context?}` and `kb_search {query, top?, overlay?, mode?, path?}`; `kb_search` is offered because the host always has `words`. `mode` is `auto`, or one the host declares in `kb_list`: `words`, `meaning` (with `embeddings`) and `llm` (with `llm_endpoint`). A call waits up to 20 seconds for an index still being made and then says so (`unavailable`).
- **Who is asking.** A template of a knowledge file is rendered with the connection's profile (`AOS_CALLER_PROFILE`), its loadout as the role (`AOS_CALLER_ROLE`), the session's own variables (`AOS_ENV_NAME`; AOS never gives a variable that changes how programs are found), the overlay's name and the person's option values, and the call's `context`. The searchable text is the served text with no caller.
- **Where the indexes are.** `<state>/kb/<overlay>/<tree digest>/<words | m-MODEL>/` (`coqui_aos::index`), made in the background the first time a version is asked for and shared by every connection that applies it. A new version starts from the last one's index and makes only the files that changed again; the versions before it are then removed. A change to `[host.kb]`'s sizes or model remakes the index. With no state directory the tools are off.
- **Meaning needs a server the person names.** With no `embeddings` an index holds words only (BM25). `embeddings` is the address of an OpenAI-compatible server (`http://127.0.0.1:60570`, with or without `/v1`; any address, Canoa chooses none), `model` the exact id it loaded (required with it), `api_key_env` the name of a variable holding a key sent as a bearer, `document_prefix` and `query_prefix` for models that ask for them, `chunk_bytes` (default 1024) and `overlap_bytes` (default 0). If the server cannot be reached when an index is made, that overlay is searched by words and says why in a `note`; if it is down when a question is asked, an `auto` search is by words and says so.
- **The `llm` mode.** `llm_endpoint` (an OpenAI-compatible server, with or without `/v1`), `llm_model` (the exact id, required with it), `llm_api_key_env`, `llm_candidates` (default 10, 1 to 100) and `llm_timeout_secs` (default 30). The search finds candidates by `auto` (meaning where there is an index of it, else words) and the model orders them: `POST {endpoint}/v1/rank` with `{model, query, answers}` where the server has it (an answer of 400, 404, 405 or 422 turns it off for the process), else a chat completion at temperature 0 whose reply is read only as a list of passage numbers. Each passage is cut to 600 characters. An entry says `"mode": "llm"` and, in `note`, what found the candidates; scores are the model's order, 1 down. A named `llm` the model cannot answer is `mode-unavailable` (a named mode is never answered by another); `auto` is answered by `llm` when the person configured it and by the mode that found the candidates, with the reason in `note`, when the model fails or is too slow. The passages and the query go to that server: a person who names one that is not their own machine sends their knowledge there.
- **Served through the tools.** `[overlays.NAME] kb = "index"` leaves that overlay's declared rules and context files out of the agent's context and puts one line in their place saying to search them with `kb_search`; `"files"` keeps them whole; with no key the overlay's own `[knowledge] serve` hint decides (`coqui_aos::knowledge_plan`). It applies only to a connection that was given the tools (a client that did not ask keeps the whole files). `HostProfile::kb_choice` holds the person's keys.
- **What an agent reads is data.** coqui-aos writes control, direction, zero-width and other hiding characters in passages, headings, paths and errors as `<U+XXXX>`.

## The mail tools (`[host.mail]`)

```rust
pub struct MailConfig { pub tools: Option<bool>, pub accounts: IndexMap<String, MailAccount> }   // [host.mail]
pub enum MailBackend { Ews, Graph, Outlook }    // #[non_exhaustive]
pub enum MailAuth { Basic, Windows }     // #[non_exhaustive]
```

An agent can use the person's own mail as a source through an MCP server the host adds to its sessions, `canoa-mail`, with a built-in skill of the same name. It is an extra server of the store's link (`store::mcp::Extra`): the same loopback server, bearer per session and stdio bridge as `canoa-store`, and the same way to ask the person. Unlike `canoa-store` it needs no `_meta`: every session of a connection whose key holds the **`mail`** grant gets it, when `[host.mail.accounts]` names an account and `[host.mail] tools` is not false. The application in this process holds `mail`; a paired key only if `[host.control] grants` or `[host.native] control` lists it (it is in no default); the key of the Office pane's page that the host's own web server admitted (`[host.web] office_local`) holds it whenever the file names a mail account and `[host.mail] tools` is not false, so the pane's chat has the tools with no step; a host run for a portal gives it to no key; a task another agent handed out gets none.

- **Accounts.** `[host.mail.accounts.NAME]` (name: 1 to 32 lower-case letters, digits, `-`, `_`): `backend = "ews" | "graph" | "outlook"`, `timeout_secs` (1 to 3600, default 60). `ews`: `server` (the EWS address) or `email` (Autodiscover asks `autodiscover` if given, else `https://autodiscover.DOMAIN/autodiscover/autodiscover.xml` and `https://DOMAIN/autodiscover/autodiscover.xml`, following up to three redirects, and keeps what it found), `auth = "basic" | "windows"` (basic when `user` is given), `user`, then the password: `id` (the entry of the host's account store that holds it, which `canoa mail add` writes; the record's one credential is `password`) or `password_env` (the variable that holds it, read when the account is first used, and used before the stored one when both are given). Basic needs `user` and one of them; `windows` and `outlook` take neither. canoa.cfg holds no password and a `password` key is refused. `canoa mail list|add|remove` (`mail/commands.rs`) change it: `add NAME --backend ews|outlook [--email --server --autodiscover --user --profile --timeout-secs]` checks canoa.cfg would still be read with the table in it, asks for the password through the front (no echo at a terminal, a line from standard input otherwise; an empty one is refused), keeps it under a new id, writes the table, and forgets the password again if the file cannot be written; `list` prints name, backend, server or email and a state (`password stored`, `needs a password`, `password from VAR`, `needs VAR set`, `signs in with the Windows logon`, `signed in by Outlook`), `--format json` the same as objects; `remove` takes the table out and forgets the stored password. The store is the one `[host] account_store` names; a host with no home reads no stored password. An `http` address is accepted only for this computer. `outlook`: `profile`, `command` (the program and arguments that run the helper; it is given the helper script on standard input). The backend is made the first time a tool needs it, so a password set after the host started is used and a server that is down does not stop the host.
- **Tools.** `mail_list {account?}` (accounts, folders with counts), `mail_search {query?, from?, to?, subject?, folder?, after?, before?, has_attachment?, unread?, flagged?, limit?, offset?, account?}` (default 25, at most 100; with no account every account is searched and one that fails is reported in `errors`), `mail_read {id | message_id | item_id, account?, thread?, max_chars?}`, `mail_related {id | message_id | item_id, account?, limit?}` (each result has `related_by`: `conversation`, `subject`, `person`), `mail_draft {to, cc?, bcc?, subject?, body, account?}` or `{reply_to, reply_all?, body}`, `mail_move {ids, folder}`, `mail_archive {ids}`, `mail_update {ids, read?, flag?, add_categories?, remove_categories?}`. A message id is `ACCOUNT:` and the backend's own id; one change names at most 50 ids of one account. A message a mail program is showing is named without an id from these tools: exactly one of `id`, `message_id` (its Internet message id, `<abc@example.com>`; the server compares it whole, so the brackets are tried both ways) and `item_id` (the server's own item id, an Exchange id in its EWS form) is given, `message_id` and `item_id` are looked for in every account in the file's order, or only in `account`, and the first account that holds it answers (an account that cannot be searched is named in the error when none does); the answer carries the usual `ACCOUNT:` id for every other tool. `Backend::find_by_message_id` is one `FindItem` with a `message:InternetMessageId` restriction over the whole mailbox (Exchange Server), `$filter=internetMessageId eq '...'` on `/me/messages` (Graph) and a DASL restriction on `PR_INTERNET_MESSAGE_ID` over every mail folder of every store (classic Outlook). Results are JSON text with a `note`; an error has `isError`. The shapes follow `kb_*` where they fit: a list of results with ids to read, never the body, and a read by id.
- **Never sends.** There is no tool or backend call that sends. A draft is saved in Drafts (`MessageDisposition="SaveOnly"`; Outlook's `Save`), and the answer says `"sent": false`.
- **Asked first.** `mail_move`, `mail_archive` and `mail_update` read the named messages from the mailbox, put them and the change to the person as a `session/request_permission` (the card's text is in the call's `content` and, for a client that draws the input under the question, in `rawInput.change`) on the client the session belongs to, and change nothing until the answer comes back from that client. A session the person put in a mode that asks for nothing is not asked and says so in the conversation (`Access::approve`). One change per session waits at a time.
- **Mail is data.** Every string of a message is stripped of control, direction and zero-width characters and cut (subject and names 300 characters, body `max_chars`, default 20000, at most 200000, newlines kept); every answer carries the note and the skill says the same.
- **Related mail** (`mail/related.rs`) is built on a backend's `search` and `thread` alone: the conversation (the backend's own, or the subject's messages with the same conversation id), the same subject without `Re:`/`Fwd:` prefixes, and mail from the sender and up to three other people on the message; conversation first, then subject, then people, newest first inside each.
- **Exchange Server (`mail/ews/`).** SOAP over `Network::current().agent`, so the system's proxy and certificates apply. `FindFolder` (deep from `msgfolderroot`), `FindItem` with an AQS query string (`from:"x" AND received:>=2026-01-01 AND hasattachments:true AND isread:false AND (free text)`, every value quoted; `Deep` over `msgfolderroot` for no folder, `Shallow` for one named, paged by `IndexedPageItemView`, newest first; `flagged` is filtered after the fetch), `GetItem` (text body), `CreateItem` (`SaveOnly` into `drafts`, a reply by `ReplyToItem`/`ReplyAllToItem`), `MoveItem`, `UpdateItem` (`message:IsRead`, `item:Flag`, `item:Categories`; add/remove read the categories first). Archive is the well-known `archive` folder, else a folder named `Archive`. A folder is a well-known name (`inbox`, `sent`, `drafts`, `archive`, `deleted`, `junk`), an id from `mail_list`, or a path or name. **Sign-in** goes through `Transport::post`. Basic sends `Authorization` on every request. `auth = "windows"` is `Transport::post_negotiate`: a `Network::login_agent` with a Negotiate rule for the server's host answers the `401` (Kerberos, then NTLM from the logon session; an NTLM exchange has three legs on one connection and the body goes with the last; a fresh agent and so a fresh exchange per call). A server that offers no scheme the logon can answer is left at its `401`, which `post` reports with the schemes offered (`it accepts Basic`); a login that fails is `MailError::Auth` naming the logon. Off Windows the build has no Negotiate (`coqui-syshttp`'s `negotiate` feature is on for Windows only), so the exchange fails there with that error. The tests' stand-in completes the exchange with the system's own security package (`mail/sspi_server.rs`, Windows test code, the one `unsafe` in the tests of this crate).
- **Classic Outlook (`mail/outlook.rs`, `mail/outlook.ps1`).** The helper script is PowerShell for Windows PowerShell 5.1, ASCII only. The host starts `powershell.exe -NoLogo -NoProfile -NonInteractive -Command <bootstrap>` and writes the script, a test's prelude and `Invoke-CanoaMail '<base64 request>'` to its standard input; the bootstrap reads standard input whole and runs it as one string (a multi-line script piped to `-Command -` is run line by line and silently does nothing, and a script file would be blocked by an execution policy a command is not). It answers one JSON object on standard output. Operations: `folders`, `search` (Jet for dates, DASL `@SQL=` for the rest, `Items.Restrict`), `read`, `summaries`, `thread` (`GetConversation`), `draft` (`CreateItem`/`Reply`/`ReplyAll` then `Save`), `change` (`Move`, `UnRead`, `FlagStatus`, `Categories`). Ids are `EntryID|StoreID`. A stand-in for Outlook's object model (`mail/outlook/standin.ps1`, PowerShell classes) runs the real script in the tests; classic Outlook was never run against it.
- **Exchange Online (`mail/graph.rs`, `graph/`).** `backend = "graph"`: `client_id` (required; the Entra app registration, a public client with delegated `Mail.ReadWrite` only), `tenant` (default `organizations`), `email` (the Windows account to ask, as WAM's `login_hint`), `server` (default `https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0`, https or this computer), `command`; no `user`, `password_env`, `auth`, `autodiscover` or `profile`. The token comes from one seam, `TokenSource` (`graph/token.rs`); `Wam` is the real one: it runs `graph/wam.ps1` in Windows PowerShell 5.1 (WinRT is reachable only there) through the same process runner as the Outlook helper (`outlook::PowerShell::with_script`), which asks `WebAuthenticationCoreManager.GetTokenSilentlyAsync` for `https://graph.microsoft.com/Mail.ReadWrite` and the account's client id, never showing a window; a refusal is `MailError::Auth` saying to register an app or use `outlook`. A helper process instead of a crate because WinRT needs a thread initialised through `unsafe` calls and a crate would join the offline mirror. `Tokens` holds a token until five minutes before its `exp` claim (ten minutes' worth when it has none) and asks again once after a 401. Every request has `Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId", outlook.body-content-type="text"`. A search with `text`, `from`, `to` or `subject` is one KQL `$search` (`(words) AND from:"x" AND received>=DAY AND hasattachments:true`; Graph does not combine it with `$filter`, `$orderby` or `$skip`, and gives no count), and `unread`/`flagged` are then applied to the pages returned; any other search is `$filter` (opening with `receivedDateTime ge ...`, since Graph sorts only by a property the filter names first) with `$orderby=receivedDateTime desc` and `$count=true`. Pages are followed through `@odata.nextLink` only at Graph's own origin, until the offset and limit are met. Drafts are `POST /me/messages` (text body) or `createReply`/`createReplyAll` with the text as `comment`; no `send`, `sendMail`, `reply` or `forward` is ever called. `Change::Archive` moves to the well-known `archive`, then to a folder named Archive when Graph says `ErrorFolderNotFound`. Not run against a real tenant: the `$search`/`$filter` limits above, the WAM scope and `wam_compat` property, and the registration's redirect URI are from Microsoft's documentation.
- **Tests.** `cargo test -p canoa-host --lib mail::` (the loopback Exchange stand-in in `mail/standin.rs`, the connector, the Outlook helper under Windows PowerShell where it is found; the loopback Graph stand-in in `mail/graph/standin.rs`, with a fixed token source, and the WAM helper run with Windows' own call replaced by a stand-in) and `store::mcp::extra_tests` (the tools through the store's link, with the approval round trip, and a message named by its Internet message id or item id) and `serve::tests` (`page_grants`: what an admitted page holds).

## Errors

- `ConfigError` (`#[non_exhaustive]`): `Read { path, source }`; `Parse { line, column, what }`, 1-based, `what` in the schema's words; `Invalid { entry, what }`. No variant quotes a value from the file; an entry's name is quoted.
- `ProviderError` (`#[non_exhaustive]`): `Load(String)`, `Source(String)`, `Missing`, `WrongKind`, `NeedsConfiguration`, `TakesNoSettings`, `Settings(String)` (names a setting, never its value, unless the provider's own words do), `HostLine(&'static str)`, `Transport(io::Error)`, `Facade(String)`.
- `PeersError` (`#[non_exhaustive]`): `NotAnId`, `NotWaiting`, `AlreadyAccepted`, `Store(String)`.
- `WebError` (`#[non_exhaustive]`): see "The web app". `source()` is the `io::Error` of `Listen`.

## Command line

```text
canoa serve [--config FILE] [--until-stdin-closes] [--allow-empty] [--local-sources] [--method noise|webcrypto] [--key-limit BYTES] [--no-rekey]
                 [--web [DIR]] [--web-listen ADDRESS] [--portal DIR --portal-user USER --portal-group GROUP... [--portal-min-uid UID]] [--supervised]
canoa portal [--config FILE] [--site DIR] [--listen ADDRESS] [--socket PATH] [factor list | enrol NAME [--code CODE] | reset NAME]      # feature `portal`, Unix
canoa check [--config FILE] [--format text|json]
canoa office-launcher [--config FILE]      # holds the add-in's address while no host runs and starts one when the page asks (see "The add-in on this computer")
canoa office-launcher (--install | --uninstall | --status) [--config FILE] [--format text|json]   # registers it to start at this user's sign-in (see "The add-in on this computer")
canoa office-launcher --detach [--config FILE] [--format text|json]   # starts it again with no console, output to <home>/office/launcher.log, prints its pid and ends; the Windows Run value runs this
canoa layers [--config FILE] [--format text|json]
canoa id [--method noise|webcrypto [--force]] [--identity FILE]
canoa peers [--format text|json]
canoa accept PEER
canoa revoke PEER
canoa pair [--listen ADDR [--origin ORIGIN] | --native] [--timeout-seconds N] [--until-stdin-closes] [--method noise|webcrypto [--force]]
canoa pair --join URL [--pin-file FILE] [--ca-file FILE] [--timeout-seconds N] [--method noise|webcrypto]
canoa serve --program [--identity FILE (--allow PEER... | --allow-store DIR) | --paired] [--listen ADDR] [--tls-identity FILE | --tls-short-lived] [--webtransport-port PORT]
                      [--origin URL]... [--permit NET]... [--deny NET]... [--linger SECONDS ...] [--service TOKEN=PROGRAM]... [--forward] [--mcp-offers] [--advertise LABEL] -- PROGRAM ARGS...
canoa dial URL (--identity FILE (--peer ID... | --trust-store DIR) | --paired | --tls-identity FILE ... | --no-peer-proof) [--fallback URL]... -- PROGRAM ARGS...
canoa forward URL (--paired | --identity FILE --peer ID...) [--local LOCAL=TARGET]... [--allow-target HOST:PORT]...
canoa grant PEER PROTOCOL [--revoke]
canoa discover [--seconds N]
canoa mcp-config --peer PEER [--name NAME]
canoa mcp-shim [--peer PEER]                   # the stdio server an offer or `mcp-config` hands to a client
canoa agent-guard PID                          # hidden: what `serve --program --linger` starts for each agent on Unix
canoa native install [--name NAME] [--extension-id ID]... [--browser BROWSER]... [--profile DIR]... [--launch PROGRAM [-- ARGS...]]
canoa native uninstall|status [--name NAME] [--profile DIR]...
canoa provider (--library FILE | --source FILE [--import-search library|path]) --provider ID [--working-root DIR]... [--max-sessions N]
                    [--directory DIR] [--settings-on-stdin [--connect ADDRESS]]
canoa tool --name NAME --spec JSON
canoa agent ID [--executable FILE] [--setting KEY=VALUE]... COMMON...        # ID: a harness provider the bundled agents offer (claude, codex, ...)
      COMMON: [--model M] [--effort E] [--max-acp-version 1|2] [--max-sessions N] [--working-root DIR]...
              [--no-client-mcp-servers] [--allow-network-paths] [--cache-lifetime default|off|short|long]
canoa launch PROGRAM [ARGS]...
canoa options (claude | codex [--executable FILE] | NAME --entry [--config FILE]) [--format text|json]
canoa accounts list [--config FILE] [--format text|json]
canoa accounts add NAME --harness ID --method METHOD [--input KEY=VALUE]... [--until-stdin-closes] [--config FILE] [--format text|json]
canoa accounts login NAME [--method METHOD] [--input KEY=VALUE]... [--until-stdin-closes] [--config FILE] [--format text|json]
canoa accounts remove NAME [--config FILE]
canoa mail list [--config FILE] [--format text|json]
canoa mail add NAME --backend ews|outlook [--email ADDRESS] [--server URL] [--autodiscover URL] [--user USER] [--profile NAME] [--timeout-secs N] [--config FILE] [--format text|json]
canoa mail remove NAME [--config FILE]
```

- `agent ID` serves the `acp_harness` provider `ID` of the bundled agents' libraries (`claude` and `codex` are two; an id nothing offers is a usage error) as an ACP agent (version 1 or 2, whichever the client asks, up to `--max-acp-version`) on standard input and output, through the same in-process connection a harness entry runs, with no Node; notices are logged at `info` (`CANOA_LOG`), as an entry's are. `--executable` defaults to the variable `COQUI_ACP_` and the id in upper case (`-` as `_`) then `_EXECUTABLE`, else the copy the Store installed, else the agent's own name. `--setting KEY=VALUE` (JSON when it parses as JSON, else text) gives the provider a setting that has no flag of its own (`setting_sources`, `allow_client_plugins`, ...), and the provider refuses a key its schema does not declare; the flags every agent takes the same way (`--model`, `--effort`, `--max-acp-version`, `--max-sessions`, `--no-client-mcp-servers`, the access, `--cache-lifetime`) are given to it as its own settings, where it declares them, and a flag nobody gave leaves the provider's own default. Every mode the agent has is offered, Bypass included, as for an entry. A `--working-root` holds a session's `cwd`, more directories and plugins, and a resumed Codex thread's own directory. It takes the process's standard streams, so `canoa-ffi` runs it only through `--canoa-child`, as `provider`, `tool`, `launch` and `mcp-bridge`. The command line reads its own variables (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `CODEX_HOME`, ...) from the environment it runs in. Exit 0 when the client closes, 1 when reading it failed, 2 for a wrong invocation.
- `options` prints what a source offers before a session starts, asked of the `account_provider` of the entry's account (`models`, the automatic `local` account when none is named) with no session and no model turn (`canoa_host::options`). A bundled agent's id runs as `canoa agent` runs it (`--executable`, else the variable `COQUI_ACP_ID_EXECUTABLE`, else the agent's own name); with `--entry`, `NAME` is a `[host.agents]` entry of canoa.cfg (`--config`): a `harness` entry is asked through its own provider and settings, an entry that runs a program on standard streams is asked through a probe session: it starts as a session would, answers `initialize` and `session/new` in a temporary directory, its model option (category `model`, else the older `models` block) and effort option (category `thought_level`) become the models and their efforts, `session/close` is sent when `sessionCapabilities.close` is offered, and the process ends; no prompt is ever sent, it is bounded by `--wait SECONDS` (default 20, `options::PROBE_WAIT`) and its answer is kept in the process by entry, program, arguments and the program's `--version` line (no version, no cache). A program that cannot be started is `failed`, one that is not there `not_installed`, one whose session lists nothing is `known: false` with that said plainly; a provider or tool entry still answers `known: false`. `--account NAME` (with `--entry`) asks a harness entry as one of its accounts, so a signed-in account lists what it may use; `options::entry_with` and `options::Asking` are the library's form. `--format json` prints one object: `{"known", "unknown_because"?, "unknown_kind"?, "signed_out"?, "program"?, "program_version"?, "cache_lifetime"?, "cache_choices"? ([{"id", "label"}]: the lifetimes beyond default the provider honours, under its own names; absent when none), "cache_lifetime_setting"?, "modes"? ([{"id", "name"}], with "default_mode"?: the modes a probe session listed, its `mode` option else the older `modes` block; absent for a harness), "models": [{"id", "name"?, "description"?, "resolved"?, "version"?, "efforts": [..], "default_effort"?, "is_default"?}]}`, `program_version` being the first line of the program's `--version` when it answers within 10 s; `unknown_kind` says why a provider could not list, `not_installed` (the program is not found) or `failed` (it is there and could not be asked; `unknown_because` has the text), and is absent for a source that lists only in its session; `signed_out: true` marks a provider that listed its models and says nobody is signed in on the machine's own login (Claude Code and Codex both list signed out; the first prompt is what fails), which says nothing of an account the host applies; `text` prints one `ID<TAB>NAME<TAB>EFFORTS` line per model. Claude Code lists `default`, `opus`, `fable`, `sonnet` and `haiku` with the model each resolves to and the efforts each takes; Codex lists its models with each one's own efforts. It uses the machine's own login and takes the process's usual streams, so `canoa-ffi` runs it as an in-process job; the app caches the answer; the host keeps a probe session's answer for the process, by program version. Exit 2 for a source that is neither built in nor an entry.
- `accounts` (see "Accounts"): `add` signs in by one of the provider's login methods (`--method` naming none or an unknown one lists them, status 2) and appends `[host.accounts.NAME]` to canoa.cfg, made if absent; `login` signs in again to an account (`--method` only when its kind has several); `remove` forgets the stored credential and the account's directory and takes the block `add` wrote out of canoa.cfg (an account written some other way is for its writer to remove; an entry that offers it keeps it, status 2); `list` prints `NAME HARNESS KIND STATE` or `{"name", "harness", "kind", "id", "state", "revision", "expires_at"}`, `state` being `signed in`, `needs sign-in`, `in use` or `signed in where it runs` (harness-login), never a credential. `add` refuses the name `local`. A page to open or a code to confirm is said on standard error (`Open this page: URL`, `Confirm this code there: CODE`), or with `--format json` printed as `{"say": TEXT}` and `{"open_url": {"url", "user_code", "expires_ms"}}`, then `{"account": {"name", "harness", "kind", "id", "label", "checked"}}` when `add` is done. A secret the method asks for is typed without echo at a terminal, or one line of standard input when that is not a terminal; it is never read from the command line. `--until-stdin-closes` cancels the sign-in when the front's input closes (the process-level `canoa` ends at once instead, so do not use it with a piped secret).
- `launch` is what `serve` runs for an agent entry with profiles: the environment (and any extra arguments) on the first line of standard input, the rest to `PROGRAM`, whose status it exits with; a first line of another form is status 2.

- `--method` names the proof: this user's identity of that method, made when absent, and the home then records that method in `method`. Without it, the method the home records (the one named last, or its first identity's), or a new identity of the default method, Noise. An identity never changes method. A client of the other method is logged as `a client was refused: … the peer proves by X, and this endpoint only by Y`, with no word of `accept`: its key has to be paired again under this host's method. `--key-limit` and `--no-rekey` set the session, each method's default otherwise, and a non-default one is logged at `info`.
- **A running host keeps its home's method.** `id --method X` and `pair --method X`, with another method than the one the home records while a host holds the home (`Home::probe`, which takes nothing and writes nothing), are refused with status 3: `NAME (process PID) is running on this home, which proves by Y: already-paired clients would be refused after its next restart if the method became X. Stop it first, or add --force to change it anyway`. `--force` changes it and logs at `info` `--force: method is X while NAME (process PID) runs on this home, which proves by Y: …`; it is the person's own flag, and what it risks is a lockout of paired clients, not a looser proof. The method the home already records, no `--method`, and a home no host holds are never refused. `serve --method` is not guarded: it holds the home itself.
- `serve` prints one JSON object per entry on standard output, `{"name", "kind", "url", "harness"?, "title"?, "peer", "method"}`, `harness` being a harness entry's provider id (absent for any other entry) and `title` the entry's title (absent when it has none), `peer` this host's id and `method` its proof's, once every entry is bound, and then `{"ready": true, "peer", "method"}`, a line with no `name`; then it serves until stopped. It logs on standard error.
- **One host on a home.** `serve` takes the home's lock (see "One host on a home") before anything else of the home, or any address: a second one, the app's or another `canoa serve`, says `canoa: NAME (process PID) already hosts this home, DIR: ...`, binds nothing and ends with status 3. A holder that died holds nothing. `check`, `peers`, `accept`, `revoke`, `id` and `pair` take no lock, and `serve --program` takes none either.
- `pair` takes its PIN back however it ends, so a connection still pairing on its own thread when it is given up pairs nobody: in an application's process such a thread outlives the command.
- **The host's own sources** (`serve --local-sources`, `ServeOptions::with_local_sources`): `Claude` and `Codex` (built-in harness entries with Bypass offered), `Antigravity` (`agy acp`, usage read, also found in `%LOCALAPPDATA%/agy/bin`), `Grok` (`grok agent stdio`, also in `~/.grok/bin`), `OpenCode` (a built-in `opencode` harness entry: coqui-acp's `opencode` provider runs `opencode acp` in this process, with the `opencode_accounts` provider beside it; also found in `~/.opencode/bin`, the found program is its `executable`) and `Pi` (a built-in `pi` harness entry: coqui-acp-pi's provider drives `pi --mode rpc` in this process, with no Node and no adapter; it is ready exactly when the Store's Pi extension finds `pi`, that is a managed copy, `PATH`, `~/.pi/agent/bin` or `~/.local/bin`; `missing` is `["pi"]` and nothing else). A managed copy (`current.json` the host wrote, every file as installed, its Node and script inside the copy) runs as the provider's `node` and `script` settings, never a `.cmd` shim; otherwise the `pi` found is its `executable`. `[permissions] protected`, when canoa.cfg has it, is the provider's `protected` setting, so the person's list replaces the provider's recommended one for this source (it is the one list the person already edits for what a session may not touch); absent, the provider's recommended list stands. The launch's `PI_SKIP_VERSION_CHECK` is not passed on yet: no environment reaches a source's provider but a profile's. Each is an entry with `Entry::local` set: no listener, no `/canoa.json` line, reachable through `Local::open` only. `serve` prints a line for each, `{"name", "kind": "agent", "local": true, "missing": [...], "harness"?, "title", "peer", "method"}` with no `url`; `missing` names the programs not found (on Windows only `.exe`, `.cmd`, `.bat` and `.com` count, so `npx` is `npx.cmd`), and the entry is served either way so a client can say what to install. A name canoa.cfg already uses is left to canoa.cfg.
- **A source of the host's own is launched with canoa.cfg's top level** (the host owns a session's profile), through the machinery of `[host.profiles]` ("An agent entry with `profiles`"), with one profile named `default`: `Config::local_profiles` holds `[profile]` (the person's layer), the names in `[overlays]` (read from `overlays/NAME` beside canoa.cfg, as for `[host.profiles]`, so a source that is a directory is not applied here), and for each agent `[agents.NAME.profile]` (a layer above the person's, `agent:NAME`), `[agents.NAME.hooks]` (an overlay's name and the tree digest its hooks run at: `allow_hooks`) and `[agents.NAME.mcp]`: `true` leaves a server of the person's on, `false` turns any server off, a `sha256:` digest allows an overlay's server at that definition (`allow_mcp`). `LocalProfiles::for_agent` is `None` when nothing is for that agent, which starts it bare. With `ServeOptions::layers` set (`serve` sets it) the offer is built again from the layered files, through `Config::load_layers`, when each connection reaches its first message, so an install reaches the next session and ends none; a file that no longer loads is logged at `warn` and the last good offer stays. A session starts with a named loadout: `[loadouts.NAME]` holds `description`, `overlays` (names from `[overlays]`), `skills` (the ones the person chose) and `mcp` (servers of `[profile.mcp]`), and `[defaults] loadout` names the one a session that names none starts with. `initialize`'s `_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/loadout"]` (`LOADOUT_META`) names it; a name `[loadouts]` does not hold is refused as invalid params, with the name, and with neither a name nor a default the session starts with every overlay the profile lists and no loadout's narrowing. The connection resolves it once (`HostProfile::resolve_loadout`, `Connection::prepare_with`), so a later edit reaches new sessions only: its overlays apply whole in the order `[overlays]` composes them (a loadout cannot reorder them; an overlay it names that `[overlays]` lacks is reported, not applied), `OVERLAYS_META`'s list wins over the loadout's overlays, and the person's own `[profile.mcp]` servers the loadout does not name are off (a name it gives that the profile does not define is reported). A skill the loadout names is on for the session (`HostProfile::chosen_skills`, set at that resolution): each connection reads its record in `overlays/.skills/NAME.json` with the checks of an overlay's fetched skill (the record says `chosen`, its files have its digest and `[skill_pins]`' pin and pass `read_fetched_skill`) and delivers it in the person's layer; one that fails is reported as `skills.NAME` and left out. Only the profile tables are re-read: entries, `[host]` and `[agents.NAME] program` change at a restart. Only entries with `Entry::local` or `own_profile` get this; an entry on an address offers only its `[host.profiles]`. An item for `model`, `effort` and `mode` carries `"value"`, since the host cannot set them and the session's options do. A project's profile (`.canoa/profile.toml`) is not read. A session may also start with a named profile: `[profiles.NAME]` (the name is 1 to 64 of ASCII letters, digits, spaces, `-`, `_` and `.`, no space at either end) holds `source`, `model`, `effort`, `mode`, `context` (the starting instructions, text), `cache_lifetime` (`default`, `off`, `short` or `long`) and `loadout`, all optional and nothing else (`Config::local_profiles.presets`, `Preset`). `initialize`'s `_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/preset"]` (`PRESET_META`) names it; a name `[profiles]` does not hold is refused as invalid params, with the name. The connection makes its model, effort and mode the session layer above the agent's (`HostProfile::session`, `Preset::session_layer`) and its context an addition to the initial context after every other layer's, reported with the layer `session`; its loadout is the connection's unless `LOADOUT_META` names one, and a loadout canoa.cfg no longer holds is reported as `loadout` and the default applies. `source` and `cache_lifetime` are the client's to apply. `[profile]` stays the person's layer every session gets; a profile sits above it, and the account and permissions are never part of one. An entry that offers only `[host.profiles]` holds no presets and refuses the name.
- **Approvals** (`profile::APPROVE_METHOD`, `_canoa/profile/approve`): a client that is this process's own link (`Local::open`, never a paired key) and whose `initialize` carries `_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/approve"] = true` (`profile::APPROVE_META`) is asked, before each `session/new`, `session/load` or `session/resume` is forwarded, which MCP servers of an overlay that the configuration does not allow this session may start: the request `{"profile", "harness", "servers": [{"name", "layer", "digest"}]}` with an unguessable text id (`canoa-approve-N-<24 hex>`), answered `{"allow": [names]}` (names it did not list are ignored). Those names are added to that session's `mcpServers` and shown `applied` in its report; nothing else changes, and nothing is kept: keeping a choice is `[agents.NAME.mcp]` in canoa.cfg. A paired key, however it opts in, and a link that does not opt in, answers with an error, takes over two minutes, or goes away gets the configuration's behaviour (off). Its other messages wait until the answer. A server the person turned off with `false` is never asked about. The scope removes a client's `_meta`, so the opt-in is read before it. An agent's own request or notification whose method starts with `_canoa/` or `_coqui_acp/secret_` is dropped before the client sees it (`scope::is_host_only`), on every entry.
- `serve --allow-empty`: a canoa.cfg with no entry, or none at all at that path, runs with nothing to serve, printing only the `ready` line, where without it that is status 2. It writes no file. A canoa.cfg that exists and cannot be read or parsed is still status 2.
- `serve --web [DIR]` serves the web app too (see "The web app"): from `DIR` (made absolute from the working directory), else `[host.web]`'s `dir`, else the site built in; on `[host.web]`'s settings, or the defaults without the table. `--web-listen` replaces `listen`, and needs `--web` or `[host.web]` (status 2). After the entries' lines it prints `{"web": URL}`, a line with no `name`. A site that cannot be served, no site, plain HTTP beyond loopback, a zero limit or an unusable certificate is status 2; an address that cannot be bound, status 1.
- **A link from inside** (`Local::open`) is served as a paired key's connection is: the entry's agent, scope, worktrees (`_canoa/worktree/*` before the first ACP message) and `max_peers`. It skips the proof, the trust store and the entry's `peers` list, since whoever holds a `Local` is this process; no network peer can obtain one. `Stopper::stop` closes every link. The peer it is logged as is the host's own id.
- `portal` (feature `portal`, Unix) reads its settings from `--config` (a TOML file, see "The portal"; no file is the defaults), `--site`, `--listen` and `--socket` replacing `site`, `listen` and `socket`, prints `{"portal": {"socket", "listen"}}` once bound, and serves until killed. Status 2 for a settings file or site that does not work, 1 for an address that cannot be bound.
- `serve --portal DIR --portal-user USER` runs the host for a portal (see "The portal"); `--portal-user` is required with it `--portal-group` (one or more) and `--portal-min-uid` (default 1000) go with it, and a name that is not in `/etc/passwd` is status 2. `--web` and `[host.web]` are ignored with it.
- `serve --until-stdin-closes`: once every entry is bound, standard input is read and discarded; when it closes, every listener and agent is ended (`Stopper::stop`) and the host exits 0. For a parent that runs the host as its child: the host ends with it, however the parent ends. Without the flag the input is never read.
- `pair` (`--listen` default `127.0.0.1:0`, another address logged at `info` as `non-default: pair --listen is ADDRESS, default loopback`; `--timeout-seconds` default 300) prints `{"pin", "peer", "method", "url"}`, a six-digit PIN, this host's id, its method and the address to dial, and pairs by that method; a native client joins with `coqui_link::pairing::join` (`canoa pair --join URL`). The joiner's key is accepted in the store once the PIN confirms both keys, and `pair` prints `{"paired": ID}` and exits 0. One wrong try ends it (status 1), as does the timeout, and with `--until-stdin-closes` its input closing. A pairing connection serves no agent. A WebSocket upgrade that carries an `Origin` header, which every browser sends, is refused (403) before the handshake, so a web page cannot spend the PIN's one try, unless `--origin ORIGIN` (one, `scheme://host[:port]`, never `null`) names it, as an extension's page or the web app's origin is named, and the key it pairs is recorded as that origin's; a browser may instead pair by connecting to `serve` once and being accepted. `pair --join URL` is the other end: it joins a waiting `pair` under this host's identity (the PIN from `--pin-file`, else standard input; `--ca-file` a PEM bundle the server's certificate must chain to) and prints the waiting side's peer id.
- On `serve --until-stdin-closes` and `pair --until-stdin-closes`, nothing is logged between the input closing and the exit, and the process exits within 5 s whatever is left: a parent that died can leave standard error a pipe a write to which never returns (seen on Windows under a Dart parent).
- `accept` and `revoke` take `--` before the id.
- `tool` serves one tool entry on standard input and output, `JSON` being the entry as `ToolSpec` serializes it (without `listen`, `working_roots`, `peers`, `max_peers`): what `serve` runs per connection. A `JSON` that is not one, or that `check` refuses, is status 2.
- `check` prints `NAME KIND LISTEN` per entry, or `{"name", "kind", "listen"}` with `--format json`, and serves nothing.
- `layers` prints the canoa.cfg files in the order they are read, lowest first, whether or not they exist (the installed, system and user files, or the ones `--config` or `CANOA_CONFIG` list), then the one that is written: a JSON object `{"files", "own"}` with `--format json`, one path a line and `own PATH` otherwise. A listed file that is missing, or an empty entry, is refused.
- `peers` prints `accepted ID` and `waiting ID [from ADDRESS]`, or `{"peer", "state", "source"}`.
- canoa.cfg is read as layers, lowest first: `canoa.cfg` beside the program (or in `CANOA_INSTALLED_DIR`), the system's (`%PROGRAMDATA%\Canoa\canoa.cfg`, else `/etc/Canoa/canoa.cfg`) and the user's (`%APPDATA%\Canoa\canoa.cfg`, else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/Canoa/canoa.cfg`, else `~/.config/Canoa/canoa.cfg`, macOS included), each in the format its first line names, merged by `canoa_host::merge_into` (tables key by key; arrays and the `ENTRIES` named entries whole). `--config`, else `CANOA_CONFIG`, is a list of files split at `;` on Windows and `:` elsewhere, the last highest and the one `accounts` writes: without a leading `+` it replaces the three layers, with one it goes over them; an empty entry or a missing file is refused naming the entry. The app reads and writes the same files.
- `provider` takes `--library` and `--source` from where it was started, then runs in `--directory`; with `--settings-on-stdin` it reads a `HostLine` first, as `serve` writes it; with `--connect` it serves on that loopback socket after sending the line's token, rather than on standard input and output.
- Exit status: 0 success; 1 the operation failed; 2 the invocation or canoa.cfg was wrong, `serve` with nothing to serve, or a text that is not a peer id; 3 another host holds this home.

### A webpage's session folder

A client that is a webpage has no file system and names no path. Its `session/new` carries `_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/site"] = {"site": "<host name>"}`; the host ignores any `cwd` it sent and gives the agent `<Documents>/Canoa/scratch/<site>/<new UUID>` instead, made (`create_dir_all`, mode 0700 on Unix) only after the scope has passed the request, so a request that is refused makes nothing. The answer to `session/new` carries `_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/site"] = {"site", "id", "folder"}` (the host removes the key from any other answer, an agent's own included). The site is taken only as written, in the form a browser gives it: lower-case ASCII labels (an international name as `xn--` punycode), no trailing dot, port, separator, colon or IPv6, a label of 1 to 63 letters, digits and inner hyphens, 253 characters at most, a first label that is not a Windows device name (`con`, `prn`, `aux`, `nul`, `com1`..`com9`, `lpt1`..`lpt9`), and a name made of numbers only an IPv4 address; anything else fails the request with `-32602`. `session/load` and `session/resume` with the same key must carry the recorded folder as `cwd`: it is taken only when it is `<Documents>/Canoa/scratch/<site>/<id>` with `<id>` one segment of letters, digits and hyphens, and is made again when gone; anything else is `-32602`. An entry with `working_roots` refuses a folder outside them. The Documents folder is `XDG_DOCUMENTS_DIR`, else the system's; a host with none fails the request. The host makes no other folder for a client.

A site names no directories of its own: `additionalDirectories` is removed from its `session/new`, `session/load` and `session/resume` (the profile's skills folders are added after this check), and a session the host placed for a site, or reopened for one, is refused `session/set_mode` and `session/set_config_option` (an option the agent did not give the category `model` or `thought_level`) to anything but `plan`, `manual`, `default`, `accept_edits`, `acceptEdits` or `dontAsk` (`-32602`), unless the message carries `_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/approval"]`, the token the host put in this connection's answer to `initialize` (`APPROVAL_META`; it is a different string on each connection, so one seen on another is refused, and the host removes the key from every answer but the one to `initialize`). A client marks its sessions as its own with `{"site": ..., "own": true}` in `_meta["dev.boriken.canoa/site"]` (the web app, and the browser extension for its own pages), and those are held to neither this nor the removal of `additionalDirectories`; the host does not echo the mark.

## Environment variables

| Variable | Effect | Default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `CANOA_CONFIG` | the canoa.cfg files to read (a list, a leading `+` layering it over the installed, system and user files), used when `--config` is not given | the three layers, above |
| `CANOA_INSTALLED_DIR` | the folder whose `canoa.cfg` is the installed copy | the executable's folder (the portable launcher sets its own) |
| `CANOA_CREDENTIAL_STORE` | `memory` keeps every credential the process stores (the accounts', the mail passwords', the `system` provider's secrets) in its own memory and out of the operating system's store: it is gone when the process ends, and a second process sees none of it. It is for tests, which set it; any other value is ignored | the operating system's store |
| `CANOA_LOG` | what to log, a `tracing` filter | `info` |
| `CANOA_WEB_DIST` | at build time, with feature `web`: the absolute directory of the site built in | none; the build stops without it |
| `COQUI_AGENTIC_PEER_HOME` | the directory of the identities (`identity` for WebCrypto, `identity-noise` for Noise) and trust store (`peers/`) | `.canoa` in the user's home folder (`%USERPROFILE%\.canoa` on Windows); no folder named after the peer library is made. canoa.cfg's place is unchanged and separate |
| `XDG_DOCUMENTS_DIR` | the Documents folder a webpage session's scratch folder is made in (absolute, or from `$HOME`), on any system | the system's: the Documents known folder on Windows, `user-dirs.dirs` then `~/Documents` on Linux, `~/Documents` on macOS |
| `NO_COLOR` | no colour in the log | colour on a terminal |

## Gotchas

- **Tests never touch the operating system's credential store, bar the ones about it.** In this crate's own unit tests every `KeyringStore::new` is the process's memory (`keyring_core::mock`); a test about the real store makes an `accounts::store::real_store::RealStore`, a service named for the process (`dev.boriken.canoa.test.<pid>.<n>`) whose every entry is removed when it drops, a panic included, and `no_entry_of_this_run_is_left_in_the_operating_systems_store` checks the store (Windows: `cmdkey /list`) afterwards. The integration tests set `CANOA_CREDENTIAL_STORE=memory` in `tests/support` before they start a host, and the C ABI's tests in `canoa-ffi/tests/common`, so the hosts they run and the ones they start in process keep credentials in memory; a test that needs a credential to outlive one process uses `[secrets] accounts = "file"` in a directory of its own. A new test that puts something in the real store takes a `RealStore`, never a service of its own.
- Under Noise a client's handshake ends before the host decides, so a client the host refuses sees its link end rather than a failed proof.
- `serve --program --paired` proves by this home's identity and admits the keys of its trust store, except a key canoa.cfg limits to entries with `peers` (read when it starts: a key an entry names is that entry's and is not served the program, whatever the store says, and a canoa.cfg that cannot be read is an error). `canoa revoke` removes a key from the store it serves. The servers this host dials (`pair --join`, then `dial --paired` and `forward --paired`) are kept apart in `<home>/dialed`, which no entry reads: joining a server does not let it in. The library's commands run in a job of an application's process only as a process of their own (`is_a_hosts_own`).
- **The library's commands** (`serve --program`, `dial`, `forward`, `grant`, `discover`, `mcp-config`, `mcp-shim`, `agent-guard`, `id --identity`, `pair --join`) are calls into `coqui-acp-bridge`'s modules, which hold their logic and their flags: `cli/bridge.rs` gives them this host's home (identity, trust store and grants are the host's own, not the library's default folder) and the program that runs `agent-guard` and `mcp-shim` (`Front::program`), and ends the process with the status the library returns, saying nothing for an agent's. `serve --program` serves one program after `--` with no canoa.cfg, by the library's proofs, TLS, WebTransport, linger, services, forwarding, offers and advertising; `--method`, `--key-limit` and `--no-rekey` are both forms' own, and the library's other flags without `--program` are refused. `--web`, `--web-listen`, `--portal`, `--local-sources`, `--allow-empty`, `--supervised` and `--until-stdin-closes` belong to a serve of canoa.cfg and are refused with it. `id --identity FILE` prints the peer id of the key in FILE, made when absent.
- A stdio agent inherits the host's environment and working directory. An agent can still start whatever its own tools start when the client allows them; the host stops only what a client could start without the agent asking.
- A profile's variable and MCP server values never reach the client from the host, but they reach the agent, which acts for that client and may show them: give a profile only what that entry's clients may use.
- A paired key reaches every session an agent keeps for this user: `session/list` and `session/load` pass to a stdio agent unchanged.
- `program = "canoa"`, `args = ["agent", "claude"]` with a profile that has skills needs `"--setting", "allow_client_plugins=true"` in `args`: without it Claude Code's translator refuses every session the host adds its plugin to. A `harness` entry needs nothing.
- On Windows the translators start `claude.exe` and `codex.exe`, as the native installers put them; an npm-installed `claude.cmd` or `codex.cmd` is untested, and a profile's context goes on the command line it is started with. Name the executable by its full path if it is elsewhere.
- `working_roots` compare paths as written: a link inside a root that points outside it is followed by the agent. Worktrees are made, read and removed only where every link is resolved, inside the roots resolved too.
- If the host itself is killed, on Windows the system ends its agents with it (each is in a job object closed with the host); on Unix each sees its standard input close, and most then exit. `serve --until-stdin-closes` ends them on every platform when the parent goes. A host inside an application has no parent to watch: on Unix, an agent that ignores its input closing outlives an application that crashes.
- Each connection is a fresh process, so a turn in progress ends with its link; a later connection resumes a session only if the agent kept it.
- `peers` is read when the host starts; a key removed from the trust store loses its link within about a second without a restart.
- A provider's process inherits the host's environment. For a Python source that includes, on Windows, `PATH`, which decides which `python3.dll` loads under `import_search = "path"` unless `import_dirs` holds one; and with `isolation = "inherit"`, `PYTHONPATH` and `PYTHONHOME`.
- Settings come from canoa.cfg only. A client reaches a provider through ACP, never through its settings; `check` does not load the provider, so a setting its schema refuses shows only when a connection loads it, on the host's standard error.
- A tool runs with the host's rights and does unasked whatever its rules do not turn into `ask`: the host shows its tool calls as they run, never before. `unasked` is the entry writer's account of that, shown, not checked.
- On Unix, ending one tool process (its session's cancel, a replaced `message` run) does not end what it started; that stays in the connection's process group and ends with the connection, unless it left the group.
- On Windows `program = "pi"` does not find npm's `pi.cmd`: name `node` with the script's absolute path, which keeps `cmd.exe` out, or `pi.cmd`.
- A tool entry is on `canoa tool`'s command line as JSON, which other users may read: nothing secret belongs in one.
- On Unix, what a tool starts in a session of its own (Node's `detached`) leaves the connection's process group and outlives it.
- Plain WebSocket carries only the proof and sealed records. Anyone who can reach the address can connect and make a proven key wait; only an accepted key gets further. On loopback, the default, that is anyone on this machine.
- The web app's page connects to the entries' addresses, which are loopback: a browser on another machine, opening it over HTTPS beyond loopback, reaches the page but not the entries until a proxy carries them. A non-empty `origins` must list the page's own origin for its connections to be let in.
- The site's files are served with this user's rights from what the directory holds when asked: whoever can write it chooses the page, and a link swapped in between the check and the open is followed (the check resolves the path, then opens it). Give it a directory only you can write. `serve` refuses a site holding canoa.cfg's directory or the identity home.
- The page's key lives in the browser under the page's origin, `http://127.0.0.1:7800` by default. While the host is not running, any program of any user on this machine that binds that port serves its own page in that origin, which can use the key paired with this host. On a machine others use, serve over HTTPS with a certificate the browser trusts and a key only you can read, or use the extension.
- The policy allows `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` and inline styles, which Flutter's web build needs, and no inline or evaluated script: build the app with `flutter build web --csp --no-web-resources-cdn`, or its code and CanvasKit are refused. The app carries its Roboto font (`app/assets/fonts`) and points the engine's fallback fonts at the site (`app/web/flutter_bootstrap.js`), so that build fetches nothing from outside the site. `python app/tool/build.py` puts the fallback fonts the engine asks for (every script, CJK and emoji: 724 files, 20.7 MiB) in `assets/fonts/fallback/` from the deps mirror and deletes `flutter_service_worker.js`: a build made with `flutter build web` alone has neither, and a symbol Roboto lacks then draws as a box. The app registers no service worker (the ETag and 304 answers below are its cache), and `splash.js` unregisters one an earlier build left, which Flutter's loader would otherwise register again and wait for up to 4 seconds. `app/web/index.html` shows a loading screen (the mark and "Loading Canoa…", light or dark by `prefers-color-scheme`) from `app/web/splash.css` and takes it away on Flutter's first frame in `app/web/splash.js`: both are files of the site's own origin, so the policy needs nothing added.
